# Decision Log

Append-only. Each entry: id, date, decision, why, consequences. To change a decision, add a new entry that supersedes the old one (and mark the old one "Superseded by D-xx"). Decisions D-01 to D-10 were made by the owner; D-11 onward were made by the project/program manager while planning the work (2026-10-18) and can be revisited by the owner.

---

### D-01 · 2026-10-18 · Keep only the RPG
**Decision.** The site stops being a collection. The hub, 找找看 (`src/find`) and 圖案數獨 (`src/sudoku`) are removed. The RPG (`src/quest`) becomes the single game and the site boots straight into its title screen.
**Why.** Owner decision: one deep, polished game instead of three small ones.
**Consequences.** Hub/find/sudoku code, tests, routes, sprites used only by them, and their storage keys (`baokaka.progress`, `baokaka.sudoku`) go away (issue #2). Shared utilities still used by the RPG (`shared/storage.ts`, `shared/random.ts`, `shared/audio.ts`, `components/*`) stay.

### D-02 · 2026-10-18 · Full story rewrite and new title
**Decision.** The 寶咖咖奇俠傳 / 仙劍 setting is replaced entirely by 《寶咖咖抓周大冒險》 — *Baokaka & Mocha: Midnight Across Taipei* (`docs/story/story-bible.md`, `docs/story/script.md`). 抓周 (the first-birthday grab ceremony) is the spine: nine treasures plus the gold lock, the red string, the 「1」 candle and the blank invitation.
**Why.** Owner request for a warm, family story rooted in Taipei that grandparents and kids both understand.
**Consequences.** Every map, event, foe, item and most sprites are new content. Terminology changes: 銅板 (coins, was 貼紙), 元氣 (MP, was 真氣), 才藝 (skills, was 仙術), 武力/身手 (was 武術/身法). Battle tone: tantrums, not violence (no 殺/死/血).

### D-03 · 2026-10-18 · 14 chapters in one night
**Decision.** 14 chapters across Taipei landmarks from 00:00 to 05:30, each chapter card shows a clock. Ending at 05:30 (阿嬤 wakes) and 10:00 (the party). Locations: home/大安森林公園, 台大, 台北車站, 西門町, 龍山寺/剝皮寮, 大稻埕, 士林夜市, 松山機場, 動物園/貓空, 碧潭, 北投, 陽明山, 淡水, 台北101; post-game 象山.
**Why.** "The night is the timer" gives a readable sense of progress and a natural colour script (navy → indigo → violet → gold).

### D-04 · 2026-10-18 · Airport, expanded 淡水, playable MRT
**Decision.** Owner-requested additions: a 松山機場 chapter (Ch8, planes asleep 23:00–05:00, the 「飛機！」 word), 淡水 expanded with 漁人碼頭 and the new 淡江大橋 (opened 2026-05-12; drawn as a non-walkable backdrop), and MRT rides as **playable** moments (the 月亮線: route-map screen + 5 walkable ride cars themed after real lines) rather than a menu.
**Consequences.** Engine work for travel (issue #9) and a ride-car content issue (#27). Accuracy rules: civilian, unbranded airport; no light rail drawn on the bridge; no designer named.

### D-05 · 2026-10-18 · Expand to 6–8 hours
**Decision.** Grow the game from ~2 h to 6–8 h with sub-maps and gate puzzles per chapter, cross-chapter side quests (lost & found, deliveries, clerk points, 長輩圖, echoes, MRT riders), hero side stories with signature gear, collections, a bestiary and a post-game (象山 superboss, boss rush, quiz).
**Consequences.** Content volume roughly ×4. Each chapter is split into a content issue and an art issue so PRs stay reviewable.

### D-06 · 2026-10-18 · Level cap 20
**Decision.** Cap raised from 12 to 20. XP table per story bible § 7. Main story ends around Lv 16–17; post-game targets 17 → 20.
**Consequences.** `MAX_LEVEL`, `XP_TABLE`, skill unlocks, foe stats from Ch6 on scaled ~×1.15 per chapter shift and settled by `sim.test.ts` (issues #5, #54).

### D-07 · 2026-10-18 · Topical-joke rules
**Decision.** Mostly evergreen Taiwanese life memes. Recent (2025–2026) memes only if verified by web search and light-hearted, each with a 12-month review date. No politics, no politicians, no real celebrities or brands, nothing mean or scary. Every joke must work for a grandparent and be harmless to a 6-year-old. Jokes sit only on optional NPCs/variants and never gate progress; each is removable by deleting one talk rule.
**Consequences.** The registry is story bible § 9. The only recent joke at the time of writing is `gag.ch13.newBridge` (review 2027-10). QA checks the registry in issue #58.

### D-08 · 2026-10-18 · Art option D, with B and C accents and A's rules
**Decision.** After comparing four options (A Picture-Book Diorama, B Paper-Cut Night, C Neon-Sign Taipei, D 動漫 Anime Cel), the owner chose **D** ("精緻一點，像是日本動漫風格"). Mix: B's 布袋戲 proscenium frames **boss** battles; C's neon is used **only** in 西門町, 士林夜市 and inside MRT cars; A's technical spine applies everywhere (sprite contract, rig groups, unique ids, pre-baked grade tokens, performance budget, Playwright visual QA).
**Consequences.** `docs/art/art-direction.md` was rewritten for D as the single art source of truth (finalised 2026-10-18: per-chapter colour scripts and the night clock, character specs, 34-foe and boss tables, UI system, implementation rules). Art issues reference its sections by name and require reading the latest version.

### D-09 · 2026-10-18 · Anatomy fix
**Decision.** On reviewing the first D renders, the owner asked for correct anatomy (proportions, limbs attaching properly, a believable crawling baby and a cat that reads as a cat). The art director produced an anatomy sheet (`docs/art/options/d-anime/anatomy-sheet.*`) and standing/walking references (`baokaka-stand.svg`, `mocha-walk.svg`).
**Consequences.** Character art issues (#16–#18) must match the anatomy sheet; reviewers check silhouettes at map size and joints at battle/portrait size.

### D-10 · 2026-10-18 · Baokaka can't walk until the finale
**Decision.** Baokaka crawls (map) and sits (battle) for the whole game; he stands only in Ch14 (first steps) and the ending. He speaks only from a fixed toddler vocabulary.
**Consequences.** The hero rig needs crawl, sit and a one-off stand pose; the script's vocabulary list is a QA check.

---

### D-11 · 2026-10-18 · Build the new story in place; `main` always playable
**Decision.** The new game is built inside `src/quest/` (reusing the engine), not as a parallel app. Engine and art foundations land first while the old 6-chapter content keeps working (old content is minimally adapted when types change). The Ch1 content issue (#21) deletes the old story content and ships Ch1. From then on, the last implemented chapter's exit leads to a 「未完待續」 event (key `wip.toBeContinued`), moved forward by each chapter issue and removed by the ending issue (#50).
**Why.** Every merge auto-deploys; the public site must never be broken, and incremental chapters are reviewable.

### D-12 · 2026-10-18 · Old saves are discarded with a friendly message
**Decision.** The save format changes (new heroes, maps, items, `coins`). Instead of migrating, the save key moves to `baokaka.quest.v2` (with a `version: 2` field). If an old `baokaka.quest` record exists, the title screen shows a one-time friendly notice (zh-Hant, e.g. 「冒險重新開始囉！舊的存檔是上一個故事的，已經收進回憶裡了。」) and the old key is removed. Find/sudoku keys are removed too. Any future incompatible change bumps the version the same way.
**Why.** A migration from a different story is meaningless; a silent reset would confuse returning players.
**Consequences.** Issue #3.

### D-13 · 2026-10-18 · Routes
**Decision.** (Superseded in part by D-72: `#/` now shows 翻翻台北.) The app has one game. `#/` (and any unknown hash) shows the RPG title screen. `#/quest` keeps working as an alias (old shared links). `#/find` and `#/sudoku` redirect to `#/`. A dev-only `#/artlab` route hosts the art lab (issue #4). No router library.

### D-14 · 2026-10-18 · Placeholder sprites during content work
**Decision.** Chapter content issues come before chapter art issues. Content may register new `SpriteName`s backed by a shared, clearly labelled placeholder drawing (from issue #11). `validate.ts` reports placeholders as warnings (not errors) and the final QA issue (#58) requires zero placeholders.
**Why.** Content and art can be reviewed separately and the game stays playable between them.

### D-15 · 2026-10-18 · Chapter cards accept labels
**Decision.** The script uses `[chapter 尾聲｜度晬｜05:30]` and `[chapter 番外｜象山看日出｜05:30]`. The `chapter` step gets `n: number` plus an optional `label?: string` (shown instead of 「第 n 章」); `run.chapter` only tracks numeric chapters.

### D-16 · 2026-10-18 · Known doc inconsistencies and how to resolve them
Resolved by the planner; issues repeat the relevant ones.
- **Chapter clock.** The chapter headings in the bible are authoritative: Ch3 is **01:10** (the script's `[chapter 3｜…｜02:00]` and the notation example are typos). Times: 00:00, 00:40, 01:10, 01:35, 02:00, 02:25, 02:50, 03:15, 03:40, 04:05, 04:25, 04:45, 05:00, 05:15, ending 05:30.
- **Levels.** The progression table in bible § 7 (*Level curve (cap 20)*) is authoritative over the per-chapter "Levels:" lines (those are from v1 numbering). The boss table's "Where (Ch)" column was corrected by the director on 2026-10-18 and now matches the chapters.
- **Map clock vs chapter card.** The chapter card shows the chapter time above. Each map's sky/grade time follows the art-direction § 4.2 *Location table* (e.g. 大安 00:15, 貓空 03:55, 信義 05:10); sub-maps and ride cars use their chapter's time (art-direction § 3.2).
- **Stamps.** There are **14** stamps (`stamp_<map>` for daan, ntu, station, ximen, longshan, dadaocheng, shilin, airport, maokong, bitan, beitou, yangming, tamsui, tower); `allStamps` needs all 14. Mentions of "11" are stale.
- **`stickers` step.** Renamed `coins` (D-02). Negative amounts floor at 0.
- **Baokaka's 勇敢的第一步** is learned at Lv15 (skill table), not Lv12 (running-jokes table).

### D-17 · 2026-10-18 · Fonts are the only external runtime resource
**Decision.** Google Fonts (Chiron GoRound TC body, Noto Serif TC for names/titles only, Fredoka numerals) with `display=swap` and subsetting where possible; everything else is bundled. Exact families and weights follow the latest `art-direction.md`.
**Superseded by D-105 (#68):** the same families and weights are now self-hosted subsets; the site has no external runtime resource.

### D-18 · 2026-10-18 · Visual QA tooling uses Playwright as a dev dependency
**Decision.** Add `playwright` (or `@playwright/test`) and `pixelmatch`/`pngjs` as dev dependencies, using the preinstalled Chromium at `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers` locally. Screenshots are attached to PRs. Baseline diffs run locally and are **never** part of the deploy workflow (keeps deploys fast and deterministic). The art direction (§ 8.6 *Visual QA*) asks for the check to run in CI for art PRs; that can be added later as a separate, non-blocking workflow once baselines are stable (after M3).

### D-19 · 2026-10-18 · Removing the mini-games: what went and how old links behave
**Decision.** (Issue #2.) A sprite is kept only if the RPG references it by name (`sprite: '…'` in content, `SPRITES.x`, `HERO_SPRITE`, the chest/toy-box sprites). Removed: `sock`, `spade`, `dandelion`, `ball`, `flask` (find targets), `cloud`, `sunDisc` (find/sudoku decor), and `fence`, `rug`, `wave` (decor sprites the RPG never drew: its `fence`/`rug` are terrain, its `wave` is a battle effect). `art/Scene.tsx` and the `Placement` type (find's scene layout) went too, and `sprites/targets.tsx` was renamed `sprites/items.tsx` because what remains are RPG items. Dead shared code that only the removed games used was deleted (13 icons, `rng`/`shuffle`, `Screen`'s `fixed` prop, `Button` size `xl`, the `touch` spacing token and the `ripple`/`breathe`/`no-scrollbar` CSS). `#/find` and `#/sudoku` are rewritten to `#/` with `history.replaceState` both on boot and on `hashchange`, so neither an old link nor an in-app hash edit leaves a dead history entry; the `artlab` route is left for issue #4. The title screen's 回遊戲選單 button is gone; the world screen's home button (which returns to the RPG title) keeps its label for now and is re-skinned with the rest of the UI in M2.
**Why.** The issue's acceptance criteria require no unreferenced art; keeping the leftovers would mislead art work in M2.
**Consequences.** If later content wants a cloud or wave decor sprite, it is drawn new in the option-D style anyway (art-direction), not restored.

### D-20 · 2026-10-18 · Save v2 details: reading never writes, and where UI strings live
**Decision.** (Issue #3.) `loadQuest()` only reads: it returns `{ save, legacyReset }`, and the old `baokaka.quest` key is deleted by `discardLegacyQuest()`, which `QuestGame` calls in an effect after mount. The issue sketched `loadQuest` deleting the key itself, but React StrictMode calls state initialisers twice in development, and the second call would no longer see the key and drop the notice. The stored record is `{ version: 2, run, sound, settings: { skipRides } }` under `baokaka.quest.v2`; a valid v2 record wins over a leftover legacy key, and a corrupt v2 record silently becomes a fresh run without the notice. `VictorySummary` gained `boss: FoeId | null` so the victory screen can say 「〈boss名〉不暈了！」. Engine/system strings (battle log, victory, defeat, inn, shop, stat names, command labels, the reset notice) live in `src/quest/engine/strings.ts` (`UI`); story dialogue stays in the event scripts. Coins are counted with 個 (「銅板不夠，還差 N 個。」, shop prices 「N 個」). The world screen's home button is now labelled 回到標題 (it returns to the title since #2), and the defeat tip says 體力少 instead of 血少 (tone guide: no 血).
**Why.** A pure load keeps the one-time notice correct in every React mode; one strings module lets the M2 re-skin and later chapters change wording in one place.
**Consequences.** Any later incompatible `Run` change bumps `SAVE_VERSION` and the key the same way and treats the previous v-key as legacy (never migrate across stories). New system-level text goes into `UI`, not inline JSX.

### D-21 · 2026-10-18 · Visual QA tooling details
**Decision.** (Issue #4.) `--serve` in `scripts/visual-check.mjs` starts a Vite **dev** server rather than `vite preview` as the issue sketched: the art lab is dev-only by design, so a production preview has no art lab targets to shoot (`--url` at a preview still works and runs only `title`). Art lab fixtures are starting states rendered through a new `src/quest/QuestScreens.tsx` (the view switch extracted from `QuestGame`, which keeps storage and music), over a live `questReducer`, so screenshots show the real wiring and a fixture can be played on. The script discovers fixture names from the art lab index (`[data-fixture]`) instead of duplicating them in `visual-targets.mjs`; duplicate target names abort the run (the `title` target is the real `#/`, the fixture with a saved run is `title-continue`). Silhouettes are a black box masked by the sprite's own markup (`renderToStaticMarkup` → data-URL `mask-image`), so no filter is involved. The `artlab-sprites-night` target uses `size=64` so the full-page sheet stays a sane height (≈3,400 CSS px); the lab itself shows all four sizes. Screenshots wait for `document.fonts.ready` and a 0.5 s DOM-quiet period (covers the typewriter dialogue) and use Playwright's `animations: 'disabled'` on top of `?still=1`. Dev dependencies are pinned exactly: `playwright@1.56.1` (matches the preinstalled Chromium build 1194), `pixelmatch@7.1.0`, `pngjs@7.0.0`. Art lab backgrounds use literal dev-only swatches (`#0B1238` night, a brick pattern), not palette tokens.
**Why.** Keeps the art lab out of production while making `npm run visual` a single command, and keeps one registry per thing (sprites, fixtures) so later issues only add entries.
**Consequences.** Baselines depend on installed fonts until #10 ships web fonts: committed baselines come from the agent environment; humans on another OS regenerate locally. `--perf` measures rAF intervals on the dev build, which are vsync-floored at 16.7 ms and pessimistic; #57 should switch to a trace-based frame-work measurement against a production build if it needs the § 8.5 12 ms figure.

### D-22 · 2026-10-18 · Four heroes on the old content: stand-ins, save v3, battle layout
**Decision.** (Issue #5.) `HERO_ORDER` is `baokaka, mocha, magpie, bear`; 小鴨鴨 is gone from `src/` entirely, including its sprite (`folk.Duck`, now unreferenced). Until #16/#17 deliver hero art, `HERO_SPRITE` (PartyPanel.tsx, also used by the dialogue portraits) draws 阿藍 as the `pigeon` and 黑糖 as the `moleKing`; the title and ending screens of the old story simply drop the duck. In the old content, 阿藍 takes the duck's place in 浴缸島 (`bath.magpie`, flag `magpieJoined`), and 黑糖 joins in `night.intro` (flag `bearJoined`) so the four-hero party is playable and every 才藝 belongs to a hero who joins (content test); both are marked temporary and go away with #21. The save moved to `version: 3` under `baokaka.quest.v3`; per D-20 the v2 key joined the legacy list (`LEGACY_QUEST_KEYS`), so a three-hero v2 save gives the one-time reset notice and is deleted. In battle, up to three heroes share one rank as before and four stand in two staggered rows (`HERO_SPOTS`, near row drawn last); the compact world party strip drops its portraits when four heroes are in it (≈75 px per card on a 375 px phone). The shield log line is now generic (`UI.shielded`), and the sim's careful/casual policies cast the party shield when a foe's next move hits everyone. FX per skill are borrowed `FxKind`s until #15.
**Why.** Stand-in sprites keep the issue engine-only (art is #16/#17); a late join in the old story is the cheapest way to exercise four heroes end to end; the legacy list keeps D-12's promise that a dropped save is always explained.
**Consequences.** #17 replaces the two `HERO_SPRITE` entries; #14 redesigns the stage and cards; #21 deletes the temporary joins; #54 rebalances (with 黑糖 the old final boss is easy: careful/casual 0 % loss).

### D-23 · 2026-10-18 · Script steps v2 details
**Decision.** (Issue #6.) `choice` and `goEvent` replace the *whole* rest of the scene, and `include` splices its event inline (both rewrite `scene.steps`/`at`, never recurse). So that a branch can never silently cut off the event that included it, `validate.ts` rejects a `choice` or `goEvent` inside an included event (directly), as well as include cycles and nesting deeper than 4. `heal` with a ratio rounds up (`ceil(ratio × max)`) so "at least ratio" holds, and `ratio: 1` is exactly the old full heal. A labelled chapter card (`尾聲`, `番外`) still needs an `n` (use 0 or 15; it is ignored for `run.chapter`); the card shows the label instead of 「第 n 章」. `inn 0` is handled in `runScene` (heal + a fixed line, `UI.innFree`), so the reducer has no free-inn branch. Choice buttons are all the same weight (no primary), because the post-game quiz must not hint the answer. The art-lab fixture `chapter` was renamed `chapter-card` (with a 00:00 clock) to match the issue; `dialogue-choice` is new. `runScene` gives up (`done`) after 64 `goEvent`/`include` expansions in one call, as a guard against a runaway `goEvent` loop that validation does not try to detect.
**Why.** Keeps the runner pure and flat as the issue asks, and moves the one sharp edge of mixing sub-calls with branches into the content linter.
**Consequences.** #21 writes the new chapters with `time` on every card; #9 fills `substitute()` in `src/quest/engine/text.ts`; #12 restyles `ChapterCard` and `ChoicePrompt`.

### D-24 · 2026-10-18 · Progress data: derived flags, quests, 圖鑑, escorts
**Decision.** (Issue #7.) All progress tables live in `src/quest/engine/progress.ts` (`DERIVED`, `QUESTS`, `TRAY`, `COLLECTIONS`, `BESTIARY_FOES`, `FOLLOWERS`) and are pure data over `run.flags` + `run.seenFoes`. Every engine path that sets flags goes through `addFlags` (script `flag` steps, chests, triggers) and victories go through `recordFoes`; both end in `refreshDerived`, which runs `applyDerived` to a fixed point. `bestiaryAll` is the one derived flag computed from `seenFoes` (every foe in `BESTIARY_FOES`, i.e. the roster minus `Foe.postGame` foes); it is checked first, so DERIVED entries may build on it, and it cannot have a DERIVED entry itself. Besides the bible's list, DERIVED seeds `lfAll` (the five `lf_*Back` flags, script § sq.lostFound) and four "at least one" flags, `clerkAny`, `elderAny`, `echoAny`, `riderAny` plus `lfAny`, so collect-in-any-order quests have a first step and `questStatus` keeps the bible's rule (started = first step set, next hint = first unset step, done = last step set) without special cases. Single-flag quests (`sq.bear`, `sq.callaLily`) only ever show as done; `sq.queue` and `sq.swanRace` start on the main-story flags that open them (`cutletDone`, `swanDone`). Collections store a title plus the page's line(s): stamps use the printed name and the stamp's picture, 長輩圖 the picture and its words, 回音 the place and both lines (no speaker, since `cloud`/`shushu` depends on `metShushu`), riders the name and the car. `Run.seenFoes` is required in a save, so the save moved to `version: 4` under `baokaka.quest.v4` and v3 joined `LEGACY_QUEST_KEYS` (D-20). Validation: DERIVED cycles, duplicates and bad `atLeast` are errors, as are repeated quest ids/step flags, hints over 24 characters, collection lines over 40, a ring that is not 9 treasures, `Foe.calmed` over 40 and `Foe.home` over 12; flags the tables read that no event sets yet are warnings (`progressWarnings()`, printed once in dev), which #58 turns into errors. Escorts: the world screen draws `activeFollower(flags)` from its own queue of the leader's walked tiles, one tile behind the last hero; after a map change it starts on `restingSpot` (screen-right of the party first, then screen-left, so it neither covers nor hides behind anyone) and waits there until the party has walked far enough to trail. The art lab passes a stand-in table (a snail under the flag `artlabFollower`) to `QuestScreens` because `FOLLOWERS` ships empty.
**Why.** One write path means no flag change can forget the derived flags; data-only pages let #19/#20 render from a save alone; the `…Any` flags keep the quest rule uniform.
**Consequences.** Chapter issues add their puzzle gates to DERIVED, their escorts to FOLLOWERS (`chickFollowing`, `dumplingFollowing`, `calfFollowing`), `calmed`/`home` per foe with the new roster, and set the flags the warnings list. The Ch14 issue may add a `bp_*` flag to the backpacker finale if the log should show a step there. When the new foe roster lands, mark 象山-only foes (`shushuBonus`) `postGame: true`.


### D-25 · 2026-10-18 · Item catalogue v2: blurbs, gifts, old-content mapping, save v5
**Decision.** (Issue #8.) `ITEMS` follows bible § 6.1/6.2 exactly (ids, names, effects, prices, in table order, which is also `ITEM_ORDER`). Blurbs come from script § *Items*. Where the script has none, `meatBall`, `grassJellyTea` and `mysteryFood` use the bible's § 6.1 blurb (with the stat written 「體力 +110。」 the way the script writes it). The 14 pieces of equipment with no blurb anywhere (`neckPillow`, `plushCharm`, `travelerHat`, `deliveryBag`, `luckyCat`, `moonPassCharm`, the four signature pieces, `pinwheel`, `clerkVest`, `championBelt`, `goldAmulet`) got one short line from their bible source plus 「裝備：…」, all ≤ 40 characters. `earthCane` keeps the script's 「全能力提升」 even though its bonus has no hp/mp: the script wins on wording. `price: 0` (the tables' "—") means a gift or quest reward. `validate.ts` rejects one in a shop stock, and the reducer's `buy` refuses it as a second guard. `neckPillow` and `flowerVest` are gifts in the story but keep their table prices (85, 100), so they could be stocked later. Signature gear uses `Item.only` and `canWear()`. The reducer's `equip` and the save validator both apply it, and the equip picker greys out other heroes with 「只有〈摩卡〉能戴」 (short party names: 寶咖咖, 摩卡, 阿藍, 黑糖). The old story's ids are mapped **by effect**, not by the issue's examples, so the sim stays put: cookie→teaEgg, bottle→pineappleCake, banana→braisedRice, driedFish→douhua, apple→railBento, shell→redTurtleCake, clothBook→gradCap, coinPurse→amulet, sunHat→bib, blanket→flowerVest, toyBoat→flipFlops, star→tigerShoes, nightLight→helmet. Chest ids (`yard-cookies`, …) are internal and unchanged. Items that no chest, shop, gift or drop hands out yet are `progressWarnings()` entries, not errors (like the flags in D-24). Icons are borrowed sprites, listed in the `TEMP icons until #19` block in `heroes.ts`. The save moved to `version: 5` under `baokaka.quest.v5`, and v4 (old item ids, already public) joined `LEGACY_QUEST_KEYS`.
**Why.** Mapping by effect keeps the old story's balance (careful/casual/reckless 200/198/119 of 200, unchanged within noise) until #21 deletes it. Price 0 plus a validator rule makes it impossible to ship a quest reward in a shop.
**Consequences.** #19 draws the item icons and replaces the TEMP sprite block. #21+ place every item; their chests, shops, gifts and drops clear the `item … cannot be obtained yet` warnings, which #58 turns into errors. #54 may rebalance prices and strengths.

### D-26 · 2026-10-18 · 月亮線 engine: stations as strings, arrival-only triggers, the `cleared` flag, save v6
**Decision.** (Issue #9.) `STATIONS` (in `src/quest/engine/mrt.ts`, with the stylised network `MRT_LINES`) holds all 14 rows of bible § 3.1 from now on, with `map`/`car` typed as plain strings. A station is drawn and can be lit before its map exists, but it can only be ridden once both its map (with `mrtArrival`) and its car exist (`rideable`); `mrtWarnings()` lists the ones that can't yet, inside `progressWarnings()`. `validateMrt` checks every station whose map exists. The art lab and the 路線圖 can show the full network now, and each chapter issue only adds its map. Riding works like this. `{ do: 'travel' }` stops the scene on `view: { screen: 'travel', fresh, note }`. Stations lit since the last opening are appended to `seenStations` right away and show `mrt.newStation` once. `ride` sets `rideTo` and puts the party on the car's entry tile. With `settings.skipRides` it puts them straight on the destination's `mrtArrival`. Either way the arrival tile's trigger plays, followed by whatever was left of the scene. The car door is `Exit.to: '@rideTo'` (no `at`), and it clears `rideTo`. If a car door is walked with no ride on record, the 路線圖 opens instead of leaving the party stuck. Two engine choices the bible doesn't spell out:
- **`Trigger.arrival`.** A not-once trigger on a car's entry tile would replay the announcement every time the party crossed the doorway on the way out. `arrival: true` makes a trigger fire only when the party arrives on its tile (exit or ride), never when walking onto it. `validateMrt` requires a car's entry trigger to be `arrival: true` and not `once`. Arrivals now honour `once` in general (all existing arrival triggers were `once`, so nothing changed).
- **`cleared` flag.** The `ending` step now also sets a `cleared` flag beside `run.cleared`, so 象山's unlock (`cleared`) and the script's `when: 'cleared'` talk rules work as flags.
Other details: `mrt.newStation`/`mrt.locked` and the screen's labels live in `UI` (`strings.ts`). They are system lines, not map events. `settings` moved into `QuestState` (`setSkipRides`, kept across new games, saved by `QuestGame`). Lines using station placeholders are measured with `lineWidth` (half-width Latin counts ½) against the 40-character limit, using the longest name of each field, because 「Next station, Taipei 101/World Trade Center.」 is 46 code points but reads as 24 full-width characters. `mrtArrival` must be a free tile reachable from the entry, not on an exit, and next to the `mrtGate` NPC when the map has one. Cars must have no random encounters. Save v6 (`baokaka.quest.v6`); v5 is legacy.
**Why.** Content-as-data keeps the engine ignorant of which chapters exist. Arrival-only triggers keep the bible's "entry trigger (not once)" without a doorway replay. The width rule follows how the dialogue box actually wraps.
**Consequences.** #27 builds the five cars with the `@rideTo` door on a `D` tile and an `arrival: true` entry trigger, and adds them to `MAP_ORDER`. Each chapter issue gives its landmark an `mrtGate` NPC and an adjacent `mrtArrival`, and keeps its `STATIONS` row's `map` equal to the new map id. #12/#56 restyle the 路線圖 (tokens are local constants in `TravelScreen.tsx` until #10). #55 fills the `TODO(#55)` audio hooks (`gate` when the 路線圖 opens, `chime` on ride start).

### D-27 · 2026-10-18 · Art tokens v2: relative world grade, map clocks, fonts, legacy colours
**Decision.** (Issue #10.) All colour lives in `src/art/palette.ts` (§ 3.1 tokens as `LINE`, `MATERIALS`, `EYES`, `RIM`, `WORLD`, `LIGHTS`, `UI`, `MRT`); `tailwind.config.ts` (now TypeScript) imports it, so the theme can't drift. Details the art direction leaves open:
- **World grade is relative to 00:00.** The § 3.1 world colours are already night-painted (they are what `options/d-anime/iso.png`, "midnight", uses), so multiplying them by the § 3.2 grade (×0.55 at midnight) would paint the city near-black. `gradeTokens(time)` multiplies `WORLD` by `grade(time) / grade(00:00)`: identity at midnight, lifting by up to ×(1.82, 1.75, 1.39) at 05:29. `LIGHTS` (windows, lamps, 101's rim, city glow) are never graded. Characters are authored in daylight colours, so `gradeCharacter(time)` applies half the table's grade, `1 − (1 − g)/2`, to `MATERIALS` directly (×0.78 at midnight, none at 05:29).
- **Sky keys.** `SKY_KEYS` encodes the table's "moon high/mid/low/setting" as a moon elevation (1 → below 0) and the dawn glow as `dawn` 0–1; "same" grades repeat the row above; "moon / local" and "local" rims are `localRim: true` with the moon colour as fallback. `skyAt` interpolates colours in sRGB and holds the 05:29 sky after the night (10:00 party, post-game).
- **Map clocks.** `GameMap.time` is optional; `mapClock()` (engine `clock.ts`) falls back to `LOCATION_CLOCK` (the § 4.2 times that differ from the card: daan 00:15, maokong 03:55, xinyi 05:10, summit 05:20) and then `CHAPTER_CLOCK` (the bible chapter times, D-16). Chapter maps can omit `time` unless they differ.
- **Fonts.** One `css2` link as § 7.1 prescribes, but Chiron GoRound TC loads **700/900 only**: the § 7.1 type scale never uses 500, and its rules cost 37 KB gzip on the render-blocking stylesheet. Measured: stylesheet 144 KB gzip (render-blocking), title screen woff2 1.51 MB in 11 slices after first paint (`display=swap`). First paint is within the 250 KB budget only because of swap; #68 cuts the total.
- **Legacy colours.** The old Tailwind colours (`ink`, `cream`, `sun`, …, `surface`, `bg`, `muted`) and `C` stay, marked deprecated, until #12/#16 replace the screens and sprites. With the page now navy, the old cream cards pin `text-ink`, and tonal buttons and neutral chips use a `tint-current` wash of the inherited text colour so they read on both surfaces. The legacy sizes `copy`/`headline`/`display`/`hero` stay beside the new scale; `caption`/`label`/`heading` took the § 7.1 values (14/16/22 px, bold).
- **Contrast.** `src/art/contrast.ts` lists every UI text/surface pair (`TEXT_PAIRS`); `contrast.test.ts` holds body text to 7:1 and muted to 4.5:1 against the solid panel and command-button stops.
**Why.** Keeps the reference art true at midnight while still walking the night toward dawn; keeps every pre-D screen playable on the new page until the UI kit lands.
**Consequences.** #11/#13 draw maps with `gradeTokens(mapClock(map))` and characters with `gradeCharacter`; #12 removes the legacy Tailwind colours and sizes once no screen uses them; #68 owns the font payload.


### D-28 · 2026-10-18 · Sprite rig contract v4: Fig output, LOD tiers, registry v2, placeholders
**Decision.** (Issue #11.) The option D contract lives in `src/art/sprite.ts` (`SpriteProps`, `SpriteComponent`, `defineSprite`, `Skeleton`/`Bones`, `SPRITE_BUDGETS`); the pieces are `geom.ts` (the `anime2.py` port), `Fig.tsx` (`Fig`, `SpriteSvg`, `RigGroup`, `SkeletonOverlay`), `defs.tsx` (`<ArtDefs/>`, `artUrl()`), `useSvgIds.ts`, `lod.ts` (`shows()`, `useLod()`), `rig.css`, `placeholder.tsx` and `spriteStats.ts`. Details the art direction leaves open:
- **Fig output.** A part's path is written once, unpainted, inside its group's contour `<g>` (which paints it `LINE.sil`), and the clip, the shifted base fill and the thin line reuse it with `<use>`. Coordinates go to the DOM through `compactPath()` (0.1u, relative commands); round joins and the 1.2u line width are set once on the sprite root. The Python `Fig` costs 8 elements and 4 copies of the path per part (`baokaka-stand.svg` is 232 elements, 41 KB); this costs 7 and 1, and the rig demo (the full reference head and body) is 205 elements / 18.3 KB at the portrait LOD. Rig groups double as depth groups and may nest (`children`), so eyes and mouth turn with the head; their origins are in the parent's units, which works with `transform-box: view-box` (checked in Chrome: the blink closes on the eye line, the head turns at the neck).
- **LOD tiers.** Layers declare the lowest tier they need (`map` < `battle` < `portrait`) or an `only` list; `icon` draws at battle detail and crops the viewBox to the head. Contours are 4u at the map LOD.
- **Registry v2.** `SPRITES` is `Record<SpriteName, SpriteComponent>`: legacy drawings (no props, no `meta`) type-check as components that ignore props, so every call site now passes `lod` (dialogue portrait, map map, party/item icons icon, battle and big screens battle). The legacy sprites' static gradient/clip ids were moved to `useSvgIds` (mechanical, no pixel change) so two copies on one screen no longer share ids and the lint can hold every sprite to the rule.
- **Budgets.** § 8.2 names hero 220, foe 160, NPC 120, tile 12 and 20 KB for heroes. Bosses get the hero's 220 (they are drawn as large as heroes), props and items the NPC's 120, and the 20 KB cap applies to every kind. Counted on serialised markup at every LOD.
- **Raw hex lint** covers every non-test source under `src/art/` except `palette.ts` (it reads them with `import.meta.glob`, as the project has no Node types). Nothing needs the allow-list today (the legacy sprites already used `C`), so it starts empty; new tokens the rig needed went into `palette.ts`: `MATERIALS.pajamaFar`/`tabbyFar` (full far-limb ramps from `anime2.py`'s `pj2`/`fur2`; § 3.1 lists only the pajama's far base), `ACCENTS` (eye, mouth, blush, star-print and footie details from the generators), `PLACEHOLDER` and `DEV` (skeleton overlay).
- **Placeholders.** `registerPlaceholder(name, { kind, label })` returns a tagged `SpriteComponent` (no global registry: a placeholder counts once it is in `SPRITES`). The label's first CJK character is HTML over the SVG, sized in container units, since sprites never use `<text>`. `spriteWarnings()` in `validate.ts` lists them; `QuestGame` prints them in dev and `placeholder.test.ts` logs the live count.
- **Reduced motion.** `rig.css` honours the OS setting and a `.reduce-motion` ancestor class, ready for the in-game 「減少動態」 toggle (no toggle exists yet).
**Why.** The reference implementation's markup would not fit the budgets it is held to; one shared path per part fixes that without changing the look. Passing `lod` at every call site now means #16–#19 can swap sprites in without touching screens.
**Consequences.** #16–#18 author heroes/NPCs/foes as `defineSprite` components built on `Fig` with `rig` metadata (the art lab overlays any sprite's skeleton the same way as the demo's), add their own art-lab cells, and delete legacy files from `src/art/sprites/` as they replace them. Content issues may add `registerPlaceholder` entries; #58 makes `spriteWarnings()` an error. The in-game reduced-motion toggle should set `.reduce-motion` on the app root.

### D-29 · 2026-10-18 · UI kit v2: dumb kit, card timing, iris, settings, legacy removal
**Decision.** (Issue #12.) The kit lives in `src/components/` and never imports the engine: `kit.css` (every § 7.2–7.9 style, colours only through `theme()`), `Panel`/`Inset`/`Diamonds`, `Button` (primary / secondary / text, sm 44 · md 48 · lg 56 px), `IconButton`, `CommandButton` (70 px), `Switch`, `GlassChip`/`Num`/`Tag`, `Bar`/`StatBars`, `Sheet`/`ListRow`, `DialogueBox`, `ChapterCard`, `DepartureClock`, `CommandPanel`/`CommandGrid`/`PartyCard`/`HeadRing`, plus the pure `typing.ts` and `motion.tsx` (`useReducedMotion`, `usePresence`). The game adapters are `quest/components/Dialogue.tsx` (`TalkBox`, `speakerOf`, `Bust`, `linePage`) and the screens. Details the issue and § 7 leave open:
- **Chapter card timing.** "2.4s total; tap skips" is read like the dialogue: the card plays for 2.4 s and then waits; a tap while it plays jumps to its end state (`.kit-card--done`, the same end state as reduced motion), the next tap moves on. It never auto-advances, so a grandparent can read the title and screenshots are deterministic. The card's 「下一站」 is a new optional `next` on the `chapter` step (≤ 8 characters, needs a `time`); the landmark is a `landmark` path prop with a generic stepped skyline until chapter art supplies one. Cards without a `time` (the old story) show no clock and use the 00:00 sky.
- **Text over the card's sky** sits on a radial navy scrim of `CARD_SCRIM` = 0.62 alpha; `contrast.test.ts` checks the title at ≥ 7:1 over every chapter clock's sky, dawn included.
- **Iris.** The reducer changes maps in one step, so the map → map iris (§ 7.9) is the opening half only: a navy iris opens on the party (the camera centre) over 400 ms on every map change while the world screen is mounted.
- **Dialogue.** The bust is the speaker's sprite at `lod="portrait"` filling a square of min(300 px, 38 dvh), set 10% down and clipped 44 px under the box's top edge; `Line.expr` is passed through. Backlights are `BACKLIGHT` tokens; `SPEAKERS` entries gained a `role` (family, folk, foe, friend → warm, warm, violet, moon) and an optional `color`, heroes use `HERO_BACKLIGHT`. Page dots count the run of consecutive lines in the scene (a window of 7 for longer runs). Choices are all secondary buttons (a quiz must not hint); the inn's 「好」 is primary. Typing restarts on line identity, so a repeated line still retypes. `【詞】` key terms are validated (paired, non-empty, not nested).
- **One primary per screen.** The shop's buy buttons are secondary with a gold price (a list of equal choices); the reset confirmation makes 「取消」 the gold one.
- **Battle panel.** Two heroes get wide cards (48 px heads, 「體力 84/100 元氣 9」); three or four get narrow cards in one row (bare numbers; heads only with three), which keeps the panel near § 7.4's 346 px at 390×844. 逃跑 (or 「逃不掉」 in boss fights, 取消 while picking) is the prompt-row link; 接下來 moved into the stage's log plate. The stage itself is untouched apart from kit chips and damage numbers (#14).
- **Settings.** 「減少動態」 is `settings.reducedMotion` (save v6 stays: a v6 save without it loads with `false`), toggled in the menu sheet's new 設定 tab beside 聲音. `QuestScreens` sets `.reduce-motion` on `<html>` (so portalled sheets and `rig.css` follow) and provides `ReducedMotionContext`; JS-timed parts use `useReducedMotion()` (toggle OR the OS query). Under reduced motion every loop, flip, draw-on, twinkle and pulse stops; slides become 200 ms fades; the card fades in. Typing still types (it is reading pace, not motion).
- **Legacy removed.** The legacy Tailwind colours, sizes (`copy`/`headline`/`display`/`hero`), shadows (`card`/`float`/`glow`/`inset`), `rounded-4xl`, `LEGACY_UI`, `tint-current` and the `screen-in`/`sheet-in`/`fade-in` utilities are gone. `C` stays for the legacy sprites (#16+).
**Why.** A dumb kit can be shown alone in the art lab (`section=ui`) and reused by #14/#19/#20 without dragging the engine along; deterministic card timing keeps both readers and visual QA calm.
**Consequences.** #14 restyles the stage around `CommandPanel` (heroes on the diagonal, 輪到你 ring, foe plate, MRT-door transition). #19 fills the menu tabs inside the same `Sheet`/`ListRow` frame. #21+ may write `【詞】`, `expr` and chapter `next` in content. #56 replaces the title art. `npm run check:ui` checks typing reflow, tap targets and reduced motion in the browser.


### D-30 · 2026-10-18 · Iso map renderer v2: materials, ground SVG, walls, sky, lights, ambient
**Decision.** (Issue #13.) The map stack of art direction § 8.4 is `SkyLayer` (screen space, `src/art/sky/`) under `IsoMap`'s camera layer: `Ground` (`src/art/tiles/`), `Ambient` (`src/art/fx/`), the tap marker, the sorted actors and `LightLayer` (`src/art/lights/`). Details the issue and § 4.4 leave open:
- **Materials.** `Terrain` = 28 `Material`s (the § 4.4 list, with 101's "polished stone and carpet" split into `polished` and `carpet`, 北投's "wet stone and wooden deck" into `wetStone` and `deck`, and the home's `rug` and `playMat` from § 4.2) + the structures `door`/`wall`/`fence`/`void` + the old names. The engine keeps only walkability and heights (`TERRAIN`, `TERRAIN_ALIAS`, `surfaceOf()` in iso.ts); looks are `TILE_MATERIALS` (pattern, `wet`, `liquid`, `seamless`) in `src/art/tiles/terrain.ts` and `TILES`/`STRUCTURE`/`FIXTURE` ramps in palette.ts, painted for 00:00 and graded with the same world grade as `gradeTokens` by `gradeTiles(time)`. Old names: floor → wood, tile → undergroundTile, path and sand → gravel, cobble → stoneSlab, dark → carpet, blanket → playMat (#21 deletes them).
- **Ground SVG.** Six symbols per material (base/alt × three seeded mark layouts); `tileVariant()` hashes (c, r, map id). Marble (and `polished`) take the fill from parity, everything else from the hash. The lit edge is skipped on seamless rugs and carpets. Kerbs are drawn on the near tile's far edges where two different ramps meet; water gets a bank shadow band. Walls: a face is drawn only where the neighbour in front is lower, each height group faces-then-tops; walls with floor in front stand 34 px, walls whose room is behind them (the camera side) are cut to an 8 px kerb so they never hide the party; fences are railings (post per tile, two rails). The skirt hangs 30 px under every edge that meets void, with each material's `deep` strata band.
- **Sky.** `HORIZON` at 40 % of the view; moon height from `sky.moon` (highest at ~¼ of the view, under the HUD), the sun from `dawn ≥ 0.85`; stars fade with dawn. Six blurred elements (milky band, star halos, clouds, city glow, bokeh, haze), all in static layers moved by `transform`; parallax stops under reduced motion. `GameMap.interior` (new) swaps in a dark room with a moon glow for maps whose sky shows only through windows.
- **Lights.** `GameMap.lights` and `neon` as specced, plus: standing kinds (`STANDING_LIGHTS`: lamp, lantern, neon) need a whole walkable tile, claim it and are solid (path.ts), and draw a fixture actor (post, lantern stand, sign pole); windows, screens and moon glints are light only and may sit between tiles. Every exit gets a warm door glow. The layer is gradients only (bloom, pool, a lamp cone, streaks on `wet` materials). At most `MAX_LIGHTS` = 24 per map.
- **Ambient.** `GameMap.ambient` with `AMBIENT_ELEMENTS` per kind and `MAX_AMBIENT_ELEMENTS` = 6 checked by the validator; the registry has leaves (2), ripples (2, need water), fireflies (3); steam, lantern sway, mist and rain are typed but render nothing until their chapter registers them.
- **Actors.** Moved by `transform`; decor sorts by its front-most tile (`depth`); party and NPCs are drawn ≈ 0.9 tile wide (`ACTOR_SIZE`); legacy sprites get a contact shadow from the map (option D sprites draw their own). `--rim` comes from `sceneRim(time, neon)` (pink on neon maps, the lamp rim at "local" hours); `SceneClock` + `useCharacterTokens()` give option D sprites the half grade (legacy sprites can't be graded without a filter and stay as drawn).
- **Old maps** got `interior`, a few lights and Ch1–2-style ambient so they show the system until #21 replaces them.
**Why.** Keeps the engine colour-free and the ground static (one SVG, zero mutations while walking), so the only per-frame work is actor transforms and the camera.
**Consequences.** Chapter art issues (#22+) author maps with materials, `lights`, `ambient`, `neon` and `interior`, register their ambient kinds, and draw landmarks as props. Perf (dev build, 4× throttle): main-thread time per frame fell from ~31 ms (old per-tile DOM) to ~18–23 ms, still above the § 8.5 12 ms, which is measured on the dev build (React development mode); #57 measures it on a production build and makes it a gate.

### D-31 · 2026-10-18 · Battle stage v2: stage units, lazy backdrops, frame variants, transitions
**Decision.** (Issue #14.) The battle stage of art direction § 7.4 is `Stage` (`src/art/backdrops/Stage.tsx`) driven by a `StageConfig` (`types.ts`: `time?`, `sky: open | room`, `horizon`, `moon?`, `far`, `mid`, `framers: [left, right]`, `floor: { kind, reflections }`, `lights`, `bokeh`). Details the issue and § 4.1/§ 8.4 leave open:
- **Stage units.** Everything is authored in a 390×500 frame (the stage at 390×844) but painted from x = −110 to 500, drawn with `xMidYMid slice`: on a portrait phone the SVG scales to the stage height and crops the sides, so the floor and horizon never move relative to the fighters. Fighters use the same scale `k` for size and height and spread across the box's full width (x as a fraction of 390), so nobody is cropped. Layers are painted sky → far → floor → mid → lights → framers → bokeh → vignette (the floor goes under the architecture that stands on it).
- **Static and memoised.** The backdrop is one SVG memoised on (config, clock); config objects come from module tables, so turns never re-render it (`npm run check:ui` plays three turns and counts 0 mutations inside `[data-stage-static]`). The arena's quake now shakes a wrapper around the fighters only. Blur stays ≤ 8 elements per stage (6 in the template; a vitest checks every config at three clock times). Nothing that animates carries a filter: `fx-hurt`'s white flash became an opacity blink and a KO'd hero slumps instead of turning grey (the UI check samples every running animation's target).
- **Keys and chunks.** `GameMap.backdrop?: string` names a key in `BACKDROP_CHAPTER` (`keys.ts`, no React, so the validator can check it; unknown keys are errors). Each chapter's configs live in `chNN.tsx`, loaded with `import()` (`CHAPTER_CHUNKS`; Vite names the chunk `chNN-<hash>.js`; `ch01` is 3.1 KB gzip). `generic` (a city park) and `genericRoom` (an interior with a window, for `interior` maps) are in the main bundle, take the map's clock and are the fallback while a chunk loads or if it fails. The world screen preloads its map's chunk. The template is `daan` (大安森林公園's 露天音樂台 at its own clock, 00:20); the old `park` map carries it until #21.
- **Frames.** `battleFrame(battle)`: random fights `none`; boss fights `boss` = the 布袋戲 valance only (vermilion band, gold rules, scalloped lobes with gold studs); fights with a foe flagged `final` (new `Foe.final`, must also be `boss`) get `final` = the full proscenium (cornice of studs, tassels, carved pillars with gold inner rules, corner brackets, an apron). This reconciles § 6.3 ("chapter bosses use the standard stage; the final fights use the proscenium") with D-08 ("B's stage frame for boss fights"). To switch later, change `battleFrame` alone. The frame drops in over 400 ms; the HUD moves under the valance when there is one. Under the full proscenium the fighters step 22 units in from the edges.
- **Layout.** Four heroes on two staggered rows (寶咖咖 and 摩卡 in front, 黑糖 behind-left, 阿藍 behind-right); regular foes 94–112 units, a boss 204, a final foe 330, escorts beside a boss 86. The panel is `CommandPanel tight` with 2×2 `PartyCard layout="strip"` cards (48 px heads): 346 px at 390×844 (stage 498 px). The § 7.4 commands stay 70 px, so on short screens (< 740 px tall, e.g. 375×667) the cards drop to 34 px heads and one number row instead of scaling the whole panel: the panel is 323 px and the stage 344 px (52 %).
- **Foes.** Per-foe mini HP bars; a violet aura behind every foe and a gold one behind bosses (§ 6.1, gradients); `--rim` = `RIM.foe` on foes. The foe plate counts standing foes. The intent preview is shown above the acting foe on its turn and under each foe's name while picking a target (「下一招 …」).
- **Numbers.** Fredoka 44 italic white with a deep-pink stroke and glow; crits 50 in gold with 「效果絕佳！」, normal hits 「好痛！」, heals green with 「好多了」; rise 24 px and fade over 600 ms (reduced motion: no rise).
- **Calmed state.** On a foe's KO step it wobbles under a ring of dizzy stars, sparkles and hops away (1 s); legacy sprites can't change expression, so swirl-eyes → smile waits for the chapter foe sprites (they receive `state="ko"` on that step). A won fight waits 1.3 s (calm, then a gold radial wash) before the victory screen, whose result card now rises over 600 ms.
- **Map → battle.** `QuestScreens` decides during render: when the view goes world → battle it keeps drawing the last world state (frozen, inputs ignored) under two navy MRT door panels (window, the station line's colour stripe, red when the map has no station) closing over 280 ms, an amber chime light (120 ms, flash ≤ 0.35), then the battle with the doors opening (320 ms). Under reduced motion there are no doors: the battle's own 200 ms fade-in plays.
**Why.** One coordinate system for art and actors keeps the scene coherent on every phone; lazy chapter chunks keep the main bundle flat as fourteen chapters of backdrops arrive.
**Consequences.** Chapter art issues add `chNN.tsx` configs and `BACKDROP_CHAPTER` keys, set `backdrop` on their maps, and may replace the generic fallback by location; the final-fight fixture uses a frame override until 咻咻's forms exist (#22-series, Ch14). The foe plate can cover a final foe's head on short phones; the Ch14 art issue should tune `FINAL_PLACE` with the real sprite. #15 builds bespoke skill FX inside the stage's FX layer (z 550).

### D-32 · 2026-10-18 · Battle VFX: kinds, accents, the impact-frame rule, budgets and flashes
**Decision.** (Issue #15.) The battle effects of art direction § 7.10 are `FX_KINDS` (engine `types.ts`: blocks, cry, claw, feather, paw, heal, shield, typhoon, impact, sparkle, neon, water, steam, dizzy) drawn by `src/art/fx/` (`battleFx.ts` pure planning, `BattleFxArt.tsx` drawings, `battleFx.css` motion) and staged by `quest/components/BattleFx.tsx`. Details the issue and § 7.9/7.10 leave open:
- **Accents.** A skill's flourish is `Spell.fxAccent` (`FX_ACCENTS`: `clap` 拍拍手, `stomp` 勇敢的第一步, `comet` 長尾流星, `moon` 月牙衝擊), carried on the `act` event as `accent`, so a kind stays a reusable vocabulary and the four special moves don't need four kinds. 九命回春 uses `heal` (party), not `sparkle`, per the issue table.
- **Plain attacks** get a look per hero, `HEROES[hero].attackFx`: 寶咖咖 `blocks` (her 攻擊 is a thrown block, as on the D battle mock), 摩卡 `claw`, 阿藍 `feather`, 黑糖 `paw`. Items stay `sparkle`. Foe moves were mapped to the nearest kind (thud/jet → impact, splash/wave → water, shout → cry, bolt → neon, dust → steam/feather/paw by meaning, slash → claw/impact/dizzy, 雷聲隆隆 → typhoon); an attack without `fx` falls back to `impact`. `validateFx` (in `validateQuestContent`) checks every spell, accent, hero attack and foe move.
- **Anticipation** is a soft gold glyph ring at the caster's feet with the hero's emblem lying in it as gold line art (寶咖咖 block, 摩卡 paw, 阿藍 feather, 黑糖 moon), only for hero 才藝 (`arcane`), not for plain attacks, items or foes.
- **Timing.** The strike connects at `IMPACT_AT` = 120 + 90 = 210 ms: impacts, the hurt reaction (squash + the 2-frame blink), the quake and the damage number all start there; the hero/foe lunges follow the 490 ms § 7.9 profile. Each further target lands 50 ms later. Damage numbers moved into their own layer above the effects (z 560) so the resolution layer is never covered.
- **Impact-frame rule.** Every FX node's resting style (no animation) is its impact frame. The art lab's `still=1` turns FX animations off (so the FX sheet and the `battle-fx` fixture show impacts, not a moment 0.5 s in), and reduced motion keeps only the shape (and accent) nodes with an opacity-only `fx-still` (in 120 ms, held, out over 200 ms); rings, particles, the glyph and the wash are not drawn.
- **Budget.** At most 40 FX nodes (separately animated elements) in flight: `planFx` gives each target `floor((40 − glyph − wash) / targets) − its shape/ring/accent nodes` particles, capped at 10. Real fights always get 6–10 (an all-target skill on three foes is 39 nodes); a vitest checks every kind × accent × 1–4 targets, `npm run check:ui` counts nodes in the browser.
- **Flashes.** A wash (≤ 0.3 opacity, 200 ms, `screen` blend) plays only when an action lands on several targets (or for a typhoon). BattleScreen allows a flash step (a hit's blink or a wash) only 500 ms after the previous one (`flashAllowed`); a step inside that window plays `fx-hurt-soft` (squash, no blink) and no wash.
- **The shield** is one magpie-bridge arch over the standing party, sized from the outermost heroes, legs on the front row's floor.
**Why.** Kinds stay few and reusable for the chapter art; the impact-frame rule gives deterministic screenshots and a reduced-motion fallback for free; the budget and flash limit are enforced by code rather than by review.
**Consequences.** Chapter art issues reuse the kinds (and may add one bespoke kind each with its keyframes in `battleFx.css`, keeping the budget and the impact-frame rule; the CSS test checks new keyframes). Option D rig sprites (#16–#18) get their `rig--hurt` delayed to the impact and their blink dropped with `fx-hurt-soft` already (battle.css).

### D-33 · 2026-10-18 · Heroes I: action poses, KO poses, the map walk flipbook, map LOD simplification, backlights
**Decision.** (Issue #16, PR #76.) 寶咖咖 (`src/art/characters/baokaka/`) and 摩卡貓 (`src/art/characters/mocha/`) are ports of `_gen/anime2.py` and the heads in `_gen/anime.py`, number for number. Details the issue and art direction leave open:
- **Poses beyond the sheet.** 寶咖咖: `clap` (拍拍手: sit legs, both forearms swung in, hands meeting over the chest; the block put down by his far foot), `cry` (哇哇大哭: sit, head thrown back 8°, the far arm dropped to a fist, the `wail` face: squeezed eyes, tear streams, mouth wide open), `lie` (KO 累倒了: flopped on his tummy asleep, eyes softly closed). `throw` (丟積木) is the sit pose with the raised arm winding back; `firstStep` (勇敢的第一步) is the stand pose with a wobble-and-charge body animation. 摩卡: `pounce` (the walk's trunk turned 14° nose-up and lifted 20u, front legs reaching, hind legs driving, round `hunt` pupils) and `loaf` (KO, paws tucked under, tail wrapped, eyes shut). Both heroes draw their KO as a pose (`rig--rest`), never tipped over; the old grey-out/tilt stays for legacy sprites only.
- **Who picks the pose.** `actionPose(fx, accent)` maps a move's look to a pose (blocks → throw, cry → cry, clap accent → clap, stomp accent → firstStep, claw → pounce); BattleScreen passes it with `state="attack"` and `state="victory"` once the fight is won. With no `expr`, the sprite picks the face from its state (hurt → 驚訝, victory → 大笑/開心, KO → asleep); an explicit `expr` always wins. 摩卡's default tail follows his face (happy → up, surprised → puff, otherwise wrapped); `flick` (annoyed) is opt-in and animated.
- **Bracelet.** The red string with its gold bead is on his right wrist only (the near arm in sit and crawl, the viewer's left in stand); the generator also drew one on the raised arm, which would contradict § 5.1.
- **Map walk = a two-frame flipbook.** Animating SVG rig groups (the #11 `rig-swing` cycle) repaints a whole sprite every frame: +40 % main-thread time while the party walked. Mounting walk frames when a walk starts re-renders the sprite twice per walk: still +15 %. So at the map LOD `SpriteSvg` always stacks two static key frames (`.rig-flip > .rig--frame-{0,1}`; diagonal pairs forward, the body rising on the second); the actor box toggles `.rig-walking` (no React work) and only the frames' opacity steps, one frame per `--beat` = 147 ms, so a stride covers a tile (SPEED 3.4 tiles/s). The cat uses the same two frames (a trot) rather than four beats: four frames added ~500 DOM nodes and broke the budget. Idle map sprites don't breathe or blink (invisible at 60 px, and a repaint per frame). Moving actors get `will-change: transform`.
- **Map LOD drawing.** At the map LOD `Fig` writes each part's path inline instead of `<use>` (a `<use>` is a shadow tree the browser keeps in sync), skips the 1.2u material line and draws parts whose cel offset is < 3u flat (a < 1 px band at 60 px); torsos and anything with `deep`/`extra` marks keep their cel. Per-frame budgets: the lint test divides by the frames in the markup.
- **Measured** (`--perf --perf-fixture world-follower`, dev build, 4× throttle, four alternating runs): main 23.96 ms vs 24.90 ms main-thread per frame (+4 %), DOM 1,600 → 1,864. The main JS bundle grew 164.1 → 176.9 KB gzip.
- **Backlights.** The issue asks for butter (寶咖咖) and tabby orange (摩卡): new `BACKLIGHT.butter` and `BACKLIGHT.tabby` replace gold and jade for the heroes and `mochaNpc` (jade stays as a token).
**Why.** The heroes are on screen every second: the approved anatomy must survive every LOD, and walking must stay as cheap as the legacy sprites.
**Consequences.** #17/#18 build 阿藍, 黑糖 and the cast the same way (`src/art/characters/kit.tsx` helpers, the flipbook comes free from `SpriteSvg` at the map LOD) and add their own action poses to `actionPose`. The story ending passes `lock` to show the 金鎖片. The art lab's silhouettes take the first `<svg>` of a map sprite.

---

## 2026-10-19 · The pivot to 《翻翻台北》 Pop-Up Taipei

D-01 to D-33 describe the RPG and stay as history. Where they conflict with the entries below, the entries below win (they supersede D-01's "keep only the RPG" in substance, D-03's 14 chapters, D-08's art option D for characters and environments, and the "inline SVG only" art rule; D-11's "`main` is always playable" and D-13/D-17/D-18/D-21's tooling decisions still apply).

### D-34 · 2026-10-19 · Pivot to concept A, 《翻翻台北》 Pop-Up Taipei
**Decision.** (Owner.) The RPG 《寶咖咖抓周大冒險》 is stopped. The game becomes concept A of `docs/design/concepts.md`, 《翻翻台北》 Pop-Up Taipei: a portrait, one-thumb, no-fail paper-diorama puzzle game where 寶咖咖 crawls by his own desires and the player rearranges the page (tabs, wheels, flaps), places 摩卡 and uses baby words. `docs/design/gdd.md` (v1.0) is the single source of truth: 65 pages (prologue + 10 chapters × 6 + the 抓周 ending), milestones M0–M5. The premise (抓周, 咻咻, 05:30, 土地公, first word 「摩卡」) is reused from `docs/story/`; party members, battles, shops, coins, levels and side quests are dropped.
**Why.** After #16 the owner judged the character art poor and the game still too shaped by the old design (a 仙劍-style RPG). Concept A is more original, plays for all three generations on the sofa, and its "no fail" rules can be proven by a solver.
**Consequences.** New epic #78 with issues #79–#141 (roadmap order, not numeric). The new game lives in `src/popup/` and reuses `src/shared/`, selected `src/components/` pieces, palette/font tokens and the Playwright visual QA tooling. Engineering on #17 stopped (it had been on hold).

### D-35 · 2026-10-19 · Character art v2: redrawn from the owner's photos, authored in SVG, baked to WebP
**Decision.** (Owner + character artist.) 寶咖咖 and 摩卡 are redrawn in a TV-anime illustration style from the owner's real photos (`docs/art/v2/`, `likeness-check.md`): 寶咖咖 has straight black hair and a two-piece outfit (not a onesie); 摩卡 is a grey-brown tabby (not orange). Art is code-drawn SVG (`docs/art/v2/src/*.py`) with no element budget, rendered by Chromium (`tools/render.mjs`) and **baked to WebP** @2x/@3x with a manifest (`tools/bake.py`, `tools/bake_poses.py` → `baked/poses.json` with fps, loop flags and foot anchors). The game displays only bitmaps, mirrors right-facing frames for left (`anchor_x' = size_pt[0] − anchor_x`), and adds the standee border at bake time.
**Why.** Filters, blurs and masks on ~700-path figures are far too heavy to render live on phones; baking keeps the detail at the cost of one image per frame (Art v2 §2.3–2.4).
**Consequences.** CI has no Python or browsers, so baked files are committed and a test checks manifest ↔ files (#94). Supersedes the character parts of `docs/art/art-direction.md` and the D-28/D-33 option-D rig for the new game. Missing frames (look L/R, watch-sway, ride-giggle, touch-watch, carried, climb ×3; 摩卡 glare, purr, in-box) are tracked in #141; cycles are 8 frames, not the GDD's 6.

### D-36 · 2026-10-19 · Environment style: pop-up paper-craft; bitmaps allowed
**Decision.** (Owner + environment artist.) Environments, the UI frame and motion follow `docs/art/popup/README.md`: 阿嬤's top-bound pop-up book opened to 90°, tunnel-book z layers (z0–z5) with baked shadows and cut-edge rims, paper stocks (card, kraft, washi, vellum, foil, glassine…), the chapter colour clock baked per page, characters as die-cut standees, and the grandparent rule: **touchables are reserved marigold with a red verb seal** (and a lint keeps decoration away from marigold). Backdrops and parts are baked to WebP by `scripts/bake-popup.mjs` (#96); `src/kit.js` is ported to TS as the default placeholder art for every part preset (#139).
**Why.** The paper book makes indirect control readable ("this is a toy, touch the gold parts"), fits portrait phones, and stays cheap at runtime when baked.
**Consequences.** The RPG-era rule "inline SVG only, no bitmaps" no longer applies to the new game: baked bitmaps are the norm, live SVG only for cheap flat pieces, never a runtime filter on anything that moves, page art ≤ 350 KB (CI check). Supersedes `art-direction.md` §2.7, §4, §7 for this game; its colour clock (§3.2), contrast rules (§3.3) and fonts (§7.1) still apply.

### D-37 · 2026-10-19 · Retiring the RPG
**Decision.** Until the routing switch (#109) `#/` keeps serving the RPG and the new game is reachable at `#/popup` (unlinked, no flag), with PageLab at `#/pagelab` in dev builds. At #109 `#/` (and `#/quest`, unknown hashes) switch to 翻翻台北, the RPG drops out of the bundle but stays in the repo unrouted, and the RPG save keys (`baokaka.quest`, `.v2`–`.v6`) are deleted at boot with a one-time warm notice on the cover; the new save key is `popup.save.v1` (#93). After the M1 playtest and the owner's sign-off (#110), #111 deletes `src/quest/`, RPG-only art, art lab sections, `scripts/sim.ts` and the RPG visual baselines.
**Why.** GDD §3.1 / §6 risk 12: no tangle between old and new code, `main` always playable, and a revert path until the slice is proven.
**Consequences.** Until #111, `npm test` still runs the RPG tests; nothing new may import from `src/quest/`.

### D-38 · 2026-10-19 · Old issues: what was closed and what was kept
**Decision.** Epic #1 is closed as **not planned** (superseded by #78): its goal, the RPG, will not ship; the completed part (#2–#16) is already closed as completed issue by issue. #17–#56 are closed as not planned (RPG heroes, cast, menus, collections, the 14 chapters and ride cars, post-game, side quests, balance, RPG audio and polish), each with a comment naming its successor where one exists. #57 (a11y/perf) and #58 (final QA) are closed as not planned and replaced by #136 and #138, which keep their automation ideas against the new budgets. **#68 (font payload) is kept**, rewritten for `src/popup/content/` and moved to #78's M5.
**Why.** The old issues describe RPG content and systems that no longer exist; rewriting them in place would hide history and break the roadmap order. #68 is game-agnostic.
**Consequences.** Successors: #55 → #105 (audio), #57 → #136, #58 → #138, chapter issues → the new chapter issues #114–#135 (see `roadmap.md`).

### D-39 · 2026-10-19 · Scaffold of `src/popup/`: entry, lazy chunks, the core boundary, the seed `POPUP` tokens
**Decision.** (#79.) The game's entry is `src/popup/PopupGame.tsx` (the GDD §3.5 folders sit beside it, each with an `index.ts` saying what goes there). App lazy-loads it as its own chunk in every build, and lazy-loads `src/popup/dev/PageLab.tsx` only when `import.meta.env.DEV` (the art lab pattern), so production drops PageLab and `#/pagelab` falls through to the RPG. The core boundary test (`src/popup/core/boundary.test.ts`) allows imports only inside `core/` and of `src/shared/random.ts`, and also rejects DOM globals, timers, `Date.now`/`new Date` and `Math.random` (the README's purity rule); `*.test.ts` files are exempt so tests can import vitest. `POPUP` in `src/art/palette.ts` starts with the kit's cover, gold, page, ink and shadow tokens; its text pairs live in `contrast.ts` (`POPUP_TEXT_PAIRS`) and are all held to 7:1 (GDD §1.3 has no muted level). `inkSoft` (`#5B4A5E`, 6.6:1 on the page) is left out until a screen needs it and the pair is checked.
**Why.** A lazy chunk keeps the RPG's main bundle flat until #109 (it grew 0.2 KB gzip, for the route and the lazy stubs); a boundary checked by a test is cheaper than review discipline; seeding only what the placeholder uses leaves the full palette port to #139 without conflicts.
**Consequences.** #139 extends `POPUP` (and its text pairs) and mirrors it into Tailwind. #109 can import `PopupGame` eagerly when it becomes `#/`. Core code may name its own types `Node`, `Event` or `history`: the forbidden-globals list is deliberately short.

### D-40 · 2026-10-19 · The kit port: filters only with a flag, flat stand-ins in game, contrast exceptions, preset art rules
**Decision.** (#139.) `docs/art/popup/src/kit.js` is ported to `src/popup/art/` as pure functions returning SVG strings, each taking a `KitCtx` `{ id, filters }`. `id(name)` scopes every def id per document (React callers build it from `useSvgIds`, the bake and tests from `kitCtx(scope)`); `filters` is set **only** by PageLab and the bake (#96). Without it (the in-game default) helpers draw the same paper with no SVG filter: depth = a two-step offset shadow, a contact shadow and a 1 px cut-edge rim drawn as flat copies of the piece's outline; blurs become radial-gradient blobs; grain, deckle, torn edges and the standee border are left out (they are baked). Randomness goes through `src/shared/random.ts` (mulberry32, not kit.js's LCG), so seeded shapes match the proofs in character, not point for point. Every colour moved to `palette.ts`: `POPUP` (≈ 120 solid tokens), `POPUP_FOIL` (gold, gold trim, coin, silver stops) and `POPUP_CHAPTER_CLOCK` (bible §3.3), with `POPUP` mirrored into Tailwind as `popup-*`. Contrast: `inkSoft` darkens from kit `#5B4A5E` (6.6:1) to `#4A3A4E` (8.5:1), red text on paper uses a new `sealInk` `#8C1A14` (7.5:1) instead of seal red (3.8:1), and the seal's white glyph on seal red (4.4:1) is held to the WCAG 1.4.11 non-text level of 3:1 (`POPUP_GRAPHIC_PAIRS`), because the bible fixes the seal red and the glyph is one of four redundant cues, drawn ≥ 22 px bold. The slider's seal grows from kit r 9 to r 11 so every seal is ≥ 22 px (bible §4). Default part art (`src/popup/art/presets.ts`, registered as the fallback in `src/popup/view/art/registry.ts`): only player-driven presets (tab, slider, wheel, flap, and a loop when it has a crank) are marigold with a verb seal; section, gate, button, lift, pushable, light, blowable, carrier and prop are printed card (grey-lilac, kraft, navy, foil, vellum), including tappable lights and props, as the bible's props rule says.
**Why.** GDD §3.9 and bible §9.1 forbid runtime filters on moving elements, but M0/M1 need art before the bake exists; flat stand-ins keep the paper read at a few extra nodes per part. Scoped ids are the existing `useSvgIds` rule. The contrast changes are the smallest that meet GDD R5 without changing the bible's look.
**Consequences.** #80 should own the preset list (`PART_PRESETS` lives in `presets.ts` until core types exist; the art should then import the core type). #96 bakes with `kitCtx(scope, { filters: true })`. #89 renders parts with `renderPart(ctx, art, preset, { state, states, touchable })`. When the artist changes `kit.js`, port the diff and update the snapshots in `src/popup/art/__snapshots__/`.

### D-41 · 2026-10-19 · Core types: what the GDD § 3.2 contract left open
**Decision.** (#80.) `src/popup/core/types.ts` is GDD § 3.2 verbatim except for these tweaks, mirrored in the GDD: (1) **`PartDef.states` and `initial` are optional**: `core/presets.ts` fills the fixed-state presets (flap, gate, button, pushable, light, carrier from its route) and `initial` defaults to the first state; count-based presets (tab, slider, wheel, section, lift, loop, blowable) and `prop` must list their states, and `compilePart` throws otherwise (lint, #86, will report it). (2) The GDD's `crank: true` (loop) and `tappable` (light) are **expressed through `input`**, not new fields: a crank is `drag-rotary` or `tap-cycle`, a tappable light `tap-toggle`; each preset has a default input and a list of allowed ones (`PRESETS[preset].inputs`). (3) Named types the GDD used without defining: `LineKey = string` (narrowed by `content/script.ts` later), `PartPreset` from the `PART_PRESETS` const (core owns it; `src/popup/art/presets.ts` re-exports it, closing the D-40 follow-up), `PartInput`, `DefinePage`, and `BabyMode` as the § 2.2.7 names without parameters (`looking(n)` → `baby.lookCount`, `carried(by, k)` → `baby.carriedBy`, `saying(word)` is one tick and its word travels as the queued event). (4) **Pushable parts keep their state in `SimState.pushables`, not in `parts`**; `evalCond`'s `{ part, in }` reads either record (`partState`). (5) Tap semantics: tab/slider advance one stop and cycle; a wheel's states are listed clockwise and a tap goes to the next; toggle swaps the first two states (any other state goes to the first); prop and crank cycle; `input: 'none'` disables taps on any preset. (6) `evalCond`: unknown ids are false, `all: []` is true, `any: []` false, `mochaAt` needs Mocha settled (`perch` matches and `path` empty). (7) `canonicalKey` = JSON with sorted object keys, `undefined` members dropped, and `baby.refused`, `consumed`, `flags` sorted; Mocha's `path` keeps its order.
**Why.** The issue asks for defaults "when states are omitted", which the verbatim type could not express; reusing `input` keeps one source of truth for player control (the art's "touchable" and the lint both read it); a separate `pushables` record is what § 3.2 already declares and avoids two copies of one state.
**Consequences.** #81 onward build on `compilePart`, `evalCond`/`holds`, `initialState` and `canonicalKey`; the fixture `src/popup/core/fixtures/c1p2.ts` (GDD § 3.4 plus the `board` prop) is shared by the engine tests. #86's lint checks `input ∈ PRESETS[preset].inputs`, that count-based presets list states, and that `initial ∈ states`. The step (#84) owns locks, `stateWhen` and `Reaction.to` (`next`/`prev`/`toggle`).

### D-42 · 2026-10-19 · Walk graph: gates, blocking and tie-breaks
**Decision.** (#81.) `src/popup/core/graph.ts`: (1) **A gate blocks the node attached to it.** GDD § 2.2.3 says a closed gate makes a node untraversable "as data conditions" without naming the field; a node with `attach: { part: <gate part> }` (the doorway, same position in both states) is blocked while that part is `closed`. No new field in the § 3.2 contract; pages may still put `when` on edges instead. (2) A creature blocks the node of its current position unless `blocks: false`; `offpage` and unknown nodes block nothing. Blocking applies to Baokaka only (Mocha's limits are perch-level, #83), and the start node never counts as blocked. (3) **"Ties broken by edge order"** = among shortest paths, the one whose sequence of edge indices from the start is lexicographically smallest; a BFS that expands each node's edges in data order (either direction keeps the edge's rank) gives exactly that. (4) `nearestReachableTo` compares squared distances (exact in integer page units), then path length, then id by code-unit order (`<`). (5) API: `nodePos`, `openEdges(def, state, walker)`, `blockedNodes`, and `walkGraph(def, state, walker)` → `WalkGraph` with `bfs`, `reachSet` (BFS order + steps) and `nearestReachableTo` (node id or point); a `Walker` is `{ who: 'baby', growth }` or `{ who: 'cat' }` because growth isn't in `SimState` (the page decides it). Edges with an unknown end or kind are ignored (lint reports them).
**Why.** Reusing `attach` keeps the contract unchanged and puts the gate's doorway where the art already needs it; the lexicographic rule is the natural, cheap reading of "edge order" and is what BFS gives for free.
**Consequences.** Page authors model a fare gate as a doorway node attached to the gate. Topology is compiled once per `PageDef` and cached by identity, so defs must be treated as immutable (PageLab's editor must create a new def per edit). #82 uses `reachSet`/`nearestReachableTo`/`bfs`; #83 uses the cat walker; the solver and PageLab overlays reuse `openEdges` and `blockedNodes`. Benchmark: ~340k walk-graph builds + BFS per second on a 40-node page (skipped test in `graph.test.ts`).

### D-43 · 2026-10-19 · Baokaka's decision loop: the readings GDD § 2.2 left open
**Decision.** (#82.) `src/popup/core/baby.ts`, `babyTick(def, state, { growth, reachHigh, prev })` = § 3.3 phases 5 (decision, at most one edge) and 6 (arrival effects): (1) **Mocha is an implicit candidate** with id `'mocha'`, kind `mocha`, at the perch's `reachNode` (the perch node when it has none, so he reaches up), while Mocha is settled (`path` empty) on a known perch without `hidesMocha`. (2) **Attractor position:** `followsPushable` → `node` while `onPerch`, nowhere while `falling`, the part's `fall.fallTo` once `landed`; `heldBy` → the creature's current node (nowhere while `offpage`; the drop is #84's); `nodeBy` → the mapped node, else `node`. (3) **Sort key** = priority (`KIND_PRIORITY`, or `priorityOverride`), reachable first, path length (unreachable candidates all tie here, at ∞), squared straight-line distance from Baokaka's node, id in code-unit order. **Decoys always count as unreachable**, so even on a reachable node he only reaches for one. (4) **Arrival effects happen on the tick of the move** (§ 3.3 phase 6); `reaching` starts the tick after he reaches the closest node ("when he's already there"). Facing follows the move by x (unchanged when level); `reaching` turns him toward the target. (5) Grabbing any shiny consumes it (the goal also sets `done`; the next tick's mode is `done`); eating consumes the snack; `cuddling` and `watching` last while the target stays. (6) `looking` lasts `lookCount` 1 → 2, then `crying` emits `cry` (radius 220, at his position) every tick; when a candidate appears he takes it **silently** (no choose-bark, no 「要！要！」 that tick). (7) **Barks** are `bark` events (`events.ts`, `BARK_TEXT`): the kind's bark when the target changes; 「要！要！」 on beats 0, 4, 8 … of reaching the same target. (8) **`refused`** clears on the first arrival at the new target (not on every tick of staying), and per id when the refused attractor's place (node and its position) or availability (visible, active, not consumed; for Mocha: settled and not hidden) differs between `prev` (the tick's start state) and now — so hiding and revealing both clear it. (9) **reachHigh** (`REACH_HIGH_ENABLED = false`, or `opts.reachHigh`) needs growth ≥ 2: a height-1 attractor counts as reachable from any node joined to it by an edge whose condition holds (any kind), approached at the nearest such node (then id). (10) Two fields join `SimState.baby` (mirrored in GDD § 3.2): `target: Id | null` (the view's bubble and "a new target" both need it) and `reachTicks` (0–3, the bark beat, kept mod 4 so the solver's state space stays finite). `carried` and `linked` babies are left untouched; `saying` is treated as resolved (#84 resolves it first).
**Why.** These are the smallest readings that keep the rules explainable and the sim deterministic: no line of sight, no hidden timers, every number in the state bounded.
**Consequences.** #84's step calls `babyTick` after the saying mini-cascade and passes `prev`; it owns ride detection, `drop`, and the `say no` bookkeeping that adds `baby.target` to `refused`. #85 may drop `reachTicks` (and `target`, which is derived) from its key if they inflate the state count. #86's lint should warn on visible ties (§ 3.6) and check that the `'mocha'` id isn't used by a page attractor. #95's animation map keys off the mode names and `bark` events; #127 (扶著站) flips `REACH_HIGH_ENABLED`.

### D-44 · 2026-10-19 · Mocha: the readings GDD § 2.3 left open
**Decision.** (#83.) `src/popup/core/mocha.ts`: (1) **State.** `SimState.mocha` gains `to?` (the perch he is heading for) and `stare?` (the pushable of the eye-contact beat); mirrored in GDD § 3.2. Settled = `perch` set and `path` empty; travelling = `perch` null and `path` = the rest of the trip with his current node first (so the view always knows where he is). Between a `mocha` input (phase 1) and phase 3 of the same tick, `perch` is still the origin. (2) **Pathing.** Phase 1 (`mochaCommand`) computes a shortest cat path (`jump` + `squeeze`, conditions now, edge-order ties) from his current node and **commits** it: later edge changes don't strand him mid-air (C1). Phase 3 (`mochaTick`) leaves (`catLeave`), moves 2 edges, and arrives when the perch node is reached. A trip can be redirected mid-air (from the node he has reached; no second `catLeave`). Tapping his own perch or his current destination, or tapping while he isn't on the page, does nothing. (3) **Refusals.** A perch on a `glass` node is always unreachable (`mochaNope`); one on a `wet` or `steam` node is unreachable while its `blockedWhen` holds — always, without one — and tapping it emits `mochaPuff` (「不要。」); any other perch with a true `blockedWhen` is unreachable (`mochaNope`). No path, or an id that isn't a perch (C1), is `mochaNope`. Refused perches are also avoided en route (the cat walker's new `avoid` list in `graph.ts`; the start node never counts). (4) **Arrival** = `catArrive`, then `onPart` (a `button` is set `down`), `set`, `fleesFromCat` (creature to `to` when the arrival perch is in `nearPerches`; no event, the view diffs positions), `push`. **C3 beat:** arriving at a `push` perch whose pushable is `onPerch` sets `mocha.stare` and emits `mochaStare`; the next phase 3 sets it `falling` and emits `push`; the one after sets it `landed`, emits `land` and applies `landSets`. Setting off during the stare cancels the push. Nothing in `mocha.ts` ever returns a pushable to `onPerch` (lint rule 7: only a page reaction may). (5) **A press is an event.** Mocha's button press and `set` emit `partEnter` with the new `state` (a new `SimEvent.state` field) **even when the part is already in that state** — that is "press again", and what 1-5's elevator (`partEnter panel down → toggle`) needs. `set` respects `lockedWhen` and `stateWhen` (jammed parts stay put) but not `lockedByCat`; pushables can't be `set`. (6) **Leaving** releases a held button (`up`, `partEnter`) unless other weight holds it (`buttonWeighed`: Baokaka on a node attached to the button, or a landed pushable whose `landSets` pressed it). `lockedByCat` locks are derived (`catLocked`: settled on a perch whose `onPart` is that part), so leaving lifts them with no event. `onPart` on other presets (a lift counterweight) does nothing beyond `catArrive`; pages express it with reactions. (7) **Pet** (`mochaPet`, settled only, not during a stare) repeats press, `set` and push (if the pushable is `onPerch` again); no `catArrive`, no scaring. A stare started by a pet lasts that tick: `mochaTick(def, state, input)` doesn't resolve a stare on a `pet` tick.
