(8) **C5/C9.** `mochaAttractorNode` (used by `baby.ts`): the perch's `reachNode` (else its node) while settled — including during the stare — unless the perch has `hidesMocha` **or its node is tagged `box`**. (9) UI events `mochaNope`, `mochaPuff`, `mochaStare` join `UiEventType`.
**Why.** Every reading keeps the rule visible on screen and the state finite and deterministic: the stare is a real tick the view can play, committed paths never leave Mocha off a perch, and treating a press as an event (not a state change) makes "tap Mocha to press again" work without inventing an up-down flicker.
**Consequences.** #84's step calls `mochaCommand`/`mochaPet` in phase 1, `mochaTick(def, state, input)` in phase 3, cascades the events both produce (arrival events need a mini-cascade after phase 3, as phase 5 has), uses `catLocked` to refuse `set` on a sat-on `lockedByCat` part (Mocha's glare), and uses `buttonWeighed` when Baokaka steps off a button. #86's lint should check that perch nodes are unique per perch, `onPart`/`set`/`push` references resolve (and `push` names a pushable), `nearPerches` names perches, and that a `wet`/`steam` perch without `blockedWhen` is intended. #128 (一起) adds the floor walker and suspends C1/C7/C8.

### D-45 · 2026-10-19 · The tick: the readings GDD § 3.3 and § 2.4 left open
**Decision.** (#84.) `src/popup/core/step.ts` (`step`, `cascade`, `matchPredicate`), `words.ts`, `creatures.ts`, `replay.ts` and the full `events.ts`: (1) **Cascade.** FIFO; at most `MAX_CASCADE` = 32 § 3.2 events per cascade (UI events pass through uncounted), else `CascadeOverflowError` naming the page and the parts that entered a state more than once (else every part that entered one). Each event fires **at most one reaction per part, then per creature** (data order): the first whose `on`, radius and `when` match, with every `when` read on the state **before** that event's transitions — so two guarded reactions toggle instead of undoing each other. `to: 'next' | 'prev'` wrap (every reaction stays reversible), `toggle` is the tap rule; `lockedWhen` and `stateWhen` stop reactions too; `setFlag` sets whenever its reaction fires. **Only a real change queues `partEnter`** (Mocha's presses always do, D-44); a `partEnter` queues the part's `emit` events from the part's origin with the event type's default radius. (2) **Radii** (`EVENT_RADIUS`: beep 120, wave/wow 160, whoosh 140, sky whole page, cry 220; a reaction's `within` replaces it) are measured from the event origin (its `at`, else its subject: a perch's node, a part, a creature, a node) to **a part's hit-area centre** or **a creature's node**; an off-page creature hears only whole-page events. 咻——'s cone is |dy| ≤ dx in his facing (exact, edges inside). (3) **`ReactionOn`** (mirrored in GDD § 3.2): a reaction's `on` may narrow by `id` (the event's subject) and `state` (for `partEnter`), which pages like 1-5 (`partEnter panel down`) need; creature reactions take the same form. (4) **Phase order.** Phase 1's events cascade in phase 2; Mocha's (`mochaTick`) right after phase 3; Baokaka's (`cry`, `babyEnter`, `grab`, his weight on a button) right after phase 6 — so a cry opens the door at the end of its own tick and he walks through on the next; `ride` cascades last. **Creature moves and drops happen inside the cascades** (no event; the view diffs positions): phase 4 has nothing left until `lonely` (#128). (5) **Input.** `set` applies only to player-driven parts (`input` ≠ `none`; never pushables); locked (`lockedWhen`, or `catLocked`) → a `locked` UI event; it walks stop by stop in state order and stops before the first stop whose `stateWhen` fails (the physical jam; "furthest allowed stop") with a `jam` UI event carrying that stop. `tap` uses the preset tap rule with the same locks; a disallowed target jams in place. `floor` is ignored until #128. (6) **Words.** A say tick puts him in `saying` for that tick only: he doesn't move or decide; the word's event cascades in phase 5 from his node; 不要！ adds `baby.target` to `refused` (after dropping refusals whose attractor changed this tick) and he re-targets next tick. A page's `words` restricts what he can say, plus its `learns` word once learned. 嗶！/哇！/掰掰 act only through page reactions; built in: 飛機！ toggles `winged` creatures without a `sky` reaction between `initial` and `sky` (else `away`), 咻—— advances `blowable` parts without a `whoosh` reaction (wrapping). 抱抱 and 一起 only emit their event (#120, #128). (7) **Drop.** A creature reaction with `drop` first lays the attractor it holds (`heldBy`, and only then) on its current node: `SimState.dropped?: Record<attractor, node>` (absent until something drops; mirrored in GDD § 3.2), which `attractorNode` reads first; it stays there for good. (8) **Weight.** Entering a node attached to a `button` presses it (always a `partEnter`, like Mocha's press); leaving releases it unless Mocha sits on it or a landed pushable holds it. (9) **`ride`** = he ends the tick on a node attached to part P and **his node moved with P while he was on it** (before his move if he started on P, or after it): a doorway or pressure plate whose node stays put is no ride; crawling onto the train after it moved, or off it, is none either. (10) **Counters** exist only for the page's predicates (mischief + `lines.onEvent`), keyed by `predicateKey` (`type`, `:id`, `?` + the stable JSON of `when`), counting the tick's matching events while `when` holds at the end of the tick, **capped at the predicate's count** so the solver's state space stays finite. `matchPredicate(events, pred, counters)` reads the counter (a level, as the solver needs), falling back to the events for untracked predicates without `when`. (11) **`learns`** fires once (`counters['!learned'] = 1`, a `learn` UI event with `word`/`growth`) on the first tick its event occurs or its condition holds at the end of the tick; a learned growth stage raises `stepGrowth` from then on. (12) A `done` state's ticks only advance `tick` (mode `done`), ignoring input. (13) `replay(def, steps)`: gestures are one tick each; `wait: n` n empty ticks; `settle` until a tick changes nothing but `tick` and `baby.reachTicks` (≤ 200 ticks, else throws); `goal` until `done` (≤ 500, else throws).
**Why.** Each reading is the smallest one that keeps the cascade predictable on paper (one reaction per thing per event, conditions on the "before" state), the state finite (capped counters, `dropped` only on drop), and the screen honest (no move on a say tick, rides only when he visibly moves).
**Consequences.** #85's solver steps with `step`, offers `say` only for `wordAvailable` words, and can read mischief reachability from `counters` via `matchPredicate`; it may drop `tick` from its key. #86's lint should check reaction targets (`to` names a state, or a position for creatures), that `drop` names an attractor held by that creature, that `emit`/reaction loops can't exceed 32 (§ 3.6 rule 12, fuzzed by the solver), and that a `winged` creature has a sky position. #89's view reads `jam`/`locked` for the wobble and glare, `ride` for the giggle, `learn` for the learn beat, and `saying` for the speech bubble. #120 implements 抱抱 (carrier, `carried`, the 1-edge reach) and #128 一起 (`linked`, `floor`, `lonely` in phase 4); both have skipped tests in `words.test.ts`.

### D-46 · 2026-10-19 · The solver: the readings GDD § 3.7 left open
**Decision.** (#85.) `src/popup/core/solver.ts`, `solve(def, from = initialState(def), { cap, timeMs, clock, stopAtGoal })`: (1) **Key.** `solverKey(def, state)` is a compact string holding every `SimState` field except `tick` (it only grows) and `baby.reachTicks` (the bark beat, D-43: it changes nothing but itself and `bark` UI events, so states that differ only there have the same futures — a test checks this on every reachable c1p2 state). Sets and records are sorted, part/pushable/creature states follow data order, fields are joined with control characters (ids never contain them). A type-level check fails to compile when `SimState` gains a field the key doesn't read; a test checks it splits states exactly as `canonicalKey` does. About a fifth of `canonicalKey`'s length and several times faster (key building was 60 % of the time). `baby.target` stays in the key (it decides refusal clearing and the bark). (2) **Inputs** (`candidateInputs`, in this order): `none`; for each unlocked part with a drag input (`drag-linear`, `drag-rotary`), a `set` to every other stop; for each unlocked part with a tap rule, a `tap` — **including tabs, sliders and wheels**, whose tap cycles (last → first) and can reach states a drag can't in one move; `mocha` to every perch with a cat path (not his own or his current destination); `pet` while he's settled and not staring; `say` for every word `wordAvailable` allows (not while carried or linked); `floor` to every height-0 node in linked mode. Tap-only parts (flap, prop, tappable light, tap crank) get no `set`: the player can only tap them. (3) **Pruning.** An input whose successor equals the `none` successor is dropped, as are self-loops and inputs repeating an earlier input's successor (the first input in candidate order is kept). Done states are terminal. (4) **Par** = fewest non-wait inputs, by a 0-1 BFS (a FIFO per move count; wait edges stay in the layer). `trivial` = par 0. `solution` = the parent-pointer path to the first done state settled (waits as one `none` per tick; `toSolutionSteps` folds them into `{ wait: n }`). (5) **Dead ends** = explored states with no path to a done state (reverse BFS over the stored edges). `deadEndCount` counts them; `deadEnds` lists the first 20 by (moves, discovery) — so the first entries are the moments of no return — each with its input path from the start and the state rebuilt by replaying it. On an incomplete search (cap, time, `stopAtGoal`, error) `deadEnds` is empty and `deadEndCount` null (unknown). (6) **Mischief / 土地公** reachable = some explored state that can still reach a done state (a done state counts) fires the mischief (`matchPredicate([], when, counters)`) / satisfies `visibleWhen` (absent: always). (7) **`nextAction(state)`** = the first input on a path with the fewest moves, **then the fewest ticks** (a Dijkstra over reversed edges, computed on first use), first in candidate order on ties; `none` means wait. The tick tie-break guarantees that following it reaches `done` even where waiting loops (he reaches forever). A state the search didn't expand — or, after an incomplete search, one with no known way to the goal — gets a fresh `solve` from it with the same options plus `stopAtGoal`. (8) **Errors.** A `CascadeOverflowError` from `step` ends the search with `stop: 'error'` and `error: { kind: 'cascadeOverflow', message, path }` (lint rule 12); any other error propagates. (9) **Clock.** The core may not read the wall clock (boundary test), so `timeMs` and `ms` need an injected `clock` (the hint worker passes `() => performance.now()`); without one, `ms` is null and `timeMs` is ignored. The clock is read every 256 expansions. (10) **Memory.** `Map<string, number>` from key to vertex, everything else in growable `Int32Array`s (moves, parent, parent input, edge ranges, edge targets and input ids into a small table of distinct inputs) plus a `Uint8Array` of flags; states are kept only while queued. Measured on a stress page (c1p2 plus four 3-state props): 268,143 states, 2.65 M edges, 39 s, 392 MB peak RSS including ≈ 150 MB of Node/vite-node baseline, ≈ 0.9 KB per state at peak; 2 M states would need ≈ 2 GB, inside a CI runner. (11) Words: the toy pages set `words: []`, because with 不要！ Baokaka can refuse the only bead and cry forever — a real soft-lock the solver reports (GDD § 2.4: "if nothing else is a candidate, he goes looking → crying").
**Why.** These are the cheapest readings that keep the answers exact: dropping only fields with no effect keeps the graph a true quotient; offering every player gesture (taps on tabs too) makes "from every reachable state" honest; the lexicographic (moves, ticks) cost is what makes `nextAction` safe to follow blindly, which hints need. Throughput is ~7,000 states/s (≈ 10 `step` calls per state), dominated by `step` itself.
**Consequences.** #87's `pages.test.ts` asserts `solvable && deadEndCount === 0 && !trivial && mischiefReachable && tudigongReachable` per page and prints `deadEnds[0].path` via `toSolutionSteps` on failure. #88's CLI reports par, `statesExplored`, `ms` (pass a clock), dead ends and the reachability flags. #100's worker calls `solve(def, current, { cap: 200_000, timeMs: 300, clock, stopAtGoal: true })` and uses `nextAction(current)`; on `stop: 'time'` without a solution it falls back as GDD § 2.7 says. The CI budget (65 pages < 60 s) means ≈ 6,000 states per page on average; pages far above that should be simplified, or #137 (solver tuning) should speed up `step` (≈ 10 µs per call today). Pages that offer 不要！ (chapter 3 on, #116) must give him another candidate or a way out of crying, or the solver will flag a dead end.


### D-47 · 2026-10-19 · The page lint: the readings GDD § 3.6 left open
**Decision.** (#86.) `src/popup/core/lint.ts`, `lintPage(def, script?) → { errors, warnings }`, each issue `{ code, severity, page, ids, message }` with a **stable code** `Lnn-name` whose number is the § 3.6 rule (table in the file header). (1) **Rule 1** also covers: enum values (`L01-invalid-value`: preset, input, edge kind, event type, word, malformed `Cond`), the wrong kind of target (`L01-wrong-kind`: `push`/`landed`/`followsPushable` not a pushable, a light's `attractor` not a `light`, a creature `drop`ping an attractor whose `heldBy` isn't that creature), `state` filters without `partEnter` (`L01-invalid-filter`), two perches on one node (`L01-shared-perch-node`), an attractor called `'mocha'` (`L01-reserved-id`, D-43), a `winged` creature with no `sky`/`away` position (`L01-winged-no-sky`), a `flag` condition no reaction sets, solution steps and event-predicate ids that name nothing, and missing line keys (only when a `script` is passed). (2) **Rule 2** = every named part state *and creature position* exists, wherever it's named (`Cond`, `stateWhen` keys, `attach.pos` keys, `nodeBy.map`, part reaction `to` other than `next`/`prev`/`toggle`, perch `set`, `landSets`, `emit.onEnter`, `on.state`, solution `set`, creature reaction `to`, `fleesFromCat.to`). (3) **Rule 3** checks the **compiled** states (preset defaults), so defaulted flaps/gates/carriers count; it adds `L03-missing-states` (instead of `compilePart`'s throw), `L03-invalid-input` (`PRESETS[preset].inputs`), `L03-bad-states` (duplicates; pushables exactly `onPerch`/`falling`/`landed`; tab/slider 2–4 and wheel/section 2–8 stops per GDD § 2.1), `L03-preset-field` (a pushable without `fall`, a carrier without `route`), and a creature's `initial` must be a position. (4) **Rule 4** adds `L04-gate-node-moves`: a gate's doorway node has one position (D-42). (5) **Rule 5** ("no vanishing ground"): since nodes structurally always exist, a node "vanishes" when it leaves the page — any node that an edge leads to must stay inside 390 × 560 in every state of its part — or rides a pushable, which falls away. (6) **Rule 6**: with rule 4 every part state is a position, so what remains is parts whose states aren't places: a node Baokaka can stand on (his start, or an end of a crawl/climb/cruise/walk edge) may not be attached to a pushable or a carrier. (7) **Rule 7** static half: every pushable is pushed by some perch (`L07-pushable-unpushed`) and a reaction may only put it back `onPerch` (`L07-pushable-reaction`); `solutionPushables(def)` lists the pushables a solution knocks off, and #87's solver test (no dead ends for every page) is the dead-end half. (8) **Rule 9** applies to player-interactive parts (compiled `input` not `none`, the same definition as rule 8's budget); a circle needs r ≥ 24; overlap is the shared area as a share of the *smaller* area (rectangles exact, circles by quarter-unit column integration). Non-interactive hit areas (gates, pushables) are event origins, not touch targets, so they may be any size or overlap. (9) **Rule 10** counts code points; the 淘氣 text is a line too (≤ 30). (10) **Rule 11** adds `L11-bad-goal`: the goal must be a non-decoy `shiny`, the only kind a grab completes. (11) **Rule 12** static half: a part-to-part trigger graph (a part's `partEnter` for any of its states and its `emit` events, reaching another part's reaction by radius between the fixed hit centres); a cycle in which every trigger is certain to change the next part (its first matching reaction, no `when`, no state filter, a relative `to`, ≥ 2 states, no `lockedWhen`/`stateWhen`) always overflows → `L12-cascade-loop` error; any other cycle → `L12-cascade-cycle` **warning**, since guards may stop it and the solver fuzz (#87) decides. (12) **Rule 13**: the § 3.2 contract has no custom-reaction field yet, so the lint warns on **any function value** anywhere in the page data, whatever shape the escape hatch takes. (13) New warnings, added to GDD § 3.6 as rules 14–16: `L14-visible-tie` (in the initial state, at the page's growth, the first two ranked candidates tie on priority, reachability and path length — the tie the player would see), `L15-lock-order` (a part's `lockedWhen`/`stateWhen` reads an earlier part that reacts to the same event type, D-45's mid-event reading), `L16-always-wet-perch` (C7, D-44). (14) **Total and fast**: the lint never throws on malformed data (state-dependent checks are skipped when presets or states are broken) and runs in ≈ 0.1 ms on c1p2.
**Why.** Each reading is the narrowest static check that the GDD's wording supports and that the engine's actual behaviour (D-41–D-46) makes meaningful; anything that depends on the reachable states (rule 7's dead ends, rule 12's real overflow) stays with the solver, so the lint has no false errors.
**Consequences.** #87's `pages.test.ts` asserts `lintPage(def, SCRIPT).errors` is empty for every page (printing the messages) and runs the solver for rules 7 and 12; #91's PageLab calls `lintPage` on every edit and shows codes and ids; `content/script.ts` (#97/#87) passes its table as `script`. New page fields must get lint checks (handbook § 3).

### D-48 · 2026-10-19 · The page registry and the first pages: the readings #87 left open
**Decision.** (#87.) (1) **Registry.** `src/popup/pages/index.ts` holds the authored pages in play order (`AUTHORED`) and exports `PAGES`, `pageById(id)`, `nextPage(id)` (the next page across chapters; null after the last or for an unknown id) and `chapterOf(id)` (the chapter number, 0 = prologue), all from `buildRegistry(authored)`. It throws on a duplicate id or a page not strictly later by (chapter, index) than the one before; `index.test.ts` also checks every id is `c{chapter}p{index}`. (2) **Defaults** (GDD § 3.2): the registry returns copies with `words` = the words learned (`learns.word`) on earlier pages, in learning order, without repeats, and `baby.growth` = the highest `learns.growth` on earlier pages, else 0. "Learned by this page" excludes the page's own `learns`, which `step` applies while it is played (D-45). Explicit `words`/`growth` win and don't feed later defaults. The authored objects are not mutated; code that needs the defaults must read pages from the registry, not import the page files. (3) **`pages.test.ts`** asserts per page: no lint errors (`lintPage(def, SCRIPT)`; warnings printed), the solution replays to `done`, and a **complete** solve (`stop === null`: no cap, no cascade overflow) with `solvable`, `deadEndCount === 0`, not `trivial` unless `allowTrivial`, 淘氣 and 土地公 reachable; par outside the § 3.7 band only warns. Plus one budget check: the summed solver time of all pages < 60 s. Every failure message carries a table (page, difficulty, par, states, dead ends, ms, lint counts, error/stop). (4) **`content/script.ts`** exists now with an empty `SCRIPT: LineTable`; pages carry no `lines` until #102. (5) **Page 1-2** is the § 3.4 data plus the `board` prop with the § 4.3 states `times`/`flip` (the engine fixture `core/fixtures/c1p2.ts` keeps `still`/`flip`; a test checks the page equals the fixture apart from the board). (6) **Page 0-1** layout: mattress nodes `crib1`–`crib2`, a `pillow` slope node joined to `crib2` only while `crib-rail` is `down`, rug nodes `rug1`–`rug2` (the bead); the rail is a vertical 2-stop tab pulled down along the bottom edge (track y 480 → 540); the mobile is a circular `prop` hit (r 40); no Mocha (`mocha.start: null`, no perches); the 土地公 is always visible (no `visibleWhen`) with an empty `hat` (the prologue has none). Solver: par 1, 22 states; 1-2 (with `words: []` from the registry): par 2, 182 states, ≈ 30 ms.
**Why.** Computing the defaults once in the registry keeps the page files to what the designer decides and keeps the core unaware of page order; a complete search is what makes "no dead ends" a proof rather than "none found".
**Consequences.** Content issues (#106, #107 onward) add a file under `pages/cN/` and one line in `AUTHORED`, and never touch the tests. #88's CLI and #91's PageLab read `PAGES`; #93's save uses `nextPage` for `furthest`. A page that legitimately needs a capped or partial search would need a new per-page option (none exists yet).

### D-49 · 2026-10-19 · `npm run solve`: the readings #88 left open
**Decision.** (#88.) (1) The report logic is `src/popup/pages/report.ts` (`reportPage`, `judge`, `formatTable`, `formatDetails`, `formatSummary`, `reportFailed`, `parseArgs`, `selectPages`, `PAR_BAND`, `CI_BUDGET_MS`), not `src/popup/dev/` (whose rule is "import nothing from here outside PageLab's lazy import") and not `scripts/` (outside `tsc -b` and vitest's `src/**` include). `scripts/solve-pages.ts` is a thin CLI over it, run by `vite-node`; `pages.test.ts` reuses `PAR_BAND`, `CI_BUDGET_MS` and the table, so the test and the CLI judge a page identically. (2) **Failures** = lint errors, the authored solution not reaching `done` (the CLI replays it too), the state cap or any other early stop, a cascade overflow, unsolvable (complete search), dead ends, trivial without `allowTrivial`, and 淘氣/土地公 unreachable (only judged on a complete search: on a capped one `false` means "not found" and the cap already fails the page). **Warnings** = lint warnings and par outside the band. (3) **Fail fast**: a page over its cap ends the run at once (exit 1) with its row and a line naming it; the pages after it are not solved. (4) **Budget**: the total is the wall time of the whole run (lint + replay + solve, all selected pages); at or over 60 s the run fails, also for a `--page`/`--chapter` subset (a subset over budget means the full run is too). (5) Exit codes 0 ok / 1 failure / 2 usage (bad argument, or a filter matching no page). (6) `--cap N` overrides the solver's 2,000,000 default for exploring a page; there is still no per-page cap field (D-48). (7) The table pads by terminal display width (CJK titles count 2 columns).
**Why.** One judgement shared by CI and the designer's command means a green `npm run solve` and a green `pages.test.ts` always agree; failing fast on the cap is what the issue asks and keeps a runaway page from eating the budget for the rest.
**Consequences.** #91's PageLab solver panel can call `reportPage` for its badges (ok / dead-end / over cap). New page-level checks go into `judge` so both the CLI and the test see them.

### D-50 · 2026-10-19 · The page renderer: the readings #89 left open
**Decision.** (#89.) `src/popup/view/` (`PageView`, `PagePlayer`, `geometry`, `interp`, `stage`, `layers`, `parts/`, `actors/`, `art/placeholders`, `art/registry`) and `src/popup/game/session.ts`:
1. **Book geometry.**
   - The 390 × 560 page model is drawn from y 0 to the floor page's front edge at **y 650**, plus 14 units of page-block edges below it. The pull tabs ride the band between 560 and 650 (proof A; 1-2's track is at y 610).
   - The gutter is at **y 360**.
   - Everything is scaled by one factor: `min((vw − 36) / 390, (vh − 96 − 120 − 12) / 664, 1.25)`. That is 0.9077 at 390 × 844 and 0.66 at 375 × 667.
   - Leftover height is split above and below the book (cover, never page).
2. **Layers.** GDD § 3.9 draws the floor before every part. The bible's z table puts the train (z3) behind the platforms (z4) it slides past.
   - So parts are split around the floor by `PRESET_Z`: carriages, `section`, `lift` and `loop` sit behind the ledges, every other preset in front.
   - The order is: backdrop (z0–z2) · cut-outs · parts-mid (z3) · floor (z4 ledges) · parts-near · creatures · actors · fx · foreground (z5) · hit.
   - Attractors and perch paw marks draw with the near parts.
3. **Placeholder stage from the walk graph.** Pages have no ledge art until #96 bakes it, so the view draws paper only where Baokaka can go:
   - **Ledges.** A ledge for each level run of static baby nodes joined by unconditional edges, padded 24 units and snapped to the page side within 70. A single node becomes a cushion (indoors) or a crate.
   - **Ramps.** A ramp for each unconditional edge between levels.
   - **Rails.** A rail for each conditional static crossing, standing 50 units past the guarded node, clear of his head; its ledge runs on to it. The rail folds flat (`scaleY`) as the condition opens, and its ramp fades in.
   - **Perch furniture.** A pillar for a height-2 perch, a bench for a seat, standing on the ledge below.
   - **Carriages.** A carriage under the nodes attached to a non-lift, non-carrier part with one level of travel. Its pit shows only in the stretch of travel no ledge covers.
   - **Themes.** The prologue and page E get the home room; chapters 1–10 get the city night with a generic 重簷 hall as the landmark (folded with `vFold`).
4. **Motion.**
   - **Actors** lerp ease-in-out over the tick with a 2 px hop (Mocha 24 px, a jump). A creature going off the page leaves past the nearer side.
   - **Parts.** Part tweens use an ease-out-back tuned so the peak is exactly 1.06 (c ≈ 1.29); the "2 px settle" is that curve's return. The 60 ms 0.97 squash follows, about the part's centre. Transforms only.
   - **Rides.** An attached node is drawn at its part's tween; an actor stepping onto it lerps to that moving point.
   - **Reduced motion:** instant swaps.
5. **Animated-node budget.** It is counted strictly: every element inside a `data-anim` group, not just the groups.
   - To fit, `depth()` puts a single-element outline's copy attributes on the element itself, and stand-ins draw one path per colour.
   - The worst frame of 1-2 is ≤ 120 nodes in 8–9 moving groups. The render test holds every page to ≤ 150.
6. **Session.**
   - **Pause** is by named reason. `elapsed()` stands still while paused, so the view freezes mid-step.
   - **Resume** runs only after a 150 ms grace with no reason left; a new pause cancels the grace.
   - **Fast-forward:** while Baokaka is `moving` with more than 4 edges left on his BFS path and nothing is queued, a tick is a quarter long. A queued input ends it at once.
   - **Done:** at `done` the clock stops scheduling ticks but keeps time, so the last frame plays out.
7. **Registry.** Unknown part keys fall back to the preset's kit art, unknown backdrop keys to `placeholderBackdrop`, unknown creature keys to `origamiBird`. Each missing key is `console.info`-logged once per kind in dev builds.
8. **Dev parameters** on `#/popup?page=<id>`: `tick=N`, `paused=1`, `speed=` and `demo=1` (three idle ticks, then the authored solution through the session). Production will open the save's page (#93).

**Why.** Reading the scenery off the walk graph means every page is legible the moment its data exists, and the drawing can never disagree with where he can go. Splitting parts by z is the only order in which a train can slide behind a platform. Counting the budget strictly leaves headroom for the real bitmaps, which replace whole subtrees with one `<image>`.

**Consequences.**
- #90 adds gestures on the hit layer and calls `session.pause('finger')` / `resume('finger')`.
- #94/#95 replace the stand-ins inside `actors/` and add hop lean, landing wobble and touch-watch.
- #96 registers baked backdrops and parts in `art/registry.ts` (the walk-graph ledges then come from the bake).
- #97 replaces the HUD and bar placeholders in `PagePlayer`.
- #98 animates the bubble's change of mind.
- #136 decides tilt (`perspective` already sits on the page container only).
- On 375 × 667 the book is 0.66 scale and 1-2's tab hit area falls to ≈ 37 px (R6). #90's tap magnet and #97's layout must answer that.

### D-51 · 2026-10-19 · Gestures: the readings #90 left open, and R6 on short phones
**Decision.** (#90.) `src/popup/view/gestures.ts` (pure) and `useGestures.ts` (Pointer Events plumbing in `PagePlayer`):
1. **R6 on short phones (the #89 note).** At 375 × 667 the book is 0.66 scale and 1-2's train tab hit (56 units) is ≈ 37 px. Every touchable's hit shape is **grown about its centre to 48 × 48 screen px after the book scale** (`minHit`), on top of the 32 px magnet. The page data keeps its page-unit hits (lint still asks ≥ 48 units); the growth is a view rule, so it holds at any viewport. A test checks every touchable on every page, every frame of its solution, at 390 × 844 and 375 × 667. #97's compact HUD may still let the book grow on short screens; it is no longer needed for R6.
2. **Hit-test order.** The topmost target whose own shape contains the point; else the grown shapes that contain it (nearest own shape; ties to the higher layer); else the magnet: the nearest grown shape within 32 screen px (ties to the higher layer).
3. **Targets.** Player-driven parts (input ≠ `none`) by their `hit`; perch paw prints (r 24, while Mocha is on the page, not his own perch); Mocha (56 × 70 above his feet) and Baokaka (96 × 66) on the actors' layers; the 土地公 (r 22) while `visibleWhen` holds. The 土地公 is drawn far back but sits **above the parts** for touch, because he is a small find hidden behind parts (1-2's board).
4. **Taps.** Under 8 px of travel. A tap-only target (prop, perch, Mocha, Baokaka) also takes a wobbly tap if the finger lifts within the magnet of it. Tab → `tap` (advance), wheel → `tap` (clockwise), flap/prop/light → `tap`, perch → `mocha`, Mocha → `pet`. **Baokaka and the 土地公 are game events, not sim inputs**: `wordRing` (the stub dims the page and circles him, `pause('ring')`; any touch closes it; #99) and `tudigong` (#101 awards the sticker). The 250 ms debounce is per target.
5. **Drags.** The magnet also picks the part a drag starts on. Linear drags are relative to where the finger landed, projected on the track, scaled to stops; over-drag eases toward 0.35 stop past the ends; release snaps to the nearest stop and queues `set`. Rotary drags accumulate angle (clockwise on screen = the listed state order) and queue `set` to the wrapped stop (the core walks to it stop by stop). A flap drag lifts by distance / reach and toggles (queues `tap`) past half. Riders (the train's nodes, Baokaka on it) follow the preview.
6. **Commit and settle.** The input applies at the next tick boundary like any other. Until that tick the part is held at its snapped stop, then glides (the part tween's curve) to where the state really landed, so a jam at `half` reads as a glide back, never a jump.
7. **Locked parts** answer in the view at once (the world is paused under the finger): `locked` flag with a wobble, Mocha's glare, a `nope` event; nothing is queued, so the core's `locked` event is not needed for this path.
8. **Touch pauses the world.** The whole screen takes pointers; any finger down pauses (`pause('finger')`), the last up resumes after the session's 150 ms grace. Only the first finger drives a gesture; more than 3 points at once cancels it. Baokaka tilts 3° toward the finger (`data-watch`).
9. **Hold-to-reveal.** 600 ms still (under 8 px) on empty space: every touchable's own hit shape gets a 2 px dashed chalk outline until release.
10. **State flags** for #140: `data-dragging`, `data-locked`, `data-glare`, `data-watch`, `data-reveal`; the page SVG also carries `data-parts` and `data-done` for tools.

**Why.** Growing hits after the scale answers R6 everywhere with no layout dependency and no data churn, and the hit layer draws the same grown shapes the hit test uses. Answering locks in the view keeps feedback immediate while the world is paused, and keeps the one-input-per-tick queue for real moves.

**Consequences.**
- #140 draws the Dragging/Locked/Hold-to-reveal looks on the flags; #95 the curious head and glare frames.
- #99 replaces the ring stub; #101 listens for `tudigong`; #105 maps `tap`/`snap`/`nope` to sounds.
- #92 folds `npm run check:play` into visual QA.
- The 1-2 土地公 is drawn behind the board's art, so when the board flips he is touchable but not yet visible (noted on #101).

### D-52 · 2026-10-19 · PageLab: the readings #91 left open
**Decision.** (#91, GDD § 3.10.)
1. **Its own stepper, not `PageSession`.** The lab must load any state (a dead end, a solve's start), step exactly one tick, and keep a log, so `dev/labModel.ts` steps `core/step` directly (`labLoad`, `labStep`) and keeps the last 20 ticks' inputs and events. A step that throws (a reaction loop) leaves the state and shows the error. Ticks animate like the game's (`PageView` given the time since the tick). The palette is `candidateInputs` without the wait: exactly what the solver tries, so "every legal input" means the same thing in both.
2. **The solver worker is PageLab's own** (`dev/solver.worker.ts` over `dev/solverJob.ts`), not #100's hint worker, which doesn't exist yet; PageLab may switch later. On load it posts the report of every lab page (`pages/report.ts`'s `reportPage`, the same judge as `pages.test.ts` and `npm run solve`) for the badges; a solve posts `SolveResult` without `nextAction` (structured clone). An edited page is sent as data; a newer request supersedes an older one's answer.
3. **Badges:** the worst problem wins — `over cap`, then `dead-end`, `lint`, any other failure (`fail`), else `ok`; `pending` until the report arrives. The badge judges the registered page; the lint panel judges the edited copy, live.
4. **Lab fixtures.** The page list ends with pages broken on purpose (`dev/labPages.ts`: `c1p2-broken`, 1-2 whose train locks for good if Mocha scares the pigeon while it is still `out`), so the issue's manual test — load a dead end from a broken 1-2 — runs on real data. They never enter the registry or the game.
5. **Overlays.** Six checkboxes: `nodes`, `edges` (Baokaka's kinds), `perches` (with the cat edges `jump`/`squeeze`, and a dotted line to each `reachNode`), `attractors` (rank in § 2.2.2 order and priority; faded when he can't want it now), `hits` (the data shape and the grown 48 px shape, D-51) and `target` (line, path preview to the nearest reachable node, Mocha's trip). Edit mode always shows the nodes. Diagram colours are a dev-only `PAGELAB` group in `palette.ts` (the "no raw hex in src/popup" rule holds).
6. **Edit mode** moves positions only (GDD: structural edits stay in code): a free node's `x`/`y`, a riding node's `attach.pos` for its part's **current** state, and either end of a part's `track`; values round to whole units. 「Copy TS」 writes the `nodes` block and one `track:` line per part (under a `// part '…'` comment) in the page files' style; the page files' inline comments aren't kept.
7. **URL.** `page`, `overlay` (absent = `nodes,edges,target`; empty = none), `tick` (0–500 idle ticks, like `#/popup`), `speed`, `edit=1`, `view=grey,squint`, `section=kit`. Changes rewrite the hash with `replaceState` (no history entry per checkbox); a new `page` or `tick` reloads the page and drops edits.
8. **Readability (bible § 4.2).** Greyscale is a CSS filter on the page only; squint is a second `PageView` at 0.25 scale; the count is touchable parts plus perches in the current state (`targetsOf`), flagged over 3 on pages 1–3 of a chapter. The art-key report calls a key "baked" when the registry has its own art for it (`isPlaceholder`, `hasBackdropArt`, `hasCreatureArt`), else "placeholder"; everything is a placeholder in M0. #140's affordance looks will show up in these views as they land.

**Why.** Designers live in this tool (GDD § 6 risk 2): one link reproduces a view, the solver never freezes the page, and the dead-end tools can be exercised before a real page has a dead end.

**Consequences.**
- #92 adds `fixture=` targets to the lab for visual QA (the kit target's header moved, baseline updated).
- #100 may reuse `solverJob.ts` or replace the worker with `hint.worker.ts`.
- New page fields that move (e.g. a rotary `center`) need `labModel` edit operations and a `copyTs` line.

### D-53 · 2026-10-19 · Visual QA for 翻翻台北: the readings #92 left open

**Context.** #92 asks for PageLab fixtures that the screenshot check picks up, `still=1` stills, and `ui-check.mjs` rules for GDD § 1.3 R5/R6, reduced motion and flashing. Several details were open.

**Decision.**
1. **Fixtures** (`src/popup/dev/fixtures.ts`, `POPUP_FIXTURES`) are a page id plus a list in the solution-step language (inputs and `wait`s), expanded by `replay` and stepped through `core/step` from the page's initial state. Each one pins what it is meant to show (`expect: { mode?, event? }`). `fixtures.test.ts` fails when the engine or a page edit moves the state away from it; the fix is to change the steps, not the expectation. Fixture names are screenshot file names, so they must be unique among popup and art lab fixtures.
2. **The view.** `#/pagelab?fixture=<name>` renders `PagePlayer` full screen with no lab chrome. It opens on the fixture's last tick: `PageSession` and `PagePlayer` take a `prev` and play that tick's tween. With **`still=1`** the clock is held, the frame is drawn at its tick's end (`prev` is dropped), and `[data-still]` turns off every CSS animation and transition. So **idle loops must be CSS animations**, never rAF or `element.animate()`, or a still will not stop them. `#/pagelab?section=fixtures` is the index the scripts read: `[data-popup-fixture]` for the fixtures and `[data-popup-page-id]` for the registered pages.
3. **Targets.** Each fixture is a visual target named after itself, at 390×844 and 375×667, dev only (like the art lab). The `popup-c0p1` and `popup-c1p2` targets stay, because they cover the `#/popup` route.
4. **R5 measurement.** Every visible text node's element is checked:
   - **Size:** SVG text is its computed font size × the screen CTM's scale. The minimum is 20 px, or 24 px inside `[data-text-size="big"]`, which #104 must set for 大字.
   - **Contrast:** the text colour comes from computed styles (`color`, or `fill` for SVG, alpha and opacity included). The background is read off a screenshot taken with all text hidden. The run passes when 90 % of the pixels in its box are ≥ 7:1, which tolerates anti-aliasing and paper fibres. A stroke painted under the fill counts as the run's background. A gradient fill fails as "cannot measure".
5. **Lettering inside pictures** is marked `data-art-text` in the art and skipped (each run counts as a skip). Examples: the station name plate, the split-flap digit, a verb seal's glyph. The game never asks anyone to read it (R5: "completable without reading"). Seals are icons, and the arrow and track carry the verb too. Never mark UI text this way.
6. **Waivers** (`WAIVERS` in `scripts/popup-checks.mjs`) name an issue and are printed on every run:
   - the static kit HUD and bottom bar (labels 12–19 px), until #97;
   - the thought bubble's 「要！」 under 20 px when the book scales down at 375×667, until #98.
7. **R6.** Every `[data-hit]` (the page's grown hit shapes) and every button, link, input, switch, tab or slider must be ≥ 48×48 CSS px. A fixture with no `[data-hit]` fails, because a missing hit layer must not pass.
8. **Motion.** Fixtures run live (not still) for about 1.5 s:
   - Under `reduce`, a running animation that changes anything but opacity fails.
   - With motion on, an animation fails when it flashes more than 3 times a second: a falling crossing of the mid level of opacity, visibility or `rgb()` luminance, with a swing of ≥ 0.1, counted per second of its run. A one-shot fade is not a flash.
   - A self-test first injects a 5 Hz blink, a slide and a slow fade, and fails the run if the detectors misjudge them, so an empty result can't hide a broken check.
   - Actor movement on rAF is the simulation, not decoration, and is not checked.
9. **One command.** `npm run qa` runs the visual, UI and play checks. `check:play` also fails when a registered page has no scenario.

**Why.** The fixture list doubles as the screenshot list and the audience-rule list. Replaying the engine keeps fixtures honest. Waivers keep the check green without hiding gaps from the issues that own them.

**Consequences.**
- #97 removes the HUD/bottom-bar waiver and #98 the bubble waiver.
- #104 sets `data-text-size="big"` for 大字.
- Every later UI issue adds fixtures for its screens.
- #111 drops the art lab half of the name-uniqueness test along with the RPG targets.

### D-54 · 2026-10-19 · The touchable affordance system: the readings #140 left open

**Context.** #140 builds the bible § 4 grandparent rule (marigold + red verb seal + grip + direction cue on every touchable), the § 4.1 states and the § 4.2 checks, with a lint "within ΔE < 12 of marigold". Several details were open.

**Decision.**
1. **ΔE is CIE76** (Lab distance, D65), the unqualified "ΔE" of the bible, measured against the card's **saturated body only: `marigold` (#FFC23A) and `marigoldDeep`**. The light stop `marigoldLight` sits by design next to lamplight (§ 3.3's lit vellum `windowMid`, ΔE 9; `lamp`, ΔE 6) and foil (`gold`, ΔE 10), and the edge `marigoldEdge` is a brown beside kraft and wood; measuring against them would flag the bible's own night palette. Against the body, no palette colour but the two card stops is within 12 (a unit test pins this), while a saturated taxi yellow (#F7B731) is caught.
2. **What the lint reads: drawn markup, not data.** `lintAffordance(def, markup)` walks the SVG `PageView` drew (in tests: rendered to static markup for every state of every page's solution, in both motion looks and with the states drawn; in PageLab: the stage's live DOM). Paint attributes and `style` declarations are resolved through gradient and pattern ids. Allowed marigold: inside a touchable's `data-part` group, and inside `data-affordance` (perch paws, linked brads, hint halos and threads). The four cues are found by markup markers the kit now emits: `data-seal`, `data-grip`, `data-cue`. Kit presets are linted the same way (touchable presets show all four cues; printed presets carry no marigold).
3. **A touchable is `wearsMarigold`:** a preset whose kit art is touchable (tab, slider, wheel, flap; a loop with a crank) with an input other than `none`. Tap toys (`prop`, a tappable `light`) take touches but stay unmarked, and shake on tap.
4. **Hit areas after the book scale** (`A06-hit-small`): the own hit shape of every touch target must be ≥ 48 CSS px at the 390×844 book scale (0.908, so ≥ 52.9 units). `PERCH_R` and `TUDIGONG_R` grow from 24 and 22 to 27. `PageView` marks hit shapes that `minHit` had to grow (`data-hit-grown`), and `check:ui` fails any at 390×844 (growing stays the safety net on short phones, D-51).
5. **Seals on a page** are ≥ 12.5 half-size (25 units, ≥ 22 CSS px after the book scale) and 15.5 in 長輩模式 (28 px); the kit's own sheet defaults are unchanged.
6. **The idle wiggle** is a seeded scheduler (`WiggleScheduler`, pure, fake-clock tested): the first round spread over one period, then each part's next wiggle at the moment in its 3.2–4.8 s window farthest from every other part's. Each wiggle is a one-shot 300 ms CSS animation restarted by switching between two identical keyframe names (no remount, and a still or reduced motion stops it). It pauses while a finger is down and skips locked parts.
7. **Locked** (bible: "darkened 35 % with a paw badge") is drawn whenever the part is locked (`partLocked`), not only on touch: a 35 % indigo veil over the part's outline and the badge at its hit's top-right corner; the touch adds #90's wobble and glare.
8. **Dragging** lifts the moving card: 5 units up, ×1.04, over a dropped shadow (a radial-gradient penumbra plus the card's silhouette at 38 %, offset down-right in screen space), and the part is drawn last in its layer.
9. **Hints** (`affordance.hint`): L1 is a filter-free halo hugging what the finger should touch (a tab's card at its stop, a wheel's rim, a flap's card; else the hit shape), pulsing in opacity three times; L2 adds the vellum ghost hand doing the target's gesture on a 2.4 s loop (drag to the next or hinted stop, turn one step, lift the corner, tap), with the gesture's printed path, and the dotted thread to a linked part. Under reduced motion both hold still. #100 decides what to hint.
10. **Fixture looks.** The screenshot browser runs with reduced motion, so a fixture's still pins its motion look (`look`, default `motion`); live, the OS setting decides.

**Why.** Linting what is drawn catches art from any source (kit, placeholder, a future baked key) with one rule; the markers make the four cues checkable without guessing from shapes.

**Consequences.**
- New art must mark its cues (`data-seal`, `data-grip`, `data-cue`) and any non-touchable marigold (`data-affordance`), or the lint fails the page.
- #100 drives `affordance.hint` (and "Mocha looks at it"); #106 uses the L2 hand for the 0-1 tutorial; #108's art passes the affordance lint and the squint test.

### D-55 · 2026-10-19 · The save `popup.save.v1`: the readings #93 left open

**Context.** GDD § 2.10 and § 3.8 define `SaveV1`, the save triggers and the unlock and ability rules; #93 asks for a complete game to save in under 4 KB. Several details were open, and one conflicts.

**Decision.**
1. **Packed page records on disk.** Stored literally, a complete game (65 pages, every sticker and best, 11 photos) is about 5.2 KB, over the 4 KB budget. In memory the save is `SaveV1` exactly; on disk each page record is a short code (`'smt12'` = solved, 淘氣, 土地公, best 12 moves; `'t'` = only 土地公). Everything else is stored as `SaveV1` has it. A complete game is about 1.7 KB.
2. **`bestPar`** is the fewest **moves** (non-wait inputs, the solver's par unit, § 3.7) the player solved the page in; it only improves. 0 is allowed (trivial tutorial beats).
3. **The guard is strict** (`isStoredSave`): every field must be well formed or the whole save is fresh. Our own writer is the only source. Then the save is **reconciled with the registry**: records of pages no longer registered are dropped; `furthest` is at least the page after the last solved one (pages added between visits open in order); `current` must be unlocked, else it is `furthest`.
4. **Migration:** the version stays in the record (`v`) under the same key. `UPGRADES[n]` upgrades a record from `n` to `n + 1`; a newer save than the game knows reads as fresh (it is not overwritten until the next save trigger).
5. **Abilities.** Owned words and growth come from the furthest point (every page before `furthest`, plus solved pages). A page plays with its **designed** words and growth from the registry. Later words appear only with `laterWords` (GDD: "hidden by default"). Growth always stays as designed: extra edge kinds change where Baokaka can walk and what he wants, so only the designed growth is the state the solver proved.
6. **Unlocks** are linear over the registry's play order, which runs across chapters. A chapter is open when its first page is. Completing a page makes the next one current (replays resume after the replayed page); completing the furthest page unlocks the next.
7. **Save triggers:** `ProgressStore` writes on every change that alters the save (page complete, sticker, photo, settings, and opening a page, since `current` is the resume point) and again on `visibilitychange: hidden`. Changes that change nothing write nothing.
8. **Reset keeps the settings.** 「重新開始」 is "play the story again", not "forget that 阿嬤 needs 大字".
9. **The RPG saves.** `src/shared/legacy.ts` holds the RPG key list (`baokaka.quest`, `.v2`–`.v6`) plus a `baokaka.quest*` prefix scan when the storage can list keys. `hadRpgSave` detects them. `cleanupLegacyStorage(storage, { rpg: true })` deletes them, and `retireRpgSave` does both and answers whether the cover shows 「寶咖咖的新冒險開始了！」 (`RPG_FAREWELL`) once. Boot still calls `cleanupLegacyStorage` without `rpg`; #109 switches it.

**Why.** The packed code is the smallest change that meets the 4 KB budget: the shape and names everyone reads stay as the GDD has them. A strict guard plus reconciliation keeps the loader simple and safe; growing the game never strands a player.

**Consequences.**
- #97 uses `ProgressStore` (`useSyncExternalStore(store.subscribe, store.get)`) for the cover, 目錄 and resume. It maps `settings.speed` 0/1/2 to the session's `slow`/`normal`/`fast`.
- #101 calls `completePage(id, { mischief, tudigong, moves })` and `earnSticker`. #103 calls `keepPhoto`. #104 calls `updateSettings` and `reset` behind the 3-second hold.
- #109 calls `retireRpgSave()` at boot instead of the RPG-preserving cleanup.
- The bonus page 「象山的日出」 (all 65 土地公) is not in the registry yet; its unlock lands with #135.

### D-56 · 2026-10-19 · Character sprites in the build: the readings #94 left open

**Context.** #94 wires the character artist's baked frames (`docs/art/v2/baked/poses.json`, `manifest.json`, D-35) into the game: assets in the build, a manifest contract, a loader, an animation player, mirroring, anchors, the standee bake and the half grade. Several choices were open.

**Decision.**
1. **Where the files live: `src/popup/assets/sprites/`, imported by Vite** (not `public/sprites/`). `npm run sync:sprites` (`scripts/sync-sprites.ts`, vite-node) writes `poses/<char>-<pose>-<i>@{2x,3x}.webp`, `stills/<name>@{2x,3x}.webp`, the shared `floor@{2x,3x}.webp` and the typed `src/popup/sprites/sprites.gen.ts` (`SPRITE_IDS`, `SpriteId`, `SPRITE_MANIFEST`). The loader takes URLs from `import.meta.glob(..., { eager: true, query: '?url' })`: hashed file names for cache busting, about 10 KB of strings in the bundle, and nothing fetched until an image asks. Eager rather than the issue's `eager: false`, which would make one lazy JS chunk per frame (150 chunks) just to learn a URL. `vite.config.ts` never inlines a sprite as base64 (`assetsInlineLimit`), so each is cached on its own.
2. **The standee bake is part of the sync, with the kit's own filter.** Chromium (Playwright, DPR 2 and 3) renders each source frame through `standeeFilter(id, { bake: true })` (`src/popup/art/defs.ts`): the 3.2 pt `#FBF6EC` border, the 0.6 pt `#8F8296` cut line, the stacked contact + soft shadow, and the easel tab on standing poses (`config.ts` `easel`). Two departures from the PageLab filter, both in its `bake` variant:
   - **Symmetric shadows** (0/5 and 0/1.6, not 3/5 and 1/1.6): the game mirrors frames for facing left, and a baked x offset would then fall against the light. The light's direction is carried by the floor contact ellipse, drawn outside the frame and nudged +2 pt.
   - **A die-cut contour:** the card is dilated from the print's alpha softened (blur 1.2) and thresholded, and the print is trimmed to the card. Raw alpha grew ghost cards around whisker tips and the artist's faint baked ground shadows.
3. **The crop:** every frame of a sprite shares one box, the union of what the filter drew, snapped to whole pt so @2x and @3x agree. Anchors move with the crop; the test checks the offset is within the padding.
4. **Encoding: Pillow WebP (`method=6`, quality 86, alpha quality 65)**, the artist's encoder (Python + Pillow are already the art pipeline's requirement). Chromium's canvas encoder made frames about 40 % larger. The border and its soft shadow make alpha most of each frame's bytes, and lossy alpha at 65 is invisible on the smooth border edge; without it the core set was 528 KB @3x.
5. **Idempotent:** each sprite records a hash of everything its bake read (source frames, entry, `config.ts`, the filter, the bake version). An unchanged sprite is not re-rendered and an unchanged manifest is not rewritten; stale files are deleted; `--force` re-bakes all.
6. **The game's readings of the artist's poses live in `src/popup/sprites/config.ts`:** the lazy-load group (0 = the core set every page preloads; `n` = first needed in chapter `n`; `'ui'` = album, photo, ending), the play mode of non-cycles (`loop` for 2-frame gestures, `once` for `jump`, `push`, `sleep`), the held key frame, events (`step` on crawl frames 0 and 4, `land` on jump frame 2, `push`), `easel`, `noMirror` (Mocha's `album` faces the reader) and hand-placed `head` / `hand` points. The automatic head is the centre of the top 10 pt of the silhouette; the footprint is the silhouette's extent in the 5 pt above the ground line. Word poses join with their word's chapter (`wave` 2, `refuse` 3, `hug` 5, `wonder` 6); `pull` / `wobble` 9, `walk` 10, Baokaka's `sleep` and `zhuazhou` `'ui'` (the ending).
7. **Payload (#94 R3):** the core set (19 Baokaka + 15 Mocha frames and the floor) is **383.5 KB @3x / 222.3 KB @2x**, under 400 KB; all 75 frames are 920 KB @3x. A test pins the 400 KB limit. No atlas: 35 requests, all issued at once as a page opens, all cacheable; an atlas would complicate the mirroring and the per-chapter split for little gain over HTTP/2.
8. **The player:** `<SpriteAnim sprite anim facing playing reduced>` draws one `<image>`, swapping its `href` per frame. One shared rAF clock (`spriteClock`) serves every playing sprite and stops when none plays; a sprite re-renders only when its frame changes. Timing starts at mount, whether or not the frames have loaded, so a `once` animation always reports `onDone` on time. Reduced motion and stills hold the key frame (a held `once` is done at once). Events crossed during a long stall (a background tab) replay at most one cycle.
9. **Mirroring and the bracelet:** frames face right; `facing: 'l'` is `scale(-1, 1)` about the anchor x (`spriteTransform`), so the foot point never jumps. In the game the actor's card-flip group (`flipScale`, bible § 5) does this mirroring for 寶咖咖. **The bracelet-wrist rule of D-33 does not apply to mirrored gameplay frames:** a mirrored standee is the same printed card seen turned, and the bracelet moving wrists at 70 pt is invisible. Poses that must not mirror are marked `noMirror`.
10. **Half grade: baked variants per chapter grade**, not a runtime overlay. A runtime overlay would need a mask or blend per sprite (or would tint the touchables under it, which must never be graded). Per-chapter lazy loading means a page downloads only its chapter's variant. No chapter grade exists yet (`gradeTokens` arrives with #96's environment bake), so the frames are ungraded for now; #96 adds the grade table to the sync (a `grade` dimension on the files and manifest).

**Why.** Everything the deploy needs is committed and checked (files ↔ manifest ↔ the artist's manifests); the game never runs a filter, and the M1 payload fits.

**Consequences.**
- `npm run sync:sprites` needs Playwright's Chromium and Python 3 with Pillow. Re-run it after the artist re-bakes, then `npm test` (the files ↔ manifest test) and `npm run visual`.
- #95 maps every sim mode to its sprite and animation (`babySprite` in `view/actors/Baby.tsx` is a first mapping) and Mocha's hop / land / push; it can use `onDone` / `onEvent`.
- #96 adds the per-chapter half grade to the sync.
- #141's new frames arrive through `poses.json` and need only a sync (and a `config.ts` line if they are not core).
- #98 reads the bubble's spring anchor from the sprite's `head`.

### D-57 · 2026-10-19 · Character animations on the simulation: the readings #95 left open

**Context.** #95 maps every simulation mode and event to a character animation (GDD § 3.9, § 2.2.7, § 2.3), moves the standees as rigid cards (bible § 5, § 8) and reports which GDD frames the artist still owes. Several choices were open.

**Decision.**
1. **One table, pure:** `src/popup/view/actors/animMap.ts`. `babyAnim({ mode, edge, riding, growth, watching, word })` and `mochaAnim(...)` return an `ActorAnim` (the pose, how its frame is chosen, the GDD frame it is, fallback flags); `chooseFrame` turns it into the frame for the time into the tick. `BABY_MODE_ANIM` has an entry per `BabyMode` and `MOCHA_STATE_ANIM` one per view state of Mocha (`sit`, `onPart`, `hop`, `squeeze`, `eyeContact`, `push`, `glare`, `puff`, `nope`, `purr`, `inBox`); tests insist on both and on every frame existing.
2. **What decides the pose, beyond the mode:** the kind of the edge stepped this tick (crawl / climb / cruise / walk; Mocha's jump / squeeze), a ride (his node moved with a part: `bounce`, unless he is crying, saying, eating, grabbing or done), a finger down (touch-watch), and the word being said (from the tick's events: 掰掰 `wave`, 不要！ `refuse` when the refused set grew, 抱抱 `hug`, 飛機！ `wonder`; 嗶！ and the rest `point`). Idle is `sit`; done is the `clap` loop. `PageView` gets the tick's events through a new `events` prop.
3. **The eye contact is the tick that ends with `mocha.stare`** (D-44). Arriving on that tick, he hops and faces the camera from the landing on; the next tick (the push) shows `push` frame 2 while the pushable starts its fall. Stills of the stare state show the eye contact.
4. **Strides synced to the lerp:** a crawl or walk cycle runs whole strides per edge (one per 44 units, at least one), its frame read from the eased step progress, so hands plant once per stride and never slide. Above 24 frames a second (two strides in a normal tick) it switches to the 4-frame `key` cycle. Between steps and in stills the cycle's key frame holds. Mocha's hop is `jump` crouch / air / land at 0 / 18 % / 82 % of his step.
5. **Standee motion (`standee.ts`):** the step finishes 120 ms before the tick ends (at least 60 % of the tick), leaving the ±2° damped landing wobble its 120 ms. The card leans 4° into the travel direction at mid-step (sin of the eased progress), none on a vertical step. The 2 px arc stays `stepPose`'s (Mocha's 24 px hop arc too). Turning stays the card flip.
6. **Fallbacks until #141** (each logged once in dev with `console.info`, never an error): look L/R → `sit` card-flipping away at 30 % of the tick and back at 70 % (the head-turn frames don't exist, and a head still pasted on `sit` reads worse than a flip); touch-watch → `sit` tilted 3° toward the finger; watch-sway → `sit` rocking ±3° over 2 s (a CSS loop, so stills and reduced motion stop it); ride-giggle → `bounce`; carried → `sit`; climb → the crawl stride; Mocha glare → `sit` with the stiff lean; purr → `loaf`; in-box → the standee hidden (the box art frames the perch). `reach` has one frame of the GDD's two; it is held. Mocha's tail flick at an unreachable perch uses `lure`, his loaf on a part (C4) `loaf`, a squeeze `squeeze`.
7. **Growth guard:** `pull` / `wobble` need growth 2, `walk` growth 3 (`GROWTH_NEEDS`); a standing pose any input asks for too early becomes the crawl. Sitting and crawling show at every stage.
8. **Reduced motion:** loops and cycles hold their key frame, a step sequence its landed frame; a step is a 180 ms fade-slide (from 25 % opacity, sliding the last 10 units in) with no arc, lean or wobble; the look flips and the sway are off.
9. **Lamp glow:** within 110 units of a lit light attractor (visible, active, not consumed), a warm radial gradient (`standeeGlow` in the stage defs, ≈ 18 % at its core) sits behind 寶咖咖. A gradient, not the bible's blur, because nothing on a mover may run a filter.
10. **`hug` moved to the core set** (it is the cuddle frame from the prologue on): the core set is 396.6 KB @3x, still under the 400 KB test.
11. **Fixtures for states no registered page reaches yet** use lab-only stages in `dev/labPages.ts` (`lab-push`: 1-2 with a cup on the pillar; `lab-cry`: 0-1 with the bead hidden until the mobile spins): fixtures `lab-eye-contact` and `lab-crying`. `fixtureFrame` resolves lab stages too. #106 adds `c0p3-eye-contact` and `c0p4-crying` on the real pages.
12. **The missing-frame report** is `FRAME_REPORT` (GDD § 3.9 rows, frames needed, delivered poses and frames, fallback) shown at `#/pagelab?section=sprites` (visual target `pagelab-sprites`): ✓ when the delivered frames cover the count, ✗ otherwise, `later` for M3/M4 rows (carried, climb, stand-cruise, walk, Mocha's linked walk).

**Why.** The view stays a pure function of `(prev, state, ms, events)`, so PageLab scrubs and stills render the same frames as play; the table is the one place #141 edits when its frames land.

**Consequences.**
- #141: replace each fallback in `animMap.ts` with its frame after the sync and watch the report's ✗ rows turn ✓ (`reach` needs its second frame too).
- #98 (bubble) reads the shown pose's head through `spriteHead(babyAnim(...).sprite)`, as `PageView` does now.
- #106 adds the real `c0p3-eye-contact` / `c0p4-crying` fixtures; the lab stages can then go.

### D-58 · 2026-10-19 · #141's character frames: drawing, wiring and the preload budget

**Context.** #141 draws the GDD § 3.9 frames the first pose delivery left out (D-57's fallbacks) with the Art v2 rig, and wires them. Adding about 110 KB @3x of frames to the core set would have put it at 446 KB, over the 400 KB test (#94 R3). Several readings were open.

**Decision.**
1. **The frames** (`docs/art/v2/src/bk_poses.py`, `mo_poses.py`; Art v2 § 5): 寶咖咖 `reach` frame 2 (higher on his knees, both hands up), `look` (2: over his shoulder, then far ahead), `watch` (2: rocking toward and away from a light), `giggle` (leaning back, feet up, laughing), `touchwatch` (head tipped up, curious), `carried` (lifted under the arms, legs dangling, no shadow; the carrier's hands are the carrier's art), `climb` (3); 摩卡 `glare` (new head expression: narrowed slanted eyes, a furrow, ears flattened out and back; tail up), `purr` (the loaf, eyes closed), `inbox` (head, shoulders and front paws, cut flat at the rim).
2. **Climb is pull-up → knee-over → up on all fours,** not "stand on the step": the step-climb stage (growth 1) comes before standing (growth 2), and the GDD's growth row says "Pull-up, then knee-over". The step is the page's art, not the frame's. Each frame stands on its own point, because the anchor moves up the edge as he climbs: frame 1's floor is the lower one, frame 2 straddles the riser at mid height, frame 3 is on top with a foot trailing over the edge. The frames are switched at 0 / ⅓ / ⅔ of the step (`CLIMB_AT`); the growth guard keeps it for growth ≥ 1.
3. **Wiring** (`animMap.ts`): `looking` → `look` (loop, 1.5 fps), `watching` → `watch` (pingpong, 1.5 fps), `reaching` → `reach` (pingpong), touch-watch → `touchwatch` (the 3° tilt toward the finger stays), a ride → `giggle`, `carried` → `carried`, climb edges → `climb`, Mocha `glare` → `glare` (the stiff lean per touch stays), `purr` → `purr`, `inBox` → `inbox`, **drawn at the box perch** in front of the box art instead of hiding him. The fallback-only code is gone (the look card flip `lookFacing`, the sway CSS, the `sway` / `lookAround` / `hidden` / `glare` flags). The report shows M1 22 of 22; only the later stand-cruise row is short (3 of 4 frames).
4. **Preload groups mean "first needed in chapter n, and every chapter after"**: `pageSprites(ch)` now preloads every pose with `group ≤ ch` (before, a chapter-2 word pose was not preloaded on chapter 3 pages).
5. **The core set is the prologue's (GDD § 4.3):** poses the prologue never shows move to chapter 1: `point` and `eat` (no words or snacks there), `giggle` (the first ride is 1-2's train), Mocha `glare`, `squeeze`, `puff` (no locked parts, gaps or water). Drawn poses no game state shows yet go to `'ui'`: `bounce` (replaced by `giggle`), Mocha `perch`, `stretch`, `sleep`, `rub`. `carried` is chapter 5 (5-3's 阿姨 picks him up), `climb` chapter 7 (7-3). Result: **core 381.1 KB @3x / 220.3 KB @2x** (≤ 400 KB); chapter 1 adds 66 KB (a test caps it at 100 KB).
6. **Re-bakes only touch what changed:** Chromium's blurs are not bit-exact from run to run (a re-render of an untouched pose moved up to 5 % of its pixels), so `bake_poses.py` keeps a hash of each pose's SVG (ids renumbered) in `baked/poses-src.json` and re-renders only poses whose drawing or box changed; `--force` re-renders all, `--adopt` records hashes for files already baked. Delivered frames stay byte-identical (only `reach` frame 1 was re-cropped, as its pose gained a frame).

**Why.** The frames come from the same rig, heads and toolkit as the delivered ones, so the style and the photo likeness carry over; the budget is met by preloading what each chapter needs rather than by lowering quality.

**Consequences.**
- #106: the prologue pages (0-2 TV watch-sway, 0-4 basket in-box, look and cry) can use the real frames; the `lab-lamp` / `lab-box` stages and `lab-looking` / `lab-watching` / `lab-mocha-inbox` fixtures can then move to the real pages.
- #120: `carried` and `climb` exist; check them in context on the first chapter 5 / 7 pages (the climb frames assume a riser about 22 pt high).
- A pose that a new chapter needs earlier than its group says only loads on demand (placeholder card until decoded): move it to that chapter's group.

### D-59 · 2026-10-19 · The environment bake and the chapter grade: the readings #96 left open

**Context.** #96 builds the bake from the environment artist's SVG to the bitmaps the game draws (bible § 9.1, § 10 item 2; GDD § 3.9), the runtime that loads them by art key, the chapter grade (`gradeTokens(time)`, bible § 3.3) and the characters' half grade left over from #94 (D-56 § 10). Several choices were open.

**Decision.**
1. **`scripts/bake-popup.ts`, not `.mjs`.** It runs on vite-node, like `sync-sprites.ts`, so it imports the kit, the stage and the pages as TypeScript; the issue's `.mjs` would have needed a second copy of them. The SVG and the hash of every bitmap come from pure functions in `src/popup/env/bake.ts`, shared with the test.
2. **What is baked.** Every registered page's backdrop key (its own drawing in `BACKDROP_ART`, else its chapter's placeholder stage, so every page ships a baked backdrop and #108 only swaps drawings), every part art key with a drawing in `PART_SOURCES` (one bitmap per state; a lifted variant per state for `tab` / `slider` / `wheel` / `flap`; `pivot` for rotary parts, `hinge` for flaps), and cut-outs a page places. Backdrops cover the page SVG's whole view box (page, floor-page widening, page block) with alpha outside the paper: the extra height of tall phones stays cover (bible § 2.1 wins over GDD § 2.1's "background art fills the extra height", as the issue's note asks). The live ledges, ramps, rails and posts stay SVG, because the train runs behind the ledges (§ 2.2 z3/z4).
3. **Encoding:** Chromium at DPR 2 with fonts loaded per glyph, Pillow WebP `method=6` (backdrops q80 / alpha 70, parts q86 / alpha 65). Backdrops are ≈ 155–160 KB each, inside the bible's 120–200 KB.
4. **Idempotence and determinism.** Each bitmap records a hash of its exact SVG (plus box and encoder settings); an unchanged source is skipped, so a second run writes nothing. A forced re-bake of the current sources was byte-identical (no blur churn here, unlike the character bake). `env.test.ts` recomputes every hash, so a source edited without a re-bake fails CI.
5. **Runtime.** URLs via an eager `?url` glob (D-56's reasoning), never inlined. Resolution order in the registry: a baked bitmap, else the live placeholder (unknown keys are logged once in dev, never an error). Baked part art is drawn at scale 1 with its local origin on the part's hit centre (not fitted like the kit art). "Per-chapter lazy loading" is per page: a page preloads only its own bitmaps and, once they are decoded, the next page's backdrop; the manifest itself is a few KB, so it is not split into chunks. `data-sprites="ready"` now waits for both the characters and the page's bitmaps.
6. **Touchables stay live SVG** until their own art lands: the affordance lint reads their seals, grips and cues. A baked wheel or flap is supported (pivot, hinge, lifted variant); its wrapper carries `data-grip` and `data-baked-marigold`, and its source must pass the lint in `env.test.ts`.
7. **The environment grade** is `envGrade(chapter) = worldGrade(time)` (D-27: relative to 00:00, because the pop-up palette is painted for the night), baked by multiplying every colour in a backdrop's or cut-out's SVG except lights (lamps, lit vellum, neon, pinholes, the moon, lamp shades), foil, the touchables' marigold and seal, chalk, the done and lock badges, the indigo shadow, the cut-edge rim, the standee card and the chapter's sky (the colour clock is the sky). Chapters 0–2 sit between the identical 00:00 and 01:00 rows, so M1's grade is identity; it starts to lift at chapter 3.
8. **The characters' half grade** is `1 − (1 − g)/2` of the table's grade (D-27's `characterGrade`, since the v2 art is authored in daylight): ×(0.775, 0.785, 0.86) for chapters 0–2. It is baked into the **print only** (a colour matrix before the standee filter): the warm-white card, the cut line and the shadows keep their own night colours, so the die-cut read stays crisp. `sync:sprites` bakes a variant per grade a registered chapter shows (`g/<key>/<stem>`, deduplicated: chapters 0 and 1 share one), only for gameplay poses that chapter preloads; stills and `'ui'` poses stay ungraded. The graded core set is 367.9 KB @3x (the ungraded one 381.1 KB).
9. **Budgets.** Page art = a page's backdrop + every state and lifted variant of its baked parts + its cut-outs, at @2x (≤ 350 KB). First load = the first chapter's environment art + its characters @3x in its grade (≤ 2.5 MB). Characters keep their own ≤ 400 KB core test (D-56).
10. **1-2's board** is the first real part art: proof A's split-flap board, a `prop` with `times` (月亮線 00:20 往寶咖咖的家) and `flip` (福德線 00:21 往土地公的家), hanging on cords from the backdrop page's top. Its hit area became the board (52, 40, 176 × 64), and the 土地公 (236, 98) peeks from behind its corner when flipped. GDD § 3.4 keeps the original numbers; the pages test ignores the 土地公's position.
11. **`PageDef.cutouts?: { art, x, y }[]`** (GDD § 3.2): pure art, drawn over the backdrop and under every part; an unknown key draws nothing.

**Why.** Everything the deploy needs is committed and checked, the game never runs a filter, and art issues only add drawings: no per-page code.

**Consequences.**
- #108 draws chapter 0–1 art into `src/popup/env/art/` (`BACKDROP_ART`, `PART_SOURCES`, `CUTOUT_SOURCES`), runs `npm run bake:popup`, and keeps each page under 350 KB (`npm run art:report`).
- A page registered in a new chapter needs `npm run sync:sprites` (its characters' grade variant) and `npm run bake:popup` (its backdrop); the tests fail until both are run.
- Chapters with a non-identity environment grade (3+) should check their lights and foil stay ungraded in the bake (the keep list in `env/grade.ts`).

### D-60 · 2026-10-19 · The UI shell: the readings #97 left open

**Context.** #97 builds the screens around the page (GDD § 2.13; popup art bible § 2.4, § 7, § 9.2): the cover, the HUD and the bottom bar, the 目錄, the pause menu, the page fold and the chapter turn, on the save from #93. It also removes the #92 text-size waiver on the HUD and the bar. Several choices were open.

**Decision.**
1. **Navigation is a pure state machine** (`src/popup/game/nav.ts`): a *base* (the cover or a page, with a `run` count so 重來 and a replay remount it) and at most one *layer* over it (the menu, the 目錄, the album, the settings). A layer over a page holds the page's session (`PagePlayer` `hold`, a `layer` pause reason) and closing it resumes the page where it was; the 目錄 opened from the menu returns to the page with 「繼續」, from the cover with 「封面」. `GameShell` (`view/screens/`) owns one `ProgressStore` and renders the state.
2. **Resume is one tap:** the cover's 「繼續」 opens `save.current` (or the furthest unlocked page if it is gone). **First launch** is "no page record yet and still on the first page": only 「開始」. Opening a page makes it current (`store.openPage`); completing it calls `completePage` and turns to the next registered page; after the last one, the 目錄 opens on the cover. The sticker card between fold and next page is #101's.
3. **HUD and bar at R5.** Every word in them is ≥ 20 px at ≥ 7:1, so the waiver is gone: the landmark is 25 px serif, the page title, chapter line, clock and labels 20 px. The tray's 「抓周」 caption and the stickers' names became accessible names only (the icons carry them); a landmark plus page title too long for the label is condensed with `textLength`, never shrunk. The 抓周 tray counts solved spreads (page 6 of chapters 1–10, at most 9 coins). The portrait's label is 「說話」 (not in § 5.7; the kit proof's word). The bar's discs are 56 px in 62 × 96 px buttons; 左手模式 reverses the row.
4. **Pause menu** = the kit's `Sheet` with a new `tone="paper"` (`kit-panel--paper` in `kit.css`, popup tokens only) and paper card buttons: 繼續 (the marigold one) / 目錄 / 設定 / 封面. Tapping the scrim is 繼續.
5. **The art outside the pages is baked** like the backdrops (D-59): `SCREEN_SOURCES` in `src/popup/env/art/screens.ts` → `src/popup/assets/env/screens/` (the 花布 quilt 430 × 932, the cover scene: 阿嬤's low table with the closed book, its debossed foil title, foil skyline and ribbon, and the folded 目錄 map), 151 KB together, counted in the first load. Baokaka (`clap`, swaying on a CSS loop) and Mocha (`sit`) stand on the cover as live sprites; every word on a screen is live text.
6. **The 目錄** draws all eleven stations on a serpentine 月亮線 ribbon (open to the furthest unlocked chapter, dotted beyond), with n/18 (n/12 in the prologue) under open stations; the strip under the map shows a chapter's pages by id `cNpK` — a page not registered yet is locked like one not reached. Thumbnails are the pages' baked backdrops. Chapters 0–1 are what M1 can play; #113 finishes it.
7. **The fold and the turn** (`view/transitions/pageTurn.ts`) animate the page's own `data-layer` groups with WAAPI: folding and rising are a `scaleY` about each layer's base (SVG groups take 2D transforms; the silhouette of a `rotateX`), grouped by depth (five groups: the fold 55 ms apart to fit 150–600 ms; the rise 60 ms apart, the standees last). The leaf is a temporary DOM of three nested strips over the page box whose faces are the two pages' baked backdrops; it shrinks to the floor's depth as it lands. The new page is swapped in under the leaf at 600 ms. The chapter turn moves the whole book (cover, HUD, bar, page) with one `perspective() rotateX rotateY scale` transform over the baked quilt, slows the leaf to 840 ms, throws 14 confetti flecks, and hangs a simple chapter card on strings (#102 replaces it with the departure board and narration). A full-screen invisible button skips any turn. Reduced motion: a 250 ms cross-fade of the old backdrop plus a 4 px rise.
8. **The album and the settings** are stub screens (「這一頁，還在畫。」 and 「返回」, not in § 5.7) until #103 / #104; the navigation to them is real. The 提示 button is wired to nothing until #100.
9. **Fixtures:** a fixture can now be a game-shell screen (`screen: { nav, save }`, a seeded in-memory store): `cover-first`, `cover-continue`, `contents-ch1`, `contents-locked`, `page-hud`, `page-hud-left`, `page-menu`. The QA scripts wait for `[data-screen-ready]` on screens that are not pages, and a `[data-screen]` without `[data-hit]` is not a missing hit layer.

**Why.** The shell is the part of the game every session touches; it reads the save, holds the world whenever anything covers it, and keeps every word at the grandparent rule.

**Consequences.**
- #100 wires the 提示 button (`PageChrome.onHint`), #101 inserts the sticker card between `onDone` and the turn and earns stickers into the bar, #102 replaces the turn's card, #103 / #104 replace `StubScreen`, #109 points `#/` at `GameShell` and calls `retireRpgSave`.
- A same-chapter page fold is wired but can only be seen once a chapter has two registered pages in a row (#106); M1 today turns 0-1 → 1-2 as a chapter turn.

### D-61 · 2026-10-19 · Bubbles, barks, subtitles: the readings #98 left open

**Context.** #98 builds everything said on the page: the thought bubble's change of mind (GDD § 2.2.6), bark bubbles (§ 2.2.1, § 2.3), subtitle bubbles and the narration strip (§ 2.13, § 5.1), and the `content/script.ts` infrastructure. It also removes the #92 text-size waiver on the bubble. Several choices were open.

**Decision.**
1. **Text ≥ 20 screen px by growing the bubbles.** Bubble text is 22 page units; every bubble is drawn × `talkScale(scale)` = max(1, 20.2 / (22 × book scale)): ≈ 1.01 at 390 × 844, ≈ 1.39 at 375 × 667. The `[data-bubble]` waiver is gone; `WAIVERS` is empty.
2. **Placement** (`view/talk/place.ts`, `layout.ts`): each bubble tries candidate boxes in order and takes the first that covers neither Baokaka's frame box nor the goal (a hard avoid). It also avoids Mocha and the bubbles placed before it (soft). If no candidate is clear, it takes the one that covers least. The thought bubble goes first, to his right (to his left past x 280), then the other side, then both raised step by step. The page's top is the HUD's edge, and nothing is clamped above it. Placement uses the tick's end positions, so a bubble changes place at most once a tick; between ticks a bubble rides with its speaker.
3. **Change of mind:** the bubble before a tick and after it are compared. When they differ, the old icon pops (100 ms) and the new one inflates 100 ms later (200 ms) while the spring and cloud wobble about his head (a damped three-cycle sine, 720 ms). 「要！」 pulses at 1.2 s while he reaches. Under reduced motion all of these are fades, with no wobble or pulse. A still draws only the settled bubble. The light icon is now a music note (§ 2.2.1). The shiny is a foil bead with a sparkle, and crying shows three tears. Every icon is a shape.
4. **Barks** come from events and mode changes (`view/talk/barks.ts`). Baokaka: the engine's `bark`, 好吃！ on `eat`, 哇—— when crying starts. Mocha: 「……」 at the stare, 「喵？」 at a nope, 「不要。」 at a puff, 「喵。」 on arrival, 「呼嚕嚕……」 when Baokaka starts cuddling him. Each speaker barks at most once a tick, and a bark stays three ticks (pop, hold, fade, 1.4 s). 這個！ and ㄅㄚ！ are in the table for pages' own lines. The bark text moved from `core/events.ts` to `content/barks.ts` (zh-Hant lives in `content/`).
5. **Subtitle queue** (`game/talk.ts`): lines arrive in groups (the intro on a fresh page, the lines an event fired). Each group is cut into beats of ≤ 3 lines, and narration counts toward the 3. A new group waits behind the beat on screen. Tapping any bubble or the strip advances the beat, and the tap never reaches the page. Nothing advances on a timer (GDD: tap to advance). An event line fires once, on the tick its predicate's counter first holds.
6. **Anchors (§ 5.1):** narration goes on a washi strip at the top of the page (or at the bottom if the top would cover him or the goal). 寶咖咖, 摩卡 and 阿伯 (from the 土地公) hang on wire springs. 廣播姊姊 speaks from a paper loudspeaker drawn at the page's top right while she speaks. Off-page voices (阿嬤, 媽媽, 爸爸, 店員小哥, 背包客, 咻咻, npc) get a card with no spring under the strip. A labelled card carries a speaker-coloured tab: dark inks at ≥ 7:1 under the cream label text. Subtitles also softly avoid touchables, because they take taps.
7. **`content/script.ts`:** `SCRIPT: Record<LineKey, { who, text, label? }>`, the `SPEAKERS` table, `loadLine` / `loadLines`.
