A few things were left open: how 3-6's glasses sit on a creature drawn by this issue; what 3-5's 「he squeezes out on top」 shows without moving Mocha's standee; what the stage draws for the chapter's single-node runs (3-3's nest, 3-6's stack); and whether the doors and the flaps need new machinery.

**Decision.**
1. **The palette kit** is `POPUP_CAMPUS` (bible § 6 row 3: lake blue and palm green, the library's warm amber as the one accent; every warm token ≥ ΔE 12 from marigold, `affordance.test.ts`) with reusable cut-outs in `env/art/c3.ts`: `royalPalm` (a tall thin flat whose fronds are serrated paper combs, in receding rows on 3-2), `campusLamp`, `campusBuilding` (the 1930s halls' arcade, lit windows, hipped roof), `azaleas`, `lakeWater` (glassine), `bookRow`, `bookStack`, `bookcase`, `archWindow`, `readingLamp`.
2. **New ledge styles** (`view/art/ledges.ts`): the 松山新店線 car body (green), the campus walk (pavers, curb, azaleas), 醉月湖's promenade (ashlar embankment), the stacks' gallery and the reading room's floor, the desk and the giant book's page block. Single-node runs draw what the node is: on the lake, the goose's nest on a grassy hummock; on the book spread, a pile of books (where 藍教授 sits). The bank's slope is a flight of stone steps. Foreground card `azalea`.
3. **Reuse, not machinery:** 2-1's doors became `carDoors(band, bandDeep)` (2-1's bitmaps unchanged, byte for byte), and `popFlap` moved to `env/art/common.ts`; 3-6's pages pop up with it (the bookmark folds flat over the gap, the turned page arches from the lamp to the stack, the cat page stands up).
4. **A held decoy sits on its holder's face** at a fixed offset from the holder's feet (`HELD_DECOY_FACE`, (−11, −31), 0.62 scale: 藍教授's eye; only 3-6 holds a decoy). A per-creature anchor can come when a second page needs one.
5. **3-5's 淘氣 is a cut-out**, `c3/squeeze` (`visibleWhen`: Mocha in the cart with aisle 2 shut): books tumbling off the shelves over the aisle, chalk squeeze lines, his 「哼。」. Mocha's standee stays where the engine puts him.
6. **Not drawn** (as D-78): the photo frames stay #103's composites with the 書 drawn in (`treasureArt` chapter 3); the chapter card has no landmark slot and the 目錄 shows the new backdrops; the 月亮線 window strip does not scroll. The 3-2 cameo is the 店員小哥's delivery bike (外送 box) and his lines, not a drawn figure (the walkway is the play path).

**Why.** Each reading keeps the art baked and the view unchanged where it could, keeps the page data's hit areas (only 3-4's plate key moved to `c3/plate`), and stays inside the payload and animated-node budgets (c3p1 128 nodes at its busiest tick, every page ≤ 308 KB).

**Consequences.** Later chapters reuse `carDoors`, `popFlap`, the campus ledge pattern (a single node drawn as what it is) and `HELD_DECOY_FACE` until a page needs a per-creature anchor.

### D-81 · 2026-10-20 · Chapter 4's pages: unreachable lures, a lit trail, the claw as a node, props are never puzzle inputs (#118)

**Context.** #118 writes chapter 4 「霓虹燈睡著了」 in full (`docs/design/pages/c4.md`, the D-77 pattern) and builds 4-1 … 4-6 as data. The outline met the engine in four places: a lure that must hold him in one spot after 不要！ (arriving at Mocha clears the refusal, so he walks straight back to the decoy); a trail of toggling lights with a cry that wakes a sign; a claw machine that "picks up Baokaka" before the `carrier` preset exists (#120); and a drop *button*, when the only tappable two-state preset without a flap's look is `prop`, which is drawn as a discovery and never wears marigold.

**Decision.**
1. **A lure meant to hold him has an unreachable place.** Where he must stay put after 不要！ (4-1's doors, 4-6's stage front), Mocha's `reachNode` is a node he can't enter (the shut doorway; behind the amp): he crawls as near as he can and reaches, never "arrives", so the refusal holds with no timing for the player (3-4's pillar, D-79, is the same idea).
2. **Light trails toggle, and only one sign answers a cry, in the dark.** 4-3's signs toggle on 哇！ (radius 160, the first sign's 120) so each 哇！ lights the next one and puts out the last; only the first sign answers a cry (`within: 120`) and only while every sign is off. Otherwise 不要！ to a light (a cry on demand, D-75) relit the first sign and left two lights on — a soft trap the solver found. The outline's 淘氣 (every sign lit at once) can't happen with toggles, so it is Mocha hidden behind a sign as it lights (his shadow).
3. **The claw is a node on a tab, not a carrier.** 4-4's claw is `hook`, attached to the cart tab (`clawX`); the drop lever is a second tab (`claw`, `up` / `down`); his entry (`babyEnter hook`) lifts the lever, and the lifted claw with him in it sends the cart home. One-way reactions (his weight → lever → cart), no cycle. The coin slot's `reachNode` is on the bead's side, so 不要！ mid-ride is harmless. The claw draws as a carriage (`c4/claw-cart`) until #119.
4. **A prop is never a puzzle input.** Props are tap toys (`PropView`: "a discovery, never marigold"); a press the solution needs is a tab, a flap or a perch. 4-4's drop button became a lever.
5. **A decoy can be made by the player.** 4-5's boombox flashing on its stack is a decoy only while it plays (`visibleWhen: boombox on`), and the boombox keeps playing (`wow → on`), so the way the music opens is closed by the music until 不要！. A light alone (priority 4) could never hold him against Mocha.
6. **Placeholders where the preset misleads** (D-77): 4-2's lantern is baked (`env/art/c4.ts`; a pushable's default is a bead and would read as the goal); the three decoys are drawn as themselves (`decoyArt`: the ad screen, the boombox, the disco ball); the 土地公's 算命墨鏡 on the live figure. Chapter 4's music box is an 8-bar loop in E.
7. **Outline changes** are listed in `pages/c4.md` and GDD § 4.4's rows: 4-1's 淘氣 is a tantrum (不要！ emits no event to count), 4-2 turns twice (once for Mocha, once for him), 4-3's 淘氣 the shadow, 4-4 two tabs, 4-5's decoy the boombox, 4-6's shutter is the stage's.

**Why.** Every page stays solver-green with no dead ends, soft traps, walkable decoys or ties and 0 lint warnings, without engine changes, and every key move was checked necessary.

**Consequences.** #119 replaces the placeholders (the claw carriage, the lantern, the decoys) and keeps the hit areas. #120's `carrier` is not needed by 4-4. Later chapters can reuse the unreachable-lure pattern.

### D-82 · 2026-10-20 · Chapter 4's art: the neon kit, 紅樓 in plan, one bitmap for a turning drawing, the claw over the pit (#119)

**Context.** #119 draws chapter 4 「霓虹燈睡著了」 (西門町) over #118's pages: the one chapter where the bible lets neon (§ 3.3 luminous ink) run free. Four things were open: how a sign is lit and asleep in baked paper; how an 8-step wheel that is a *building* reads (and which face is which, since the page's spec numbered them before any art existed); how to keep 4-2 inside its 350 KB when a wheel bakes a bitmap per state (and a lifted one each); and what 4-4's claw hangs over, since a carriage's tab draws a train's track pit under it.

**Decision.**
1. **The neon kit** (`POPUP_NEON`, `env/art/c4.ts`): plum and 紅樓 brick dominant, neon pink and cyan the accent (`POPUP.neonPink` / `neonCyan`, never graded; violet and red tubes added to the never-graded list). Luminous ink is `neonTube` / `neonChars`: the stroke under the kit's 1.6-unit `glowS` with a white-hot core and a 12 % spill (`spill`); a sleeping sign is grey ink with chalk 「zz」 (`signBoard(…, { lit: false })`). Reusable cut-outs: `signBoard`, `brickFace`, `shopfront`, `streetLamp`, `speakerCab`, `ximenSky`, `streetFloor`, `crowd`, `scooter`, `cardboardBox`, `plushHeap`. Ledge styles `neonStreet`, `arcadeShelf`, `karaokeStage`.
2. **紅樓 is drawn in plan** — the octagonal house seen from above as it turns (brick walls, a dark eight-facet roof to its lantern, three lit doorways, the roof's lantern window) inside the marigold knurled rim — while the backdrop shows its upper storey in elevation behind it, the lantern's lit sill being Mocha's `window`. Faces are numbered clockwise from the front: face `k` at bearing 180 + 45k unturned (`RED_HOUSE_FACE`), so doorways 0 and 4 line up west–east in states 2 and 6, the third doorway comes to the front in state 5 (the 土地公) and the window — over face **7**, not the spec's provisional face 2 — faces the street lamp in state 4. `pages/c4.md` is updated; the page data is unchanged.
3. **A wheel whose drawing never changes is baked once.** `c4/red-house` declares one state; the loader already serves a state's bitmap from the first state's when it has none. 4-2's parts went from 388 KB to 66 KB.
4. **A carriage can hang over the backdrop's own floor.** `StageDress.trackPit: false` skips the track pit under a carriage; 4-4's claw (`carriages.ts`, live) hangs over the baked plush heap, and its tab's bitmap is the gantry rail.
5. **The playing boombox is drawn once.** 4-5's boombox part draws the silent boombox `off` and only the music round it `on` (rings, notes, the EQ), because the decoy (`decoyArt('c4/boombox-shine')`) draws the boombox itself on the same spot while it plays; both use `boomboxBody` (`view/art/ximen.ts`).
6. **The dancers' arch is a lift.** Mocha's `archMid` sits 154 above the street; two dancers hold the third up on their hands and Mocha sits on his cap. The cast (`ximen.ts`) are die-cut figures with merged paths (c4p5's busiest tick 141 animated nodes of 150).

**Why.** The page data and every hit area stay as #118 left them; every page is ≤ 283 KB of page art; the chapter reads at a squint as plum and brick with neon accents, the marigold touchables still the brightest warm thing.

**Consequences.** Later chapters with a turning building or a decoy that is also a part reuse the one-bitmap and draw-once patterns; `trackPit: false` is there for any carriage that is not a train.

### D-83 · 2026-10-20 · The M3 engine: carriers and 抱抱, counterweight lifts, cranked loops (#120)

**Context.** #120 builds GDD § 7 M3 items 30–31: the `carrier` preset and 抱抱, the `loop` with a crank, a lift Mocha's weight drives, 飛機！ and the 爬樓梯 growth. The GDD names the carrier's states (`idle` / `carrying@k` / `done`) and says "an idle carrier on an adjacent node picks him up, walks its route 1 edge per tick, then sets him down", but leaves open what "adjacent" means, what happens to a carrier after `done`, what a route may cross, what a closed path does mid-carry, whether he can talk while carried, and how a lift "gets Mocha's weight" (C4). 飛機！'s core (D-45) and the climb frames (#141, D-58) were already in.

**Decision.**
1. **Within reach (one edge)** means she stands on his node, or on a node joined to his by a baby edge (`crawl`, `climb`, `cruise`, `walk`, whatever his growth: he is lifted, not climbing) whose `when` holds, either way. The first carrier in data order answers. An idle carrier stands on `route[0]`; `done`, on the route's last node; `carrying@k` on `route[k]`.
2. **She picks him up only when her whole route is clear** (every step's edge `when` holds) and she isn't `lockedWhen` ("抱抱 a carrier only once its route is clear", 5-5). He moves onto her node on the say tick (at most the one edge), `carried`, wanting nothing; the say tick ends `carried`, and the view still shows the word.
3. **The walk.** One edge per tick; a step whose edge has closed since makes her wait there, holding him, until it opens. Creatures don't stop her (adults step over a crowd of legs). At the route's end she sets him down on a tick of its own: he is `idle` there and decides again next tick.
4. **A carry is never one-way.** 抱抱 next to a `done` carrier carries him back along the route and leaves her `idle` (`carriedBy.back`). Pages need no second carrier to undo a crossing, and the 淘氣 「抱抱 the 阿姨 a second time」 works.
5. **No carrier in reach:** the `hug` event still fires (pages may count it), he stays `saying` with the 抱抱 pose (arms up), and the UI event `hugNobody` makes Mocha purr.
6. **Words wait while he is carried** (`canSay`); the view opens no ring for a tap on him. He weighs nothing while carried: picked up he releases the plate he left, set down he presses the one he lands on.
7. **The state a carry adds is the route index** (`carrying@k`, mirrored in `carriedBy`, plus `back`): the toy pages solve in 5–25 states.
8. **Lint.** A carrier's route steps only between nodes a baby edge joins, never through a height-2 node (`L05-carrier-route`); it has two or more nodes, starts `idle` or `done` (`L03-carrier-start`), and only 抱抱 moves it — no reaction, perch `set` / `onPart` or `landSets` (`L03-carrier-driven`). `route` belongs to carriers and `counterweight` to lifts (`L03-preset-field`).
9. **Counterweight lift (C4):** `PartDef.counterweight: { on, off }` on a `lift`. While Mocha sits on a perch whose `onPart` is the lift it is in `on`; leaving puts it in `off`; petting him re-enters `on`. Lint: some perch weighs it, and its `initial` matches Mocha's start (`L03-counterweight`).
10. **Cranked loop:** the preset already took `drag-rotary` / `tap-cycle`; `loopAttach(part, states, slots, seat)` (`core/define.ts`) builds a rider's `attach` so seat s sits on slot (s + i) mod N in state i.
11. **The view.** Carried, he is drawn `CARRY_LIFT` (34) above the carrier's feet and `CARRY_AHEAD` (16) in front of her, on her art's own tween so the two never drift; lifted over the pick-up tick and lowered over the set-down. The kit's carrier placeholder is an adult (160 tall). A fixture can pin a still mid-step (`show.stepAt`), so the climb's knee-over frame is in a baseline.

**Why.** Every rule keeps a carrier's state to one index, gives the solver nothing new to explore beyond the route, and never strands him: a route can't drop him somewhere he can't be, a closed step only pauses her, and every carry can be undone.

**Consequences.** Chapters 5–7 (#121, #123, #125) build their carriers, the balloon basket and the gondolas from these. Real carrier art draws her hands in her own art; if her arms sit far from the placeholder's, adjust `CARRY_LIFT` (or make it per carrier) then. Gondolas on a loop whose slots differ in height get no carriage art yet (`stage.ts` draws level carriages only): #126 draws them. The climb frames fit the 22-unit riser of the toy page (`climb-step`); #125 checks them on the real terraces.

### D-84 · 2026-10-20 · Chapter 5's pages: a grown-up's step, a printed state part, a one-way leap, and the hint fallback with Mocha (#121)

**Context.** #121 writes chapter 5 「夜市抱抱」 (士林夜市) from the GDD § 4.4 outline on #120's carriers and counterweight lifts. Open: how a carrier crosses what he can't (a counter, reclined chairs) when a carrier walks baby edges; how 5-4's ball, routed by two flipper tabs, is modelled without a one-way pushable; how to keep 5-2's counterweight from stranding him; and the stored-solution hint fallback, which looped on pages whose parts come back to where they were (5-2's basket goes down and up again) and broke off mid-carry.

**Decision.**
1. **A grown-up's step is a `climb` edge.** A counter (5-3, 5-6) or a reclined chair (5-5) is joined by a `climb` edge, which a carrier walks and reaches over (D-83: any baby kind) but Baokaka can't use until chapter 7's 爬樓梯. A carrier idle behind a counter answers 抱抱 from the other side of it. When chapter 7 brings 爬樓梯 these pages are not replayed with it (pages keep the growth learned before them).
2. **A carrier can be called** with `lockedWhen` (5-3: the 雞排老闆 answers only while Mocha holds the counter's bell down). Data order decides who answers when two are in reach: the called one is listed first, so the other (the 阿姨, `done` beside him) answers only while he isn't called — the 淘氣's second hug.
3. **A ball routed by tabs is a printed state part.** 5-4's ball is a `tab` with `input: 'none'` (states `start` / `jackpot` / `tray`) whose reactions to the launch read the flippers; the bead follows it (`nodeBy`). Its baked art is the board (`c5/pachinko-board`), printed per state; a `section` would draw as a turning disc. The launch is a button Mocha presses (and presses again by a pet), not a pushable, so a wrong launch is never one-way.
4. **One-way cat edges keep a counterweight honest.** 5-2's leap from the stall's pole into the basket is `oneWay`: with Baokaka up at the shelf and Mocha back on the pole, a 不要！ to the bead would leave him reaching down for Mocha forever (a soft trap); from the awning or the basket Mocha is always somewhere he can reach.
5. **Lures that hold.** 5-6's lantern and sign have unconnected `reachNode`s, so a refusal holds until the 蔥 shows (as 4-6's amp); visibility conditions can name where he is (`babyAt`), so the 蔥 is seen only from inside the stall, where it is reachable whenever it shows (no tie with the griddle).
6. **The hint fallback** (`core/hint.ts` `solutionAction`, D-63): it now matches Mocha's perch first (parts, creatures and Mocha's perch; then as before), then the scene without its props (a `prop` is never the puzzle: 5-2's untied knot), then the driven parts. A word counts as doing something only if it changes the world (parts, creatures, refusals, a carry), not just his pose for a tick: an out-of-reach 哇！ or a 抱抱 with no one near is no hint. While he is carried the hint waits (a carry ends by itself). 5-3's solution settles after the first carry so its gestures are recorded from the states a player sees.
7. **Placeholders where the preset misleads** (D-77, D-81): steel NT$10 coins (`c5/coin`; a pushable's default is a bead), the pachinko board, the glowing phone and the griddle as decoys (`decoyArt`), and the 土地公's 鴨舌帽. Creatures (the passengers, the crowd of legs, the 彈珠台阿伯, the dog) stay origami birds and carriers the kit's adult placeholder until #122.

**Why.** Every page stays solver-green with no dead ends, soft traps, walkable decoys or ties and 0 lint warnings with no engine change beyond the hint fallback, and every key move was checked necessary.

**Consequences.** #122 replaces the placeholders and keeps the hit areas; real carrier art should show who answers 抱抱 (the 老闆 out at the counter only while called). Later chapters can use `climb` edges for a grown-up's step until 爬樓梯 (chapter 7 learns it; after that such an edge is his too, so chapter 7+ pages need another barrier). The hint fallback's Mocha tier helps any page where Mocha's moves are the puzzle.

### D-85 · 2026-10-20 · Chapter 5's art: the market kit, a carrier baked per look, the board printed on the backdrop, a beat's subtitles never stack (#122)

**Context.** #122 draws chapter 5 「夜市抱抱」 (士林夜市) over #121's pages: the bible's hottest, warmest pages (§ 6 row 5: vermilion and bulb amber, steam white; corrugated stalls and vellum steam). Open: how a carrier's bitmaps should be cut (a part bakes one bitmap per state, and a carrier's states are route indices — `carrying@0 … carrying@3` — that all look the same); how 5-3's 雞排老闆 shows he is in the back until the bell calls him (D-84: `lockedWhen`, which is not a state); how 「轉圈圈」 — the 阿姨's twirl on a carry back — is drawn when the carry back has no state of its own; how to keep 5-4 in its budget when the pachinko board is a tab (a bitmap per state, and a lifted one each); and what to do with what `check:ui` found on the chapter's fixtures.

**Decision.**
1. **The market kit** (`POPUP_MARKET`, `env/art/c5.ts`): vermilion and bulb amber dominant, steam white the accent; cut-outs `bulbString` (a sagging wire of warm pinholes), `awning` (striped, scalloped), `steam` (vellum loops), `lantern`, `signBoard` (luminous ink, or printed cream on vermilion), `marketSky`, `farStalls`, `crowd`, `laneFloor` (the calm dark band the play path runs on, bible § 6), `corrugated`. Ledge styles `marketLane`, `stallFloor`, `stallShelf`, `stallCounter` (5-6: the inner ledge's face is the stall's counter). The kit's warm decorations are held ≥ ΔE 12 from marigold by `affordance.test.ts`.
2. **A carrier is baked per look, not per state.** Its art declares `idle`, `carrying`, `back`, `done` (and `away` for a called carrier); the view picks the look (`MoverView.tsx` `carrierLook`): every `carrying@k` shows `carrying`, a carry back (`carriedBy.back`) shows `back`, and an `idle` carrier whose `lockedWhen` holds shows `away`. A look the art lacks falls back to the state. So 5-3's 老闆 stands at the counter only while Mocha holds the bell down (otherwise he is drawn turned away at the fryer), the 阿姨's `back` is the twirl, and a four-step route costs four bitmaps, not seven. Page data is unchanged.
3. **The carriers hold him at the hip**: `CARRY_LIFT` (34) and `CARRY_AHEAD` (16) checked against the real art (#120's note) — her near hand under his seat just past him, the far arm round his back; unchanged.
4. **5-4's cabinet is printed on the backdrop.** The ball's part (a tab nobody drives, D-84) draws only what lights per state (the launcher's lamp, the jackpot's ring of bulbs, the tray's lamp), as D-82's draw-once pattern; 5-4's parts are 98 KB.
5. **The cast** (`view/art/market.ts`) are built on one die-cut body (`adult`: capsule limbs bent at elbows and knees, a look, a pose). `limb` (#119) drew its round ends inward, so two capsules meeting at an elbow left a pin-hole of card showing; the ends now bulge outward (chapter 4's cast redraws a hair smoother).
6. **A beat's subtitles never stack** (`talk/layout.ts`): a later subtitle treats the beat's earlier ones as hard to cover, as it treats him, and an off-page voice (no one to hang from) has ten steps down the page to choose from, so the lines keep their reading order. `check:ui` caught 5-2's three-line clerk beat at 375×667 (the third line covered Mocha's, 1.0:1).
7. **QA waits retry once** (`scripts/popup-checks.mjs` `gotoAndWait`, also used by `visual-check.mjs`): a fixture or target whose ready selector times out after 20 s is loaded again and given 60 s, because a cold dev server's first load of a route can outlast the first wait. Tabs hold 8 fixtures (was 15: one target per tab still hit `ERR_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES`), and a visual target that hit it anyway is shot again in a fresh tab.

**Why.** Every hit area and the page data stay as #121 left them; every page is ≤ 300 KB of page art; the chapter reads at a squint as vermilion stalls and amber bulbs over a dark lane, the marigold touchables still the brightest warm thing.

**Consequences.** Later carriers (the 101 cleaning 阿伯, 黑糖 the bear) bake the same four looks, `away` when called. The chapter-card landmark vignette, the 目錄's station art (no chapter has one yet: suggested on #193) and the photo frames stay with #193 (D-78, D-82).

### D-86 · 2026-10-20 · Chapter 6's pages: a train a word starts, birds as a weight both ways, a light he never reaches, a pushed treasure (#123)

**Context.** #123 writes chapter 6 「飛機睡著的時候」 (松山機場) from the GDD § 4.4 outline on #120's 飛機！ and cranked loops. Open: how 6-1's driverless train moves when nothing in the engine is timed, and how its depot loop stays out of the soft traps (#183); how 6-4's "roosting birds weigh down a seesaw" makes both halves of 飛機！ matter; how 6-5's searchlight lure survives 不要！ (a combination page offers it) without a timing trap; and how 6-6's 印章, which Mocha pushes, is drawn when a pushable's preset art is a bead and the goal already draws the treasure.

**Decision.**
1. **A train a word starts.** 6-1's driverless train is a printed state part (a `tab` nobody drives, D-79/D-84: `junction` / `loop` / `airport`) that reacts to 嗶！ (a robot, § 2.4) by the switch tab's position. The depot loop comes back to the junction on the next 嗶！, and the switch thrown while the train runs the loop sends it on to the airport: without that, a 嗶！ on the loop was a soft trap (the train looked alive and two moves had to be undone).
2. **Birds are a weight both ways.** 6-4's gangplank is a two-state `lift` toggled by 飛機！ in step with the `winged` flock roosting on it; the flock's roost is a node riding the cab's wheel. While they roost the plank drags on the apron and the cab's wheel is `lockedWhen` they're there; at the A321 they must come back to tip it onto the sill. Mocha has no part in 6-4.
3. **A light he never reaches.** 6-5's searchlight pool lands on the catwalk's railing (unconnected nodes): he crawls up under it and reaches, and never *arrives*, so a 不要！ to Mocha holds while he follows it (on arrival a refusal clears, § 2.4, and he turned back: a timing trap). Hiding Mocha in the crate is the other way. Mocha has only two perches there: a third let a Mocha move undo a 不要！ (a soft trap).
4. **A pushed treasure.** 6-6's 印章 is a pushable (`c6/stamp`, a placeholder baked in #123) that draws only while it tumbles; the goal attractor follows it (`followsPushable`) and is hidden for that one tick (`visibleWhen: not falling`), so the treasure is drawn once. It lands one edge from where he waits (`R1`), so the grab is a crawl.
5. **Placeholders where the preset misleads** (D-77, D-81, D-84): 6-1's front window per state (a nobody-driven tab draws as a pull-tab), the 印章, and the 土地公's 機場背心 (an ochre vest with a reflective band, never marigold). The cabin-crew 姊姊 is the kit's adult carrier, the birds origami, the rest preset art until #124.
6. **Chapter 6's lines** beyond § 5.2: the 姊姊's line on 抱抱 (6-1), the 店員小哥's cameo at the carousel (6-2, § 5.3), and Mocha's X-ray line (6-6); § 5.2 updated. An 8-bar music box in F (`chapter6`).

**Why.** Every page stays solver-green with no dead ends, soft traps, walkable decoys or ties and 0 lint warnings with no engine change, and every key move was checked necessary (6-5's two ways past Mocha: either, not neither).

**Consequences.** #124 replaces the placeholders and keeps the hit areas: the window's three views, the carousel and its curtain, the deck gate under its pigeons, the bridge's tunnel, cab and plank (the plank drawn riding the cab, `ridesOn`), the tower's catwalk and searchlight pool on the railing, the X-ray belt and the 印章. A light attractor on an unconnected node is a pattern for any lure that must hold a refusal.

### D-87 · 2026-10-21 · Chapter 6's art: the airport kit, a belt that is printed and a crank that is live, riders on a loop's slots, the runway as the beauty (#124)

**Context.** #124 draws chapter 6 (松山機場, 02:50) over #123's pages. Open: how a touchable `loop` (6-2's carousel, 6-6's X-ray belt) bakes, when a baked part replaces its preset's art wholesale (a baked loop would lose its marigold crank, seal and cue: the affordance lint); what draws the suitcases and the tray that ride a loop's slots (the stage's carriages are level only, and those nodes change height); how 6-4's cab is drawn when the plank `ridesOn` the cab and the view anchors the lift at the plank's end; and where the chapter's beauty lives (the issue: "the plane-watching moment's beauty: runway lights, sleeping planes").

**Decision.**
1. **The palette kit** `POPUP_AIRPORT` (bible § 6 row 6): runway blue and white dominant, taxi-light amber and wing-tip red the accent, with the taxiways' blue and the centre line's green; the airfield's lights are added to the grade's never-graded list (they glow, like neon in chapter 4). Every colour is ≥ ΔE 12 from marigold (`affordance.test.ts`). The 文湖線's brown is deepened (`#8E5A36`) so it never reads marigold.
2. **Planes are origami and unbranded**: one unit jet (`jet`: three-tone faceted fuselage with a crease line, swept wings, fin, engine, cockpit glass, door outlines) drawn at any scale, facing either way, and its lights as a separate layer (`jetLights`: asleep, only the wing tip glows — red to port, green to starboard; awake, the cabin windows, beacons and tail light). 6-3's `c6/plane-lights` part is that layer over the backdrop's plane. No airline livery or codes (GDD § 6 risk 11); 圓山大飯店 is drawn small on its hill, with affection.
3. **A baked loop is its belt; its crank stays live.** `LoopView` draws a loop's bitmap (printed, never marigold) at its hit centre and, when the loop is touchable, a live marigold 轉 crank at the part's `center` (rim grip, turn cue, upright seal, brad; the disc turns a step per state). An unbaked loop keeps the kit's art.
4. **Riders.** What rides a loop's slots is drawn by the page at the riding node (`riderArt(key, node)`, `view/art/airport.ts`; `RiderView`), so a suitcase (or the tray) slides exactly as its node does and Baokaka riding it lerps with it. A loop's own bitmap is one drawing for every state.
5. **The cab rides in the plank's bitmap.** 6-4's `c6/bridge-plank` (a lift that `ridesOn` the cab) draws the cab where the cab node sits relative to the plank's end per state (`CAB.stow − PLANK_END[state]`), so cab, bogie and plank move as one piece with the cab's swing. The bridge's telescoping section is the `extend` tab's carriage (`bridgeSection`).
6. **The beauty is the runway.** `lightLine` / `runway` / `taxiway` draw airfield lights as perspective rows of pinholes (spacing and size shrinking with distance, a blurred bloom and a hot core); 6-3's deck looks over the whole sleeping field, and 6-5's runway is dark until 哇！ lights it (`c6/runway-lights` draws the lit runway over the backdrop's dark one, same geometry). New fixture `c6p5-runway`.
7. **Cameos.** The 店員小哥 minds 6-2's baggage-service desk; 咻咻 peeks over 6-5's tower (the radio's log). The photo frames, the chapter-card vignette, the 目錄 station illustration and the 月亮線 window strip stay with #193, as for chapters 2–5.

**Why.** Each choice keeps the grandparent rule intact (only the crank is marigold), keeps hit areas exactly as #123 set them, needs no engine change, and keeps every page under its 350 KB budget.

**Consequences.** A later chapter with a loop (7's gondolas ride a cranked loop: #126) can bake the loop's belt and register riders the same way. `riderArt` lives in `airport.ts` for now; move it to a shared module when a second chapter adds riders.

### D-88 · 2026-10-21 · A beat never covers his thought bubble: move the cards, then the cloud, then split the beat (#197)

**Context.** At 375 × 667 (`talkScale` ≈ 1.39) a three-line beat needs more height than the page has between its top and Baokaka's head with the thought cloud over it (6-2's clerk cameo: three cards of about 118 units and a 145 × 80 cloud). `layoutTalk` treated the cloud as a soft avoid, so the last card covered it (5-3's learn beat hid his 「要！」 cloud; 6-2's third line lay over it). Making the cloud hard only moved the overlap onto him. The issue offered three levers: re-place or shrink the cloud, more candidates for an off-page voice, or cap a beat at two lines on small books.

**Decision.**
1. **The first pass is unchanged.** Every layout that was clean stays exactly where it was (no baseline moves where nothing clashed).
2. **A clash is measured** (`talkClash`): a card's overlap with Baokaka, the goal or another card × 100, with the cloud × 1. Zero is clean.
3. **Then the cards move, then the cloud** (`CloudPlan`): `firm` keeps the cloud over his head and makes it as hard to cover as he is, and every card may also take any spot on the page below the line before it, so the beat still reads top to bottom (`denseCandidates`: 8-unit rows, against either edge, centred, or under its speaker), and only when none keeps off him, the goal, the cloud and the other cards does it wrap narrower and taller (72 % and 52 % of its width) to fit a gap beside him or the goal; `beside` moves the full cloud level with his head on the side with room. In these passes the cloud tries each of its spots that keeps off him and the goal (over Mocha or a paw if it must: they are drawn over it) until the cards fit.
4. **Then the beat is split, not shrunk.** If no full-cloud plan is clean, `layoutTalk` reports `fit`, how many of the beat's first lines are; `PageView` hands that to the queue (`onCrowded` → `splitBeat`) in a layout effect, before paint, and the rest follow on the next tap. The serial is kept, so the lines already up do not replay their entrance. A cap for every small book was rejected: with the moves above every three-line beat in the fixtures fits at 375 × 667 and keeps its single tap; the split is the safety net (the unit test drives it with 5-3's and 6-2's beats at 375 × 667 大字 scale laid out whole).
5. **The tucked cloud is the last resort**: only when not even one line clears the full cloud does it fold into a 60 × 40 cloud at his ear (`THOUGHT_TUCK`, the icon alone: his reaching pose says 「要！」; text kinds keep their glyph at 22 units, so R5 still holds).
6. **A card takes in a hanging closing mark.** `subtitleSize` no longer caps a card at its wrap width: kinsoku lets a closing mark (「……」) hang past it, and the capped card let 5-1's 「他說：『開燈。』……」 spill off its card (2.3:1 in 大字 at 375 × 667, found by this check).
7. **`check:ui --popup` asserts it** on what is drawn ("Talk clear"): no `[data-subtitle-card]` (label tab included) or narration strip over `[data-bubble-cloud]` or his frame cut at his feet, at both viewports and with `UI_TEXT=big`.

**Why.** The cloud is the main teaching device (GDD § 2.2.6), so it keeps its size and its 「要！」 wherever there is any way to. One more tap for a crowded beat on a small phone costs less than a hidden cloud, and only crowded beats pay it.

**Consequences.** On a small book a crowded beat's cards may sit away from their speakers (on longer springs) or wrap narrower, and a beat too big even so takes two taps; on 5-3 at 390 × 844 Mocha's line now sits clear of the cloud and of the 「要！」 bark it used to cover. A future speaker anchor or card shape must keep the `data-subtitle-card` marker on its card art, or the check fails (it fails a subtitle without one).

### D-89 · 2026-10-21 · Chapter 7's pages: 爬樓梯 by watching, a step too tall is `cruise`, refusals that hold, placeholders for riders and floating cups (#125)

**Context.** #125 writes chapter 7 「纜車上的月亮」 (貓空) from the GDD § 4.4 outline, the chapter that teaches 爬樓梯. Open: what stands for "a grown-up's step" once every `climb` edge is his (D-84 said later pages need another barrier); how 7-1's review and 7-4's clouds use 不要！ and lures without soft traps (a refusal that is only useful if the next move comes at once is a timing trap the solver flags, #183); how 7-2's "Mocha refuses the glass cabin" stays within § 4.2's three touchables on page 2; how 7-6's "a cry wakes him" reaches a bear asleep across the spread; and what 7-2's cabins and 7-5's floating cups look like before their art (#126), when the stage draws no carriage for a loop that climbs or for cups on a lift.

**Decision.**
1. **爬樓梯 by watching.** 7-3 learns growth 1 when 掰掰 sends the tea farmer up the terrace (`learns: { growth: 1, when: farmer up }`, #120's toy page). Every climb is a 22-unit riser (the climb frames' height, D-58, D-83); `c7p3-climb` pins the knee-over frame on the real terrace.
2. **A step too tall is a `cruise` edge** from 7-3 on: 7-6's terrace walls, which only 黑糖 can carry him up (a carrier walks any baby edge, D-83). Before 7-3 a `climb` edge is still a grown-up's step (7-1's stairs).
3. **Refusals that hold.** A lure that must hold a 不要！ is one he can't arrive at: 7-1's Mocha down in the plaza (he reaches from the top of the stairs), 7-6's Mocha on the scarecrow's hat (an unconnected `reachNode`, as 5-6, D-84). 7-4 needs no refusal: its lights and snacks only steer (a snack beats a light, Mocha beats both, so Mocha stays in his basket all page), its pool of light is reachable (a refused 珍奶 sends him to sit in it, which clears the refusal), and with the lamp on nothing below shows (a refused bead leaves him nothing: he cries and forgets, D-75). 7-6's bean trellis is a waypoint, not a perch: a stop there would undo a 不要！ to Mocha for nothing. Two attractors that could tie are never seen together (7-1's sign and bead by where he is, 7-6's moon and 芹菜 by the bear).
4. **7-2's choice of cabin.** The turnstile at the top opens when the 店員小哥's shop cabin docks with Mocha aboard (`partEnter cable 0` while `mochaAt cabinShop`); the glass cabin's roof is a perch on a `glass` node (C8: a tap gets 「喵？」). With the crank and two roof rails, page 2 shows three touchables. The top platform runs back over the valley so a 不要！ at the top leaves him on the platform, not riding back down.
5. **A cry carries.** 7-6's sleeping bear reacts to `cry` within 400 units: wherever he cries, the bear wakes. The bear is two things: a creature asleep on the bed (hiding the 芹菜) and a carrier `lockedWhen` that creature is asleep (its `away` look: not there, D-85).
6. **Placeholders where the preset misleads** (D-77, D-81, D-84, D-86), baked in `env/art/c7.ts`: 7-2's cable (a cut-out, `c7/cable`), 7-4's clouds (`c7/cloud`, flaps: live they also cost the page its animated-node budget), 7-5's pool and floating cups (`c7/tea-pool`, the lift drawn at its first cup); 7-2's cabins are riders (`view/art/gondola.ts`, served by `riderArt`); the panda sign a placeholder decoy (`c7/panda-sign`), the 土地公's 叢林帽, the moon decoys reuse `c3/moon`. Everything else keeps its preset art or the kit's placeholders (the keeper and 黑糖 awake are the kit's adult carrier, the herons, pangolin, farmer and 黑糖 asleep origami) until #126.
7. **Chapter 7's lines** beyond § 5.2: the keeper's (7-1), the 店員小哥's cameo and his 「貓空站到了，謝謝光臨！」 (7-2), the farmer's (7-3), Mocha's on 7-4's and 7-5's 淘氣, and 黑糖's hum (7-6); § 5.2 updated. An 8-bar music box in G (`chapter7`).

**Why.** Every page stays solver-green with no dead ends, soft traps, walkable decoys or ties and 0 lint warnings with no engine change, and every key move was checked necessary (7-6's two ways past Mocha: hide him, or 不要！ him).

**Consequences.** #126 replaces the placeholders and keeps the hit areas: the zoo station and its stairs, the cable, cabins and turnstile, the tea terraces, the glass cabin with its clouds, seat and lamp, the teapot, pool and cups (the big 茶海 cup is the stage's crate at `C2` until then), the garden's walls, fence and latch, the bear asleep and awake. From chapter 8 a step he can't take is a `cruise` edge until 扶著站 (chapter 9), then a `walk` edge.

### D-90 · 2026-10-21 · Chapter 7's art: the 貓空 kit, mist in three layers, a teapot in a cradle, a mirror floor, 黑糖 away and awake (#126)

**Context.** #126 draws chapter 7 (貓空, 03:30) over #125's pages and replaces D-89's placeholders. Open: how 7-5's giant teapot tilts when its wheel's bitmap turns a full 120° a step (a pot drawn in the disc would end upside down); what shows the pool's level when a lift's bitmap slides with its first node; how 7-4's glass floor "turns into a mirror" without covering the clouds (touchables); where 7-6's rain jar goes, when the decoy moon lies 36 units under the path's top, behind the path's ledge face; what 黑糖 looks like while he sleeps on the bed (D-89 § 5: the carrier's `away` look); and where `riderArt` lives now that a second chapter has riders (D-87's consequence).

**Decision.**
1. **The palette kit** `POPUP_MAOKONG` (bible § 6 row 7): tea green and mist lilac dominant, gondola amber the accent (a light: added to the grade's never-graded list); every colour ≥ ΔE 12 from marigold (`affordance.test.ts`). Its signature is **vellum mist in three layers** between the night hills (`hills`, `mistBand`), tea rows on every hillside (`teaRows`, `lowerTerraces`), the 貓空纜車 on the ridges (`pylon`, `cableSpan`, `farCabin`), tea houses with lanterns (`teaHouse`) and Taipei's lights down the valley to a tiny 101 (`cityValley`). The moon is bigger here (r 27, bible § 6). Ledges: `zooPlatform`, `zooPlaza` (with the station's stairs as its ramp), `gondolaDeck`, `teaTerrace` (stepped box folds of clipped tea over dry stone; a stone step as its ramp), `teaDeck`, `chahai` (7-5's big cup), `cabinBench` / `cabinRack` / `cabinGlass`, `gardenPath`, `gardenPathJar`, `gardenTerrace`.
2. **The teapot hangs in its cradle.** 7-5's wheel is a marigold knurled **ring**, open in the middle (`c7/teapot`, one drawing turned a step per state), pinned by the wheel's brad; the pot itself is three cut-outs (`c7/teapot-0/1/2`, `visibleWhen` the wheel's state: upright, 22°, 46° and pouring) behind it. Cut-outs are pure art (the sim never reads them), so the page data gains only `cutouts`.
3. **The pool is the lift's bitmap per level.** `c7/tea-pool` draws the tea from its surface down to the pool's front lip (y 540) for each level, compensating for the bitmap's slide, plus the floating cups; the back wall and the tiled bottom (the 土地公 painted there) are the backdrop; the big 茶海 cup is the ledge `chahai` (it stands in the tea). For the 250 ms of a level change the column's foot slides with the cups.
4. **A mirror floor is a cut-out under the parts.** 7-4's `c7/glass-mirror` (`visibleWhen` the lamp is on) covers the glass and the night below with the cabin's reflection and the lamp's warm pool; the clouds (flaps) stay on top, and Mocha's basket is redrawn in it. The cabin is drawn cut away and its whole floor is glass (the ground far below shows through, the three places under the clouds).
5. **The rain jar is the path's ledge.** `gardenPathJar` draws 7-6's glazed jar at the path's right end, its water 36 under the path's top, in the floor layer — so the decoy moon (drawn over the floor) lies on its water, and 黑糖's `idle` look scoops from it with ripples.
6. **黑糖** is a die-cut figure (like the cast, not origami: he is a named character who speaks) — black card, the cream V moon mark, a tan muzzle, round ears. Asleep he is the creature `c7/sleeping-bear` (curled on his side on the celery bed, the 芹菜's leaves peeking out; off the page, nothing). Awake he is the carrier `c7/black-bear`, baked per look: **`away` is his paw prints** (dark earth, never chalk: Mocha's paws are chalk) trailing from the jar to the wall — he is up on the bed; `idle` scoops a drink, `carrying` holds Baokaka with his head beside him (his face shows), `back` the same mirrored, `done` steps back by the fence, waving and humming. The night-shift keeper (`c7/zookeeper`) is the market's `adult` in khaki and green, dozing on an upturned feed bucket.
7. **Riders are shared.** `riderArt` moved to `view/art/riders.ts`; each chapter registers its riders there (`AIRPORT_RIDERS`, `MAOKONG_RIDERS`). 7-2's cabins are cut away (white shell, tea-green band, no operator's livery): the 水晶車廂 with its glass floor, the 店員小哥's 「行動分店」 with its awning, shelves and him at the counter. 7-2's loop bitmap is the bullwheel drive (`c7/bullwheel`), its crank live; the cable and the two bullwheels are the `c7/cable` cut-out.
8. **Also drawn:** the panda-sign decoy in the kit, the 芹菜 treasure (a bunch of celery tied with foil), 7-1's herons as 6-4's flock, the origami pangolin, the tea farmer in his 斗笠, the turnstile, the folding seat (a pop-up flap: tapped down, the seat pops down onto the bench), the reading lamp, the fence's latch and gate. The photo frames, the chapter-card vignette, the 目錄 station and the 月亮線 window strip stay with #193.

**Why.** Each choice keeps the grandparent rule (only the cradle ring, the crank and the flaps' ears are marigold), keeps every hit area exactly as #125 set it, needs no engine or sim change, and keeps every page under 350 KB.

**Consequences.** A wheel that must show an object tilting less than a step's angle can use the same cradle-and-cut-outs split. Chapter 8's steam and 9's tide can reuse `mistBand` and the lift-column trick.

### D-91 · 2026-10-21 · The M4 engine I: coupled sections slip past a lock, reachHigh on, 咻—— proven on toys, the cruise sidestep (#127)

**Context.** #127 builds GDD § 7 M4 item 33: the `blowable` preset with 咻——'s facing cone, the 扶著站 growth (`cruise` edges and `reachHigh`), and 101's coupled sections for 10-3. Much was already in: 咻—— emitted its event with his facing and `blowable` parts advanced in its cone (#84, D-45), `cruise` edges opened at growth 2 (#82) and `reachHigh` was built behind `REACH_HIGH_ENABLED = false` (D-43). Open: how coupling is written and what "turning one turns its neighbour" does when the neighbour is locked, how it avoids loops (the issue: "> 32 iterations throws"), what a height-1 decoy means once he can reach up, and stand-cruise's missing fourth frame (#141, D-58).

**Decision.**
1. **`PartDef.coupled: Id[]`** on a `wheel`, `section`, `loop` or `blowable`, naming parts of those kinds with the same number of states (`L03-coupled`). Whenever the part turns by the player (`set`, `tap`), a reaction or 咻—— (the cascade), each coupled part turns the same number of steps the same way, in list order, right after it (its `partEnter` queued behind the driver's). Same direction only; a reversed gear is a later field if a page needs one.
2. **One hop.** A turn caused by coupling drives no further coupling, so coupling alone never loops; a mutual coupling (A ↔ B) is written on both parts and is no cascade cycle. A loop needs a reaction answering a coupled turn: then the cascade's 32-event cap throws `CascadeOverflowError` (the solver reports it as a page error), and the lint's rule 12 sees "a reaction turns P, P's coupling turns Q" as an edge (`L12-cascade-loop` when certain).
3. **The clutch slips.** A coupled part that is locked (Mocha on it with `lockedByCat`, or `lockedWhen`) or whose `stateWhen` forbids the new state holds still, and the driver turns alone (no `locked` / `jam` event: nothing was refused). That is Mocha's job on 10-3: he holds one section so its neighbour can turn alone. Turning the locked part itself is refused as before.
4. **`REACH_HIGH_ENABLED` is on.** From growth 2 a height-1 attractor is reachable from any node joined to it by an open edge, of any kind (a cat's `jump` included), as #82 built it; a page's growth makes it apply only from 9-3 on (no earlier page has growth 2). The solver's walkable-decoy check (`reachableDecoys`) counts a height-1 decoy next to a node he can walk to as walkable from growth 2: such a decoy must be two edges from anywhere he can stand.
5. **Toy pages** (`core/fixtures/m4.ts`, lab stages): `toy-steam` (learn 咻——; the steam and the 阿嬤's hat in one cone, the hat blown back on from past the steam), `toy-pinwheel` (facing matters: Mocha draws him round to face the pinwheel), `toy-ferry` (learn 扶著站 reaching up to Mocha on the railing; `cruise` past the rope; reachHigh from the deck to the bench, only a cat's hop joins them), `toy-101` (two dials coupled to an undriven middle `section`; the ledges line up only with Mocha holding the middle). All lint-clean, solver-green, 15–656 states; each key move is proven necessary.
6. **Three turns, not four, on `toy-101`.** With four, a half turn of a dial before Mocha sits down costs two moves to undo — a soft trap by #183's bar — while with three every wrong first turn can be absorbed. 10-3 (#133) should measure its own traps early (the issue's note) and may need the same.
7. **Stand-cruise is complete.** A new pose `cruise` (2 frames: the near foot reaching out along a rail at chest height, then the far foot closing up) joins `pull` and `wobble` (2): five frames for the GDD's four. A `cruise` step plays it over the step's progress (`CRUISE_AT`); a reachHigh grab shows `pull` (`reachedHigh`, `animMap.ts`). Preload group 9, like `pull` and `wobble`.

**Why.** One hop keeps coupling a pure function of the turn — no new state for the solver, no loops to prove — and the slipping clutch gives the lock a use beyond "it can't move", which is what makes 10-3 a puzzle (with the middle undriven, its turns are always the sum of its neighbours' until Mocha holds it). Turning reachHigh on now lets #129/#131 write pages against the real rule.

**Consequences.** #129 (chapter 8) uses 咻—— and blowables (8-3 learns it on the steam, 8-4's pinwheel); a `blowable` with `coupled` can drive a belt or lift directly. #131 (chapter 9) learns 扶著站 on 9-3 and should give each cruise ledge a rail in its art at the frames' height (≈ 50 pt). #133 (chapter 10) builds 10-3 on `coupled` + `lockedByCat`. Placeholder art for the toy pages only; the real pinwheel, steam, rail and 101 sections are #130/#132/#134's.

### D-92 · 2026-10-21 · The M4 engine II: linked mode (一起), the first steps and the lonely 咻咻 (#128)

**Context.** #128 builds GDD § 7 M4 item 34: 一起's linked mode (Mocha walks the floor where you tap, Baokaka follows; Mocha priority 0; C1/C7/C8 suspended), `walk` edges from 第一步, `lonely` creatures following the pair, `floor` inputs in the solver and PageLab, and 10-6's first-steps beat holding the sim for its lines. #84 left 一起 a stub (D-45): the bare `together` event, `floor` ignored, `canSay` refusing words while linked. Open: what "Mocha has priority 0" means for grabbing the goal, how fast Mocha walks, where he may walk ("never onto a node a page marks unsafe"), how a lonely creature moves, what teaches 第一步, and how a line holds the world.

**Decision.**
1. **一起 links them for the rest of the page.** Said with Mocha on the page, he hops down to Baokaka's node (leaving a perch is a normal leave: `catLeave`, the button or counterweight lets go; a trip or a stare is dropped). `baby.linked` stays true; Mocha stands on the floor (`mocha.floorNode`, `perch` null). With no Mocha on the page, nothing links. The say tick stays `saying` (the ring motif and pose), linked from the next.
2. **Floor taps go to link nodes only.** A link node is one a baby edge (`crawl`/`climb`/`cruise`/`walk`) touches, at height ≤ 1, not riding a pushable or a carrier (`linkNodes`). That is the reading of "unsafe": everywhere else is a perch, a cat ledge or a moving thing. Mocha walks there over every baby edge kind whatever Baokaka's growth (he is a cat: he can cross where the baby can't yet), blocked like Baokaka by creatures and closed gates (he'd lead him into a wall otherwise), through wet nodes (C7 suspended for him). Lint rule 18 keeps pages honest: on a page that can say 一起, no baby edge may touch a height-2 node (`L18-linked-edge`), and fixed link nodes ≥ 53 apart (`L18-link-crowded`, warning) so each floor tap is ≥ 48 CSS px; the view also shrinks each floor circle to half the gap to its neighbour so they never overlap.
3. **One edge a tick, side by side.** Mocha walks `LEAD_EDGES_PER_TICK = 1` (Baokaka's pace) in phase 3; Baokaka follows in phase 5 the same tick, so they arrive together. A tap commits the path in phase 1 (like a perch trip); a tap on Mocha's own node stops him; an unreachable node is a 「喵？」 (`mochaNope`). Arriving he emits **`lead`** (a new § 3.2 event, `id` = the node), which reactions, predicates and `learns` can use.
4. **Priority 0, read as "nothing else is his target".** Linked, Baokaka's decision loop is `linkedTick`: toward Mocha's node at his growth (`moving`; `linked` when with him), else as close as he can and `reaching` for Mocha. He never cries and refusals are cleared. To finish a page he **picks up** a shiny or snack on his node or one open edge away after his move (`grabbing`/`eating`; the goal sets `done`) — never a decoy, and never one a creature still holds (the 金鎖片 comes back when 咻咻 is hugged and drops it).
5. **Words while linked:** all but 一起 (already) and 不要！ (he never refuses Mocha). One function, `sayable`, answers for the tick, the solver and the word ring. 抱抱 while linked picks up no carrier (he holds on to Mocha); its event still reaches reactions (咻咻's).
6. **Lonely creatures** (phase 4, `followPair`): each tick while linked, a `lonely` creature on the page moves to its position nearest Mocha's node (squared distance; ties keep it where it is, then data order), never onto Mocha's or Baokaka's node (his node before he moves: it trails them). Off the page it stays away. Lint: a lonely creature must not block (`L18-lonely-blocks`).
7. **`learns` may narrow its event by `id`** (`{ event: 'lead', id: 'G' }`): 10-6 teaches 第一步 when Mocha waits on the far side of the gap.
8. **A line can hold the world:** `lines.onEvent[i].pause`. `PagePlayer` pauses the session (`'story'`) while a beat holding such a line is on the page; reading it on (a tap) resumes. The 10-6 lines (`c10p6.*`) are in the script.
9. **Solution steps** gain `{ floor: Id }`; the solver offers `floor` to every link node but the one Mocha stands still on, and counts it as a Mocha move for soft traps.
10. **The page.** Floor marks (a chalk paw and a baby footprint on a marigold halo, `data-affordance="floor"`) replace the perch paws while linked, on the floor layer. Mocha walks (`mocha.walk` stride, no hop) and stands `LINK_SIDE` (36) ahead of Baokaka on his facing side, drawn in front of him; together on a node he purrs and a pool of light lies under both. A walk step plays the wobbly `walk` with a sway (`walkSway`) and leaves soft footprints that stay for the session; the last step lands in the plop (`sit` + `.bk-plop`, a new `plop` row in the frame report). 咻咻 gets creature art (`common/shushu`: a raining lilac cloud up high, a small white cloud hovering on the floor) and wet floor nodes a puddle.

**Why.** "Priority 0" taken literally would leave the goal unreachable in linked mode; picking things up on the way keeps Mocha the only target (the pair walks where you tap) while still letting the finale end on a grab. One edge a tick makes "follow" read as walking together, and lets Mocha cross a gap ahead of him for the first steps. Link nodes need no new data (every page already says where people stand), so "unsafe" can't be forgotten by an author.

**Consequences.** 一起 is for good: on a page whose puzzle needs Mocha's perches, saying it is a trap (the lab's all-words 1-3, `lab-words`, now shows one). Pages offer it only from 10-5 on (#133); page E (#135) and the bonus page must decide whether 一起 is in their words (page E's goal is Mocha, which linked mode never grabs). #133's 10-5/10-6 build on `toy-party`/`toy-steps`: keep link nodes ≥ 53 apart, give 咻咻 `blocks: false`, and wire the `c10p6.*` lines with `pause`. #134 draws 咻咻 and the roof for real; the toy art is a stand-in.

### D-93 · 2026-10-21 · Chapter 8's pages: steam that keeps Mocha off, facing by a lure, tubs that drift, an abacus staircase, cushions that remember (#129)

**Context.** #129 writes chapter 8 「熱呼呼的煙」 (北投) from the GDD § 4.4 outline, the chapter that teaches 咻—— (#127's `toy-steam`, `toy-pinwheel`). Open: how 8-2's "steam perches block Mocha while the vents are on" becomes a puzzle without a soft trap (a vent turned on first must not cost two moves); how 8-3's hat 淘氣 stays apart from the steam the solution blows; how 8-4 makes "facing matters" the player's problem; how 8-5 differs from 7-5's teapot (also a level wheel, floating steps and a dry-only Mocha); how 8-6's three abacus rods, a step (22 units) apart, get ≥ 48 px handles that don't overlap; how 8-1's 淘氣 "climb onto every seat" is remembered (a mischief predicate sees only the tick's events and state); and what the chapter's placeholders are before its art (#130).

**Decision.**
1. **8-2: the steam is Mocha's, the clouds Baokaka's.** Two valves (two-stop wheels) each lift a paper-cloud lift. The only lure spot is the rock by the far vent, a `steam` node `blockedWhen` that vent is on; the far vent starts on, the near one off. So: far off (Mocha jumps), near on, far back on — the steam comes up under Mocha (he stays: a `steam` perch refuses arrivals only) and lifts the cloud beside him. Turning the near vent on first is still on a shortest way (no trap). The 淘氣's puff is the steam coming on under him three times (D-77: a puff is not a page event).
2. **8-3: the cone separates the steam from the hat.** The 阿嬤 sits behind him at the steam's edge; her hat is in his cone only from the bench facing left, which only Mocha's return to the bench brings about. The solution can't blow it off by accident, and the 淘氣 is a deliberate second use of the word.
3. **8-4: facing by a lure, wind by a window.** The pinwheel is `coupled` to the book lift's belt (#127's pattern) and `lockedWhen` the window it sits outside is shut. Mocha on the balcony rail draws him onto the tray, reaching up and left: facing the pinwheel. It is in his cone from every slot of the tray (≤ 138 units) and from nowhere on either floor, so the belt moves only with him on it and no slot is a dead end.
4. **8-5: tubs that drift, in a sequence.** The tap (a three-stop wheel) sets the water, a lift that draws the level, and two tubs, lifts whose nodes move across as well as up: the near tub comes to the deck half full and drifts out beside the far one full; the far tub drifts to the far step half full. The crossing is half, full, half again (par 5), where 7-5's was half then full. Mocha crosses a stepping stone (`wet`, blocked unless drained) to the 店員小哥's shop counter: the chapter's cameo. The 淘氣 counts Mocha's tub reaching the middle with Baokaka in neither tub — read at the tick's end, when the near tub's rider has already crawled into the far one (found by `check:play`: it fired on the solution and its line swallowed the next tap).
5. **8-6: handles at the page's foot.** The rods are sliders whose bead blocks are the staircase's steps (a block climbs to the next rod's when that one sits one stop right); their handles sit side by side at the bottom of the page (56 units each, 24 apart), as 3-5's cranks do, since hit areas on rods 22 apart would overlap. The steam on the counter is both 咻——'s target (in reach from the middle and top rods only) and what keeps Mocha off the bell (C7); the bell sits on the 番台's raised desk, so its paw is clear of Baokaka standing on the counter (a tap there opened his word ring: `check:play`). 37,494 states, ≈ 6.5 s: the largest page so far, inside the budget (all 52 pages ≈ 21 s of 60).
6. **8-1: cushions remember.** Each seat's cushion is a `button` that a `babyEnter` reaction presses and nothing releases; the 淘氣 is a `babyEnter` with all three down. Of the seats, the solution climbs the middle one (under the window decoy) and the far one (the bead); the left bench is the detour (不要！ to the window while Mocha is still on the rack over it). The 泡湯阿伯 (a carrier) stands one node past the middle seat: beside it, 抱抱 at the start solved the page in one move.
7. **Placeholders where the preset misleads** (D-77 … D-89), baked in `env/art/c8.ts`: the steam (`c8/steam`: a blowable is a cloud in every state), 8-3's sun hat and 8-4's pinwheel, 8-2's clouds and 8-5's tubs and water (lifts), 8-4's belt (a loop; its tray a rider, `view/art/beitou.ts`), 8-1's cushions, 8-6's bell and window (baked also to keep 8-6 inside the animated-node budget); 8-6's rods are carriages (`c8/abacus-rod`, flat: three move at once); the stained-glass decoy and the 土地公's 毛巾 in `view/art/placeholders.ts`. The touchables keep their preset's live art.
8. **Chapter 8's lines** beyond § 5.2: the 阿伯's, Mocha's 8-2/8-5/8-6 淘氣 lines, the 阿嬤's hat, the 店員小哥's cameo, the 番台 cashier's; § 5.2 updated. An 8-bar music box in E♭ (`chapter8`).

**Why.** Every page is solver-green with no dead ends, soft traps, walkable decoys or ties and 0 lint warnings, with no engine change, and a check proved each key move (each word, perch and part of the solution) necessary.

**Consequences.** #130 replaces the placeholders and keeps the hit areas (the rods' handles stay at the page's foot; the bell's perch on the 番台's desk). Chapter 9 (#131) can reuse 8-5's drifting lifts for the tide and 8-4's lure-to-face for 9-1's curtain and 9-6's sail.

### D-94 · 2026-10-21 · Chapter 8's art: the 北投 kit, wood-grain card and soft vellum steam, the 阿嬤 under her hat's part, an abacus without a pit (#130)

**Context.** #130 draws chapter 8 (北投, 04:00) over #129's pages and replaces D-93 § 7's placeholders. Open: what the chapter's steam looks like (it is on every page, and 8-2/8-6's 咻咻 beat is a plume that swirls the wrong way); how 8-3's straw hat sits on the 溫泉阿嬤's head when parts are drawn *under* creatures (the hat is a `blowable` part, the 阿嬤 a creature); what the abacus rods' slider bitmaps are when the thing they move (the bead blocks) is a live carriage; how 8-6 keeps its animated-node budget (≤ 150) once its treasure and bubble are drawn; and where the chapter-card vignette, the 目錄 illustration and the photo frames go.

**Decision.**
1. **The palette kit** `POPUP_BEITOU` (bible § 6 row 8): cedar brown and teal water dominant, steam white the accent; every colour ≥ ΔE 12 from marigold (`affordance.test.ts`). Its signature is **wood-grain card** (`planks`, `woodGrain`: the car's benches, the bathhouses, the library, the tubs and buckets) and **vellum steam** (`steamPlume`: many small billows rising and drifting, blurred soft in the bake, a few crisp vellum edges and curls on top — never a hard shadow). Reusable cut-outs: `beitouHills` (大屯山 and 紗帽山 with steam between), `treeCrown`, `hotLantern` (a 湯 提燈), `tiledRoof`, `springMuseum` (the hot-spring museum, no lettering), `mosaic`, `rockLump`, `stoneLantern`, `floorBoards`, `pebbles`, `fern`. **咻咻's beat** is `wrongSwirl`: on 8-2's valley and off 8-6's urn, one plume spirals back down against all the others, the little cloud peeking from its eye.
2. **The 阿嬤 is drawn by her hat's part.** A part lies under the creatures, so a hat part could never sit on a creature's head. `c8/granny-hat` draws the 阿嬤 sitting on the bench *and* her hat (`on`: on her bun; `off`: she looks up startled and it tumbles away), 34 above her node; its hit stays where D-93 put it (the cone reads its centre). Her creature (`c8/hot-spring-granny`, which keeps her node occupied) is the basket of hot-spring eggs at her feet.
3. **The rods' slider bitmap is a plate.** 8-6's bead blocks are carriages (live); `c8/abacus-rod`'s baked bitmap is the ebony plate under each handle's slot with three brass stops, and the backdrop runs a red cord from the abacus's bottom beam to each slot (the bead on a string). The abacus's frame and brass rods are the backdrop's, so 8-6's dress sets `trackPit: false`.
4. **8-6's budget.** The 算盤 is drawn twice there (on the page and in his bubble), so it is six elements (its frame and beam one evenodd card, the rods, the beads, the foil corners); the bead blocks are two paths and the brad. The busiest tick stays ≤ 150.
5. **Ledges** (`view/art/ledges.ts`, chapter 8's set): `carSeat` / `carBodyBeitou` (8-1's benches over the car's cedar floor and cream shell), `valleyBoardwalk` / `valleyRock`, `bathBoardwalk`, `libraryLoft` / `libraryStacks` (the ground floor's ledge face is a low bookcase), `bathDeck` / `bathStep`, `hallFloor` / `bantaiCounter`. Climbs need no ramp: the boxes are the steps.
6. **Also drawn:** the car's backdrop draws its body shell across the page, so the floor's gaps (the bath bags by the middle seat, the bucket) read as one car; the stained-glass decoy in the kit (a moon over 北投's hills and a steaming spring in cedar leading); the 泡湯阿伯 (the market's `adult` in a 汗衫 with a pale bath towel folded on his head, baked per look); 8-4's book tray rider; the 番台 window opening on the smiling cashier; the bell, the tap and the valves (marigold knurled rims round a brass cross and red iron handwheels); the 算盤 treasure. The photo frames, the chapter-card vignette, the 目錄 station illustration and the 月亮線 window strip stay with #193 (as D-90 § 8).

**Why.** Each choice keeps the grandparent rule (only the rims, the flap's ear, the knobs and the cradle stay marigold), keeps every hit area exactly as #129 set it, needs no engine or sim change, and keeps every page under 350 KB.

**Consequences.** A prop that must sit on a creature can be drawn with it in the prop's part, and the creature becomes a small companion piece. Chapter 9 (#132) can reuse `steamPlume` for sea mist, `mosaic` and `planks`.

### D-95 · 2026-10-21 · Chapter 9's pages: shutters that pick what he sees, a tune with no wrong notes, a sail he blows from the basket (#131)

**Context.** #131 writes chapter 9 「河的盡頭是海」 (淡水・漁人碼頭・淡江大橋) from the GDD § 4.4 outline, the chapter that teaches 扶著站 (#127's `toy-ferry`: `cruise` edges and `reachHigh`). Open: how 9-2's "shutters change visibility" makes a puzzle without a visible tie (a decoy and the bead both in sight) or a timing trap (D-89); how 9-5's "the right three-note order" stays free of soft traps (a wrong note that resets the order costs two moves) and inside the state budget (chapter 8 left the suite at ≈ 21 s of 60, and 8-6 alone at 6.5 s); how 9-6's sail can be blown while the boat it moves carries him (the simulation reads a part's authored hit, not where its node has gone); which parts need placeholders before #132; and the 背包客, the cameo and the 咻咻 beat.

**Decision.**
1. **9-2: what the shutters show, never two shinies at once.** The 阿給 shop's shutter starts up on a glinting 招財貓 (a decoy, a height-2 node joined to nothing, seen only from in front of the shop, `X2`–`X4`, and placed so that the nearest of those is where he reaches); the 鐵蛋 shop's starts down on the bead (seen only at the shop, `X5`/`X6`/`C`). They are never both in sight. The decoy is hidden from the lamp's node too, so a refusal spent on the way back to Mocha is no timing trap (a first version showed it from `X1`: the solver found tapping anything after 不要！ cost two moves). Mocha on the 鐵蛋 shop's sign is the lure; the 背包客 (§ 5.3's third time) blocks the lane until 掰掰; the 鐵蛋阿婆 (a carrier) lifts him onto her counter, a grown-up's step (`cruise`) before 9-3. The third shutter the outline implied (a 魚丸 shop for the 土地公) made the start four touchables (the affordance lint allows three on pages 1–3) and doubled the states: dropped; the 淘氣 is both shutters up at once.
2. **9-5: one tune, and no wrong notes.** The platform's tune is 生日快樂's first three notes (Sol Sol La) on three cables (props: Sol, La, Do), shown on the maintenance box's plate; the 淘氣 finishes the line (Sol, Do). One `section` (`tune`, 0–5) follows both: up to 3 a note out of turn changes nothing (no reset, so no wrong tap is a soft trap), after 3 the 淘氣's two notes must come at once (a wrong one goes back to 3; the platform stays down). Two trackers (a strict song and a separate code) were 31,259 states; one is 12,947. Gulls roost on the cables and mute them (`lockedWhen`) until 飛機！. The § 5.2 beat is nine lines on `babyEnter T1`, each `pause` (#128), and 咻咻 (a plain creature: no 一起 here) leaves on `babyEnter T2`, as he goes on.
3. **9-6: blow from the basket.** The sail is the fishing boat's, fixed, `coupled` to its derrick (a loop) whose four stops swing a net basket from the quay to the deck. The stops sit within 125 units left of the sail's hit and in the cone facing right, so 咻—— from the basket always turns it, from the quay (164 units) or the deck never: the basket moves only with him in it, and every stop is four blows from any other — no dead end. Mocha rides the basket with him (he is the lure in and the way to the wheelhouse); gulls on the deck rail are 飛機！'s; the rail is a `cruise` edge and the 聽診器 on the mast a height-1 `reachHigh`.
4. **9-1, 9-3, 9-4.** 9-1 reviews 咻—— (a lure along the racks turns him to the blind; from the bench he faces away, from mid-car it's out of reach) and 飛機！ (sparrows on the sill). 9-3 is `toy-ferry` with the pier's 悠遊卡 gate (嗶！) first, difficulty 2 (page 3's breather). 9-4's sun dial (dawn · noon · sunset) sets the tide: dawn bares Mocha's stepping stone (`wet` otherwise) to the 店員小哥's ice-cream kiosk — the chapter's cameo — noon floats the dinghy to the boardwalk's end, sunset drifts it across (8-5's drifting lift, D-93's note); the boardwalk past the lamp is a `cruise` plank and the bead hangs on a bollard (`reachHigh`).
5. **Placeholders where the preset misleads** (D-77 … D-93), baked in `env/art/c9.ts`: 9-1's blind and 9-6's sail (blowables are clouds), 9-4's tide and dinghy (lifts are bare platforms), 9-6's derrick (a loop is a level belt; its net basket is a rider, `view/art/tamsui.ts`), 9-3's horn, 9-5's cables (the prop preset is a split-flap card: each cable shows its note) and the maintenance box's plate (a cut-out showing Sol Sol La). 9-2's shutters (baked flaps, the marigold dog-ear the grip) and its 阿婆 (baked per look) keep the page inside the 150 animated-node budget beside the 背包客's live art (163 at the busiest tick live, 141 baked). The 招財貓 decoy and the 土地公's 船長帽 in `view/art/placeholders.ts`. The sun dial keeps the wheel's live art.
6. **Chapter 9's lines** beyond § 5.2: the 背包客's third question and Mocha's translation (§ 5.3), the 阿婆's, the horn's, the 店員小哥's kiosk cameo, the 生日快樂 淘氣's; § 5.2 updated. Baokaka's 「一起！」 at 9-5 is a line (the vocabulary test learns its form). An 8-bar music box in D (`chapter9`).

**Why.** Every page is solver-green with no dead ends, soft traps, walkable decoys or ties and 0 lint warnings, with no engine change; a check proved each key move (each word, perch and part of the solution) necessary. Chapter 9 costs ≈ 2.9 s of the solver budget (the largest page, 9-5, 12,947 states, ≈ 1.4 s), leaving the suite near 21 s of 60 (58 pages) for chapter 10, page E and the bonus page.

**Consequences.** #132 replaces the placeholders and keeps the hit areas (the cables' tags, the derrick's stops, the shutters); every `cruise` edge (9-3's rope, 9-4's plank, 9-6's gunwale) gets a rail at the frames' chest height (≈ 50 pt). #133 can reuse the one-tracker idea for 10-3 (a section that ignores wrong turns has no soft trap) and should keep its pages near chapter 9's sizes.

### D-96 · 2026-10-21 · Chapter 9's art: the 淡水 kit, glassine sea at first light, 淡江大橋 drawn true, rails at his chest; 大字 lifts the card's time chip (#132)

**Context.** #132 draws chapter 9 (淡水・漁人碼頭・淡江大橋, 04:40) over #131's pages and replaces D-95 § 5's placeholders. Open: how the dawn arrives (bible § 3.3: chapters 9–10); how to draw 淡江大橋 (opened 2026-05-12, the world's longest-span single-pylon asymmetric cable-stayed bridge) accurately on six pages, and on 9-5 so the three tapped cables (props whose hits are fixed) lie on the bridge's own stays; where the rails for the `cruise` edges go (the frames hold a rail at ≈ 50 pt) when the ledges are drawn over the backdrop; how 9-4's sun dial reads at a glance; what the parts #131 left on live preset art become (9-3's gate, 9-4's dial, 9-5's tune dial and platform); and the open 大字 issue on the chapter card (a seven- or eight-character title at the 24 px floor ran under the time chip).

**Decision.**
1. **The palette kit** `POPUP_TAMSUI` (bible § 6 row 9): violet and sea teal dominant, the first-light peach the accent; every colour ≥ ΔE 12 from marigold (`affordance.test.ts`). The chapter's bake grade lifts toward the dawn (×1.32, 1.23, 1.13), so the kit is authored dusky and the bake warms it. Every sky is `dawnSky`: the chapter's sky, a warm radial glow low on the right, the stars only in the upper left, long vellum cloud strips lit peach from below (`dawnClouds`); right-facing faces take the peach, left ones stay violet. The signature material is **glassine sea** (`seaWater`: teal under two diagonal glassine sheens, ripples, the dawn's peach broken in a column under the glow). Reusable cut-outs: `guanyin` (觀音山's reclining profile with a lit mist band and 八里's lights), `tamkangBridge`, `loversBridge` (the 情人橋's inclined mast and curving deck), `harbourLamp`, `ropeCoil`, `lifebuoy`, `railing`, `farBoat`, `mangroves`, `shophouse` (淡水老街's red-brick arcades).
2. **淡江大橋 drawn true** (`tamkangBridge`): one slender tapering pylon (211 m) with a red light at its tip; its cables in one plane fanned from its upper third, asymmetric — many over the 450 m main span to 八里, fewer over the 175 m back span to 淡水 (27 and 20 pairs) — so seen from upriver the long span runs left and the tower stands near the right bank; the deck a fifth of the way up, its approach piers, the pylon's reflection broken in the water. It stands at the river mouth on 9-2, 9-3, 9-4 and 9-6. On **9-5** the page is on its deck at the tower's foot: the tower rises off the page (both edges on the page so it reads as one column, the shade face violet, the dawn face peach), the main span's stays sweep down to the left across the whole sky like harp strings, and the three the tune is played on are the backdrop's own stays at 45° (x + y = 368, 406, 444) through their parts' centres; the parts draw only a short length and the note tag (a small box, so the gulls' lock veil is small). The back span's stays leave its right face.
3. **Rails at his chest.** The ledges are drawn over the backdrop, so each `cruise` edge's rail is the backdrop's, ≈ 48 above the edge's feet line where the ledge's top face meets the page: 9-3's ferry railing (`D2`–`D3`: its middle rail at y 428, its top rail Mocha's perch at 404, the coil of rope on the deck's back between them), 9-4's boardwalk railing (`W2`–`W3`: the boardwalk is under repair there — `wharfBoardwalk` cuts the boards away but for the plank along the rail — with a 施工中 barrier), 9-6's gunwale rail (`F1`–`F2`, y 376).
4. **Parts.** 9-3's gate (`c9/ferry-gate`: two steel cabinets, the card reader, flaps that fold back with a green light and a chalk 嗶); 9-4's sun dial (`c9/sun-dial`, one drawing: three little skies — the sun rising out of the sea, high at noon, sinking red — a third round each so the one under the tide board's red pointer is the time; marigold rim, `data-grip`); the tide warms with the sun (the bible's sunset-gold trick: dawn only the channel, rose-lit; noon a teal flood; sunset gold in its ripples; it stops at the far pier's wall); 9-5's tune dial (`c9/tune-dial`, a printed brass gauge, the six steps of the line round it, 60° apart) and the maintenance platform (`c9/maintenance-lift`, its cables up to the gantry's arm, per state); the blind, the shutters, the horn, the cables, the sail and the derrick redrawn. The far pier's backdrop matches its ledge (`wharfPier`) so the single node's box sits in it.
5. **Creatures** (bible § 5: origami): the gulls (`c9/gulls`: on 9-5 one on each stay cable they mute, on their 45° lines; on 9-6 on the boat's deck) and 9-1's sparrows (`c9/sparrows`) — one path per tone for the flock; `sky`, flying. The 聽診器 (`treasureArt`, five elements: it is drawn twice on 9-6).
6. **Ledges** (`view/art/ledges.ts`, chapter 9's set): `mrtSeat` (the far end's 博愛座 teal) and `windowSill` with the 淡水信義線's `carBodyXinyi`, `oldStreetLane`/`arcadeStep`/`eggCounter`, `pierDeck`/`ferryDeck` (split at the gate), `wharfBoardwalk`/`wharfPier`, `bridgeWalkway`/`towerBase` (split at the gap), `quay`/`boatDeck`. Climbs need no ramp.
7. **The chapter card's title row** (`titleLayout`): the title shrinks to its room beside the time chip, but never under the smallest a word may be (R5: 20 px, 24 with 大字). #131's shrink took 10's 「離天空最近的地方」 to 17 px (`check:ui` R5 caught it here) and with 大字 a seven- or eight-character title at 24 px still ran under the chip; when a title cannot fit beside the chip at that floor, the chip is lifted onto the card's top edge (clipped on like a luggage tag) and the title takes the whole row.
8. The photo frames, the chapter-card vignette, the 目錄 station illustration and the 月亮線 window strip stay with #193 (as D-90 § 8, D-94).

**Why.** Each choice keeps the grandparent rule (only the dial's rim and the shutters' ears are marigold), keeps every hit area exactly as #131 set it, needs no engine or sim change, and keeps every page under 350 KB (108–176 KB) and inside the animated-node budget (9-2 at 150 is #131's, unchanged).

**Consequences.** Chapter 10 (#134) can reuse `dawnSky`, `dawnClouds`, `seaWater` and `tamkangBridge` (101 is visible from the river mouth's skyline the other way) and should author for the stronger 05:10 grade. A `cruise` edge's rail is a backdrop element by convention.

### D-97 · 2026-10-22 · Chapter 10's pages: a crank that turns three sections, a damper that needs the cat, crying together, and Mocha in the photo of the first steps (#133)

**Context.** #133 writes chapter 10 「離天空最近的地方」 (台北101) from the GDD § 4.4 outline: the book's last chapter, teaching 一起 (10-5) and 第一步 (10-6) on #128's linked mode, and 10-3's coupled sections on #127's clutch. Open: a 10-3 that is "the hardest pure puzzle" and still free of soft traps (#127 warned that 4-state sections trap); how 10-4's damper is both "a slider" and "blowable"; how 10-5's "say 不要！ to everything" works when a refusal is forgotten on reaching the next target (D-43); the outline's 10-1 淘氣 「use every word once」 (a 7-flag tracker); and how the finale's lines, photo and end play in the shell, which no linked page had done yet.

**Decision.**
1. **10-3: a sweep picked the puzzle.** Every combination of crank (which sections have one), coupling (each part to its neighbours), Mocha's rims and start states was solved (18,144 variants, three turns per section). The only family with par ≥ 5, no soft trap, no dead end and Mocha necessary: the crank on the middle section, `coupled` to both neighbours, and `lockedByCat` rims on the bottom and top sections; start `1 · 0 · 1` (par 5: crank, hold the top, crank, hold the bottom, crank). Every perch has a `reachNode` at the tower's foot, so a 不要！ to the bead has somewhere to go and is forgotten there (without it the refusal stuck while Mocha held the top: a soft trap). The stored solution starts with Mocha on the gondola (where he already is) so the hint fallback, from a rim he sat on too early, takes him back first.
2. **10-4: one blowable pendulum.** The damper is a `blowable` (`left` · `centre` · `right`; from the right it swings back to the left) with `stateWhen: { right: mochaAt sphere }` — Mocha's weight takes it to the far side. Nobody can drag it (a slider made the page three moves and dropped the breath). Par 5. The 淘氣 counts all swings (five), not those with him on it: from the far stop the sphere is behind him, out of 咻——'s cone, so with him aboard it only ever swings twice.
3. **10-5: 不要！ to the one thing there is.** A second thing to want (the party's lanterns, tried) only sent him back to Mocha once he reached them; so the party offers only Mocha, perches `wet` under 咻咻's rain. 不要！ → he cries → learns 一起 (`event: cry`). Linked, the pair must cross the puddle to reach 咻咻 (whose positions are only round its table), so Mocha walks through the water for him in the solution. The 淘氣's "full circle" is all four corners of the table, order-free (a `section` cake with a `setFlag` per corner: ×16 states, 10,064). The stored solution says 一起 while he is still crying (the hint fallback, matching the settled state, otherwise said 不要！ again forever).
4. **10-1's 淘氣** is 「對著窗外的101說哇！」 (a 7-flag 「every word」 tracker is ×128 states on a warm-up page); the medley is 嗶！, 掰掰 and 咻—— in the solution, 哇！ in the 淘氣. The card plays chapter 10's first two intro lines; 10-1 plays 「ㄅㄚ！」 and Mocha's as 101 appears in the window.
5. **The finale in the shell.** (a) **+M for a linked Mocha:** `mochaInFrame` counts Mocha standing still on a floor node inside `photoRect` (the photo of the first steps has him beside Baokaka). (b) **No translation jokes once linked** (`firstUseLine(def, word, linked)`): 一起's own translation, then none — 10-6's 抱抱 to 咻咻 was getting 「他說：『載我一程。』」. (c) **Beat lines on the hug, not the grab:** lines on the grab of a page's goal are still up over the album; 10-5's and 10-6's hug lines fire on the hug that reached 咻咻 (`hug` while 咻咻 is in its after-hug position: 10-6 gains a `calm` position just over the deck's end, never nearer Mocha than the deck), each `pause`. (d) The 抓周 tray's nine coins are chapters 1–9's (`treasuresFound`), so chapter 10's end fills none; after the four end lines, the sticker card and the 目錄 (page E is #135's). (e) **End lines in beats** (`endGroups`, `END_BEATS`): chapter 10's four are two beats — 「一起。」 with Mocha's one exact translation, then the sunrise and 廣播姊姊 (three in one beat put the narration strip over the page's foot, and `check:ui` failed its contrast at 375 × 667).
